20-1-1. Advisory council-Appointment of members.-Within the department of agriculture and conservation there shall be an advisory council on fish and game, consisting of seven (7) qualified clectors of this state, who shall be appointed by the governor as herein provided. In the month of February in each year, the governor shall appoint a member or members of said council to hold office until the first day of March in the fifth year after such appointment and until his or their respective successor or successors are appointed and qualified to succeed the member or members whose term will next expire.

Any vacancy which may occur in said council shall be filled by appointment by the governor for the remainder of the unexpired term. The members of said council shall be chosen with due regard to their knowledge and interest in fish and game conservation. 

History of Section. 

P. L. 1939, ch. 660, § 168; P. L. 1953, ch. 3157, § 1. 

20-1-2. Chairman of advisory council.-The members of said council shall elect one of their number as chairman upon the appointment of any new member for a full term and whenever the office may become vacant.

History of Section. 

P. L. 1939, ch. 660, § 168; P. L. 1953, ch. 3157, § 1. 

20-1-3. Secretarial service to council-Powers and duties.- 

Secretarial service shall be provided by the director of the department. Said council shall make suggestions to and advise the director of the department concerning the policies, rules and regulations of the department relating to fish and game restoration and conservation; provided, however, that said council shall have no administrative powers.

History of Section. 

P. L. 1939, ch. 660, § 168; P. L. 1953, ch. 3157, § 1. 

20-1-4. Hatcheries-Enforcement of fish and game laws.-The department and director of agriculture and conservation shall have charge of the Wickford hatchery and the Goose Nest Spring hatchery, and shall enforce the provisions of chapters 29 and 34 of this title and other provisions of law relating to birds, fish, and game. 

History of Section. 

P. L. 1939, ch. 660, § 163; P. L. 1939, ch. 718, § 11.

20-1-5. Office and facilities of division.-The department of administration shall furnish the division of fish and game with suitable office and telephone service for the transaction of its business, where the maps, charts, books, leases, and the other property connected with said division shall be kept. 

History of Section. 

G. L., ch. 137, § 39, as enacted by P. 

L. 1934, ch. 2117, § 1; G. L. 1938, ch. 

230, § 10; impl. am. P. L. 1951, ch. 2727, art. 1, § 2. 

20-1-6. Bond of division chief.-The chief of the division of fish and game, previous to entering upon the duties of his office, shall give a bond, with sureties satisfactory to the general treasurer, in the sum of one thousand dollars ($1,000), with condition to faithfully perform the duties of the office according to law. 

History of Section. 

P. L. 1901, ch. 853, § 2; G. L. 1909, ch. 203, § 2; G. L. 1923, ch. 230, § 2; G. 

L. 1938, ch. 230, § 2. 

20-1-7. Control of fishing in Wallum Lake.-The chief of the division of fish and game in the department of agriculture and conservation may make suitable rules and regulations governing fishing in Wallum Lake, and for such purpose may confer with the division of fisheries and game in the department of natural resources of the state of Massachusetts concerning fishing privileges to residents of 

Massachusetts in said Wallum Lake.

History of Section. 

G. L., ch. 235, § 55, as enacted by P. 

L. 1936, ch. 2348, § 1; G. L. 1938, ch. 

230, § 8. 

20-1-8. Fixing of seasons and bag limits.-Notwithstanding any provisions of the general laws and/or the public laws, the director of agriculture and conservation under the provisions of title 42, after holding a public hearing, notice of which shall be published twice a week for two (2) successive weeks in some daily newspaper published in the city of Providence, and with the approval of the governor, is hereby authorized and empowered to fix seasons and bag limits on any species of game, bird, fresh-water fish or fur-bearing animal in any specified locality or localities or the entire state when he shall find after investigation that such action is expedient. 

History of Section. 

P. L. 1949, ch. 2357, § 1.

20-1-9. Publication and effective period of seasons and bag limits. -Notice of any such regulation so made shall appear in at least one (1) newspaper published in each county of the state once each week for two (2) consecutive weeks and such regulation shall become effective on a date to be specified in said regulation which date shall be subsequent to the date of the second publication. 

Any such regulation shall be and remain in effect for not longer than one (1) year from the effective date.

History of Section. 

P. L. 1949, ch. 2357, § 1. 

20-1-10. Appointment and delegation of powers to conservation officers. The chief of the division of fish and game shall appoint such number of conservation officers as he may deem necessary, for the detection and prosecution of any violations of the laws of this state relating to fisheries, shellfisheries, wild birds and wild animals. 

Said chief may delegate any and all of his powers and duties to each of such conservation officers, who shall serve at his pleasure. 

History of Section. 

P. L. 1899, ch. 678, § 3; P. L. 1901, ch. 

853, § 6; P. L. 1903, ch. 1098, § 1; G. L. 

1909, ch. 136, § 22; G. L. 1909, ch. 203, § 7; 

P. L. 1912, ch. 851, § 1; P. L. 1916, ch. 

1385, § 2; P. L. 1917, ch. 1535, § 2; G. 

L. 1923, ch. 137, § 20; G. L. 1923, ch. 

230, §7; G. L. 1938, ch. 230, § 3; P. L. 

1949, ch. 2301, § 1; P. L. 1954, ch. 3358, $1. 

20-1-11. Records and reports of conservation officers.-Each conservation officer shall keep a record of his official acts, receipts, and expenditures and, when requested by the chief of the division of fish and game, shall make such report and in such form as the chief of the division of fish and game may prescribe.

History of Section. 

G. L., ch. 230, § 3, as enacted by P. L. 1954, ch. 3358, § 1. 

20-1-12. Enforcement powers of chief and conservation officers. 

The chief of the division of fish and game, and each conservation officer, shall have the power: (1) To enforce all laws, rules and regulations relating to fish, game, fur-bearing animals, shellfish and lobsters, and to go on any property other than private dwellings, in the performance of his duties; (2) To execute all warrants and search warrants for the violation of laws, rules and regulations relating to fish, game, fur-bearing animals, shellfish and lobsters;

(3) To serve subpoenas issued for the trial of all offenses against the laws, rules and regulations relating to fish, game, fur-bearing animals, shellfish and lobsters; (4) To carry firearms or other weapons, concealed or otherwise, in the course of and in performance of the duties of such office; (5) To arrest without warrant and on view any person found violating any law, rule or regulation relating to fish, game, fur-bearing animals, shellfish and lobsters, take such person before a court having jurisdiction for trial, and detain such person in custody at the expense of the state until arraignment; (6) To seize and take possession of all fish, game, fur-bearing animals, shellfish or lobsters, which have been caught, taken, or killed, or had in possession, or under control of any person, or which have been shipped or are about to be shipped, at any time, in any manner, or for any purpose, contrary to the laws of this state; (7) To seize all fishing tackle, guns, shooting and hunting paraphernalia, hunting, fishing or trapping licenses, traps, decoys, tongs, bullrakes, dredges, or other implements or appliances used in violation of any law, rule or regulation relating to fish, game, furbearing animals, shellfish or lobsters when making an arrest, or found in the execution of a search warrant, and hold the same at the owner's expense until the fine and costs imposed for the violation have been paid in full.

History of Section. 

G. L., ch. 230, § 3, as enacted by P. L. 

1954, ch. 3358, § 1. 

20-1-13. Deputy wardens.-The chief of the division of fish and game shall appoint such number of deputy wardens as he may deem necessary. The deputy wardens shall not be authorized to carry pistols or revolvers but shall be authorized to detect violators of the laws of this state relating to fisheries, shellfisheries, wild birds and wild animals. Such detection shall be reported to the chief of the division of fish and game who may proceed with the prosecution. Deputy wardens shall be given an identification badge by said chief and shall serve at his pleasure.

History of Section. 

G. L., ch. 230, § 3, as enacted by P. L. 

1949, ch. 2301, § 1; P. L. 1954, ch. 3358, 

§ 1. 

20-1-14. Oyster guards.-The said chief may also appoint such oyster guards as he may deem advisable for the detection and prosecution of any person committing depredations upon leased oyster

st e, ground. Each of said oyster guards shall be, by virtue of his office, a special constable, and as such oyster guard may, without warrant, arrest any person whom he may find taking oysters wrongfully from leased oyster ground and detain him for prosecution not exceeding forty-eight (48) hours, and may seize any boat or vessel used in such wrongful taking of oysters, together with her tackle, apparel and furniture and all implements belonging thereto. 

G. L., ch. 203, § 7, as enacted by P. L. 

1912, ch. 851, § 1; G. L. 1923, ch. 230, $7; G. L. 1938, ch. 230, § 4. 

20-1-15. Enforcement powers of division.-The chief of the divi sion of fish and game and his deputies and assistants shall protect the wild birds, wild animals, and shellfisheries throughout this state, and they shall enforce the law of this state and prosecute every person who shall violate any of the laws of this state relating to wild animals, wild birds, shellfisheries, fisheries, and the setting or attendance of snares and traps. They shall also have the power to execute all warrants and search warrants issued for the violation of the laws of this state with reference to wild birds or wild animals and the setting or attendance of snares or traps for wild birds or wild animals, and they shall have the power to arrest without warrant any person whom they find violating such laws; provided, that any person so arrested without warrant shall not be detained longer than twenty-four (24) hours, except as otherwise specifically provided by law.

History of Section.

20-1-16. Power to search without warrant.-The chief of the division of fish and game and his deputies and assistants shall have power to make search where they have reason to believe that wild animals, wild birds, shellfish, or fish, or any parts thereof, are possessed in violation of law; and without search warrant to examine the contents of any boat, car, wagon, or other vehicle, box, locker, basket, crate, game bag, or other package, or the contents of any building other than a dwelling house, to ascertain whether any of the provisions of the laws of this state relating to the protection of

wild animals, wild birds, shellfish, or fish, have been or are being violated, and to use such force as may be necessary for the purpose of such examination and inspection; and with a search warrant to search and examine the contents of any building or dwelling house; seize all wild animals, or wild birds, shellfish, or fish, or any parts thereof, possessed in violation of the law, or showing evidence of illegal taking, and hold the same subject to the order of the court issuing such search warrant. If any such wild birds or wild animals, shellfish, or fish shall be seized without search warrant under the provisions of this section, the same procedure shall be pursued as is hereinafter in this title provided for the forfeiture of wild birds and wild animals, shellfish, or fish taken under a search warrant. 

P. L. 1899, ch. 678, § 4; G. L. 1909, ch. 

136, § 23; P. L. 1916, ch. 1385, § 2; G. L. 

1923, ch. 137, § 21; G. L. 1938, ch. 230, 

§7; P. L. 1954, ch. 3400, § 1. 

20-1-17. Regulations for enforcement of shellfishery laws-Appropriations.—Said chief shall make all necessary regulations for enforcing the laws of the state relating to shellfisheries, and for executing the duties imposed upon him by law. 

The general assembly shall annually appropriate such sum as it may deem necessary for the purpose of enforcing said laws, and the state controller shall draw his orders on the general treasurer on receipt by him of proper vouchers, duly authenticated, and approved by the chief of said division of fish and game, and by the director of agriculture and conservation.

History of Section. 

G. L., ch. 203, § 7, as enacted by P. L. 

1912, ch. 851, § 1; G. L. 1923, ch. 230, §7; P. L. 1935, ch. 2250, § 149; G. L. 

1938, ch. 230, § 4; impl. am. P. L. 1939, ch. 660, § 65. 

20-1-18. Operation of patrol boat. The general assembly shall annually appropriate such sum as it may deem necessary to protect the oyster fisheries of the state, patrol and police the oyster grounds, to check licenses of "free fishermen," protect the scallop areas, collect egg lobsters, plant the fry and execute special work incidental to the lobster and other shellfisheries, said sum to be expended under the direction of the director of agriculture and conservation for the purpose of conditioning a patrol boat for continuous service, paying the salary of a man in charge of and to pilot said craft and providing fuel, coal and such other subsistence as may be necessary in said continuous patrol, police and other entailed services; and the

controller is hereby authorized and directed to draw his orders upon the general treasurer for the payment of such sum or sums, or so much thereof as may be required from time to time upon the receipt by him of proper vouchers approved by the director of agriculture and conservation.

G. L., ch. 230, § 4, as enacted by P. L. 

1940, ch. 908, § 1. 

20-1-19. Obligation of fishing vessels to heave to on command of patrol boat. Any fishing vessel or boat while operating in Rhode 

Island territorial waters shall immediately heave to upon command or signal of a marine patrol boat operated by the division of fish and game and carrying the identification of the state of Rhode Island. Said signal shall be a shouted command, or a white colored signal flare fired into the air. It shall be unlawful for the occupants of any fishing vessel or boat so commanded, or signaled by the division of fish and game marine patrol, to dump, destroy or throw anything from said vessel or boat. Any person violating the provisions of this section shall, upon conviction he fined twenty-five dollars ($25.00).

History of Section. 

G. L., ch. 230, §§ 11, 12, as enacted by

P. L. 1954, ch. 3302, § 1. 

20-1-20. Prosecution of violations.-The chief of the division of fish and game and his deputies and assistants shall, by virtue of their respective offices, make complaints of any violations of the laws of this state relating to fisheries, shellfisheries, wild birds or wild animals, or relating to the setting or attendance of snares and traps, and they shall not be required, or any of them, to give recognizance or to furnish surety for costs or be liable for costs on such complaint. 

History of Section. 

P. L. 1899, ch. 678, § 2; P. L. 1901, ch. 853, §5; G. L. 1909, ch. 136, § 21; 

G. L. 1909, ch. 203, § 6; P. L. 1916, ch. 

1385, § 2; P. L. 1917, ch. 1535, § 1; G. L. 

1923, ch. 137, § 19; G. L. 1923, ch. 230, $6; G. L. 1938, ch. 230, § 5. 

20-1-21. Employees of division not entitled to share in fines. 

Any deputy in the division of fish and game who is employed by the said division, under pay, for a period exceeding two (2) consecutive days at a time, shall not be entitled to any portion of fines imposed, in case of arrests and convictions, during such period of employment, either under this chapter or under chapters 2, 24, 25, 27, 29, and 34 of this title. In such event the entire amount of fines imposed shall enure to the state.

P. L. 1909, ch. 381, § 9; G. L. 1923, ch. 139, § 9; G. L. 1938, ch. 254, § 9. 

20-1-22. Annual report of chief.-The said chief shall make annual report to the general assembly, at its January session, of his doings and the condition of the division, including a detailed statement of all moneys received and expended on account thereof; and also including the names of all of the lessees of oyster ground, the number of acres leased each person, and the value thereof. 

History of Section. 

G. L. 1896, ch. 170, § 3; P. L. 1901, ch. 809, § 39; P. L. 1901, ch. 853, § 3; 

P. L. 1901, ch. 927, § 1; G. L. 1909, ch. 

203, § 4; G. L. 1923, ch. 230, § 4; G. L. 

1938, ch. 230, § 9. 

20-1-23. Statutory references to administrator or wardens. 

Where in any general or special law, reference is made to "administrator of fish and game" the same shall mean the chief of the division of fish and game in the department of agriculture and conservation and his conservation officers as the case may be. 

Where in any general or special law, reference is made to "fish and game warden" the same shall mean conservation officer.

History of Section. 

P. L. 1954, ch. 3358, § 2. 10

CHAPTER 2 CO-OPERATION IN FEDERAL PROJECTS 

20-2-1. Federal fish-cultural work aythorized. 20-2-2. 

Assent to wildlife restoration projects. 20-2-3. 20-2-4. 

Disbursements for restoration projects.

Assent to fish restoration and management projects. 

20-2-5. Appropriations for fish restoration projects. 

20-2-6. Power of department and divi- 20-2-1. sion preserved.

Federal fish-cultural work authorized.-The fish and wildlife service of the department of the interior, or its duly authorized agents, are hereby granted the right to conduct fish-cultural work in the waters of this state, and to do such other necessary operations connected therewith as may by them be considered proper. 

P. L. 1914, ch. 1104, § 1; G. L. 1923, ch. 

235, § 48; G. L. 1938, ch. 247, § 1. 

20-2-2. Assent to wildlife restoration projects.-The state of 

Rhode Island hereby assents to the provisions of the act of congress entitled "An act to provide that the United States shall aid the states in wildlife restoration projects, and for other purposes," approved September 2, 1937 (public, No. 415, seventy-fifth congress), and the division of fish and game, with the approval of the director of agriculture and conservation, is hereby authorized, empowered and directed to perform such acts as may be necessary to the conduct and establishment of co-operative wildlife restoration projects, as defined in said act of congress, in compliance with said act and with rules and regulations promulgated by the secretary of the interior thereunder; and no funds accruing to the state of Rhode Island from license fees paid by hunters shall be diverted for any other purpose than the administration of the division of fish and game of the department of agriculture and conservation.

History of Section. 

P. L. 1938, ch. 2577, § 1; G. L. 1938, ch. 251, § 7. 

20-2-3. Disbursements for restoration projects.-The state controller is hereby authorized and directed to draw his orders upon the general treasurer for the payment of such sums as may be required for the purposes of $ 20-2-2, out of such funds as may, in accordance with the provisions hereof, at any time hereafter be 11

available therefor, upon receipt by him of proper vouchers duly authenticated and approved by the chief of the division of fish and game and by the director of agriculture and conservation. 

P. L. 1938, ch. 2577, § 3; G. L. 1938, ch. 

251, § 9; impl. am. P. L. 1939, ch. 660, 

§ 65. 

20-2-4. Assent to fish restoration and management projects.-The state of Rhode Island and Providence Plantations hereby assents to the provisions of the act of congress, entitled "An act to provide that the United States shall aid the states in fish restoration and management projects, and for other purposes," approved August 9, 1950 (public no. 681, 81st congress), and the division of fish and game, with the approval of the director of agriculture and conservation, is hereby authorized, empowered and directed to perform such acts as may be necessary to the conduct and establishment of fish restoration and management projects, as defined in said act of congress, in compliance with said act and with rules and regulations promulgated by the secretary of the interior thereunder, and no funds accruing to the state of Rhode Island from license fees paid by fishermen shall be diverted for any other purpose than the administration of the division of fish and game of the department of agriculture and conservation.

History of Section. 

P. L. 1951, ch. 2773, § 1. 

20-2-5. Appropriations for fish restoration projects.-The general assembly shall annually appropriate such sum as it may deem necessary to carry out the provisions of § 20-2-4, and the state controller is hereby authorized and directed to draw his orders upon the general treasurer for the payment of such sum, or so much thereof as may be required from time to time, upon receipt by him of properly authenticated vouchers.

History of Section. 

P. L. 1951, ch. 2773, § 3. 

20-2-6. Power of department and division preserved.-Nothing in §§ 20-2-2 to 20-2-5, inclusive, shall be construed to divest the department of agriculture and conservation or the division of fish and game of any power or authority now or heretofore vested in said department or division.

History of Section. 

P. L. 1938, ch. 2577, § 4; G. L. 1938, ch. 

251, § 10; P. L. 1951, ch. 2773, § 4. 12

CHAPTER 3 PROTECTION OF FISHING GROUNDS 

20-3-1. Depositing fish offal in tide waters. 

20-3-2. Seizure and forfeiture of vessels violating chapter. 

20-3-4. 

Oil extraction on board vessels. 

Placement of brush or tree limbs in Charlestown pond. 

20-3-1. Depositing fish offal in tide waters.-Every person who shall throw into or deposit in, or cause to be thrown into or to be deposited in, any of the public tidewaters of the state or upon the shores of any such tidewaters any fish offal or any water impregnated with fish, unless the same be filtered in such manner as may be determined by the town council of the town wherein such deposit shall be made, shall forfeit one hundred dollars ($100). 

G. L. 1896, ch. 175, § 1; G. L. 1909, ch. 

211, § 1; P. L. 1920, ch. 1914, § 17; G. L. 

1923, ch. 239, § 1; G. L. 1938, ch. 246, 

§ 1. 

20-3-2. Seizure and forfeiture of vessels violating chapter. 

Every vessel, craft, boat or floating apparatus employed in the procuring of fish oil, or in the dressing of bait for the mackerel fisheries, or the dressing of fish for other purposes, in violation of this chapter, shall be liable for any forfeiture and costs resulting from prosecution hereunder, and the same may be attached on the original writ and held as other personal property attached may be held, to secure any judgment which may be recovered in any action brought to enforce any such forfeiture; and any person upon view of any offense in violation of this chapter may seize and detain any vessel, craft, boat or floating apparatus, the same to be detained for a period not exceeding six (6) hours.

History of Section, 

G. L. 1896, ch. 175, § 2; G. L. 1909, ch. 

211, § 2; G. L. 1923, ch. 239, § 2; G. -L. 

1938, ch. 246, § 2. 

20-3-3. Oil extraction on board vessels.-Every person who shall boil any menhaden fish or press any fish for the purpose of extracting oil therefrom, on board of any vessel on any of the public tidewaters, shall be fined not exceeding fifty dollars ($50.00). 

History of Section. 

G. L. 1896, ch. 175, § 3; G. L. 1909, ch, 211, § 3; G. L. 1923, ch. 239, § 3; G. L. 

1938, ch. 246, § 3. 13

204. Placement of brush or tree limbs in Charlestown pond. 

Any person who shall wilfully place any brush, trees, or limbs of trees in any of the waters of Charlestown pond, shall be fined not more than twenty dollars ($20.00) nor less than five dollars ($5.00) for each offense, and all fines under this section shall enure one-half thereof to the use of the complainant and one-half thereof to the use of the town of Charlestown; provided, any lessee or lessees of oyster grounds in Charlestown pond may, upon obtaining permission of the town council of the town of Charlestown, place brush, shells, or limbs of trees on the grounds so leased, for the protection and cultivation of oysters.

G. L. 1896, ch. 175, § 4; 

1316, § 1; G. L. 1909, ch.

CHAPTER 4 PROPAGATION AND CULTIVATION OF FISH 20-4- 1. Shellfish projects - Notice of 

20-4- 2. 20-4- 3. 20-4- 4. 20-4-5. 20-4- 6. land taken.

201. Shellfish projects-Notice of land taken. The chief of the division of fish and game may in his discretion, from time to time, make any experiments in planting, cultivating, propagating, managing and developing, any and all kinds of shellfish; and for the purpose of so doing, may from time to time, take, hold, and occupy as long as he shall deem necessary, to the exclusion of all persons, in one or more parcels, any portion of the shores of the public waters of the state, or land within the state covered by tidewater, at either high or low tide, not within any harbor line, and which is not at the time of such taking, under lease as a private and several oyster fishery. Said chief upon taking such land, shall forthwith place or cause to be placed such stakes, bounds, or buoys, with the words "Division of Fish and Game, State of Rhode Island and Providence Plantations," marked or painted thereon, as will definitely locate or designate said land so taken; upon taking such land he shall forthwith give public notice thereof by advertisement in some newspaper published in the county in which said land is situated, which advertisement shall describe and give approximate location of said land. Said chief may make rules and regulations necessary for the protection and management of the land so taken, together with all animal life or any property situated within the lines thereof.

G. L., ch. 174, § 2, as enacted by P. L. 

1901, ch. 869, § 1; G. ¡L. 1909, ch. 210, § 3; P. L. 1917, ch. 1602, 1;; G. L. 1923, ch. 234, § 1; G. L. 1923, ch. 238, § 3; G.

20-4-2. Closure of land for shellfish projects.-The chief of the division of fish and game may in his discretion, from time to time, make any experiments in planting, cultivating, propagating, managing and developing any and all kinds of shellfish, upon the public fisheries (free and common), wherever he shall have planted any part, parcel or portion of any of the land upon the shores of the public waters of the state or any land within the state covered by tidewater, with any shellfish of any description; he may close, any land planted as aforesaid, to the use of the public, for such period of time as in his opinion may be necessary to complete the management undertaken. Said chief upon planting any land as aforesaid shall forthwith stake, buoy, or otherwise mark the bounds thereof with stakes, bounds or buoys, marked with the words "Division of 

Fish and Game, State of Rhode Island and Providence Plantations.”

20-4-3. -Any person violating any of the provisions of §§ 20-4-1 to 20-4-4, inclusive, or any of the regulations. made by the chief of the division of fish and game under the provisions of said sections, shall, upon conviction therefor, be subject to a fine of twenty dollars ($20.00) and costs.

History of Section. 

P. L. 1917, ch. 1602, § 3; G. L. 1923, ch. 234, § 3; G. L. 1938, ch. 237, § 3. 

20-4-4. Enforcement of provisions. It shall be the duty of the chief of the division of fish and game to enforce the provisions of $ 20-4-1 to 20-4-4, inclusive, and to prosecute any and all persons who, in his opinion, are guilty of the violation of any of the provisions of said sections, and the said chief and his duly appointed deputies shall not be required to enter into recognizance or become liable for costs. It shall be the duty of the attorney-general to conduct the prosecution of all cases brought by said chief or his duly appointed deputies under the provisions of $$ 20-4-1 to 20-4-4, inclusive.

History of Section. 

P. L. 1917, ch. 1602, § 4; G. L. 1923, ch. 

234, § 4; G. L. 1938, ch. 237, § 4. 

20-4-5. Cultivation of inland fish.-The division of fish and game shall introduce, protect, and cultivate fish in the inland waters of the state, and the chief of said division may make all needful regulations for the protection of such fish, and he shall prosecute for the violation of such regulations and of the laws of the state concerning inland fisheries.

20-4-6. Filing and publication of regulations.-The said chief shall cause a copy of any regulation made under the authority of § 20-4-5 to be filed in the office of the town clerk of any town in which any waters stocked with fish, or land occupied for experiments under the authority of § 20-4-1 and to which such regulations may apply, may be, and shall also cause a copy of such regulations. to be advertised in some newspaper published in the same county.

207. Co-operation with other states-Annual report-Prosecution of violations.-The chief of the division of fish and game may co-operate with the fish commissioners or other similar bodies or agencies of other states, and he shall make an annual report to the general assembly of his doings, with such facts and suggestions as he may deem proper. Said chief, whenever complaint is made by him for a violation of any regulation made by him, as aforesaid, or for violation of any of the provisions of this chapter or of chapters 3, 6, 12, 13, and 17 to 19, inclusive, of this title, shall not be required to enter into recognizance on such complaint or become liable for costs thereon.

20-4-8. Violations as to fish cultivation projects.-Every person who shall violate any of the regulations, made by the chief of the division of fish and game, under the authority of the provisions of §§ 20-4-5 and 20-4-6, or who shall take any fish, fish spawn, or any apparatus used in hatching or protecting fish, from any pond, lake, river, or stream stocked with or set apart by said chief, or by private parties for the protection and cultivation of fish with the consent of the town council of the town where such cultivation is carried on, without the consent of such chief or, if the cultivation of fish be carried on by a private party, without the consent of the person cultivating the same, or who shall trespass within the boundaries of any land which may be taken and occupied by said chief for his experiments in relation to shellfish, authorized by s 20-4-1, shall be fined not exceeding three hundred dollars ($300) or be imprisoned not exceeding six (6) months or be both fined and imprisoned, in the discretion of the court before which the offender shall be tried.

20-4-9. Acquisition of land for fish cultivation.-For the purpose of protecting and propagating fish and providing public fishing preserves, said chief may, in the name and for the use of the state, acquire by gift, lease, or purchase any land, fresh-water stream, lake, pond, or part thereof or rights and interests therein, upon such conditions as may be agreed upon between the owner and said chief; provided, that before any such property, rights, or interests are accepted by said chief or any payment made therefor, the deed, lease, gift or condition shall be approved by the attorney-general, 18

and no agreement shall be made by said chief for the payment of any sum in excess of the amount appropriated for the use of the chief, nor shall the state be obligated for future payments in excess of any amount that may be appropriated therefor. Said division shall have complete control over the land, water, rights and interests acquired as aforesaid and said chief may make such regulations for the protection of fish contained therein as he may deem best. Any person who shall violate any of the provisions of this section shall be fined not exceeding twenty dollars ($20.00) for each offense. 

G. L., ch. 238, § 18, as enacted by P. L.

20-4-10. Obstructions to fish culture.-The chief of the division of fish and game may personally or by deputy seize, and remove summarily if need be, all obstructions erected to hinder the passage of fish, or which are illegally erected to obstruct or in any way to impede the growth and culture of fish; and every person who shall rebuild or continue such obstruction after the same has been removed and notification thereof given by the chief of said division or his deputies, shall be fined fifty dollars ($50.00) for each offense.

20-4-11. Fishing in waters set aside for cultivation.-No person shall take or catch fish of any kind from any of the inland waters of the state, set apart by the division of fish and game for the cultivation of fish, except at such times and in such manner as is hereinafter provided.

20-4-12. Duration of prohibition of fishing-Ice fishing for black bass.-The prohibition of the catching of fish by hook and line, from fisheries stocked as hereinbefore provided, shall extend and be continued for and during the term of three (3) years from and after the time when such fishery was first established; provided, however, that fish may be caught through the ice only, and with hook and hand line only, in those ponds set apart for the cultivation of black bass, from the first day of December to the twentieth day of February until the expiration of the aforesaid term of three (3) years, 19

G. L. 1896, ch. 174, § 8; G. L. 1909, ch.

20-4-13. Open season in waters set aside for shad and salmon cultivation.-After the expiration of said three (3) years no fish shall be taken by any person from any waters legally set apart by said division for the cultivation of shad or salmon or within one (1) mile of the outlet of the streams so set apart, except from and after the fifteenth day of April until the fifteenth day of July, or at any time except by hook and hand line or by not less than three-inch mesh nets or seines.

20-4-14. Unlawful fishing in or near waters set aside.-Every person who shall catch any fish or shall use any seine for catching fish within half a mile from the mouth or outlet of any fishery set apart as is herein provided, and within any waters into which the waters of such fishery are let out, and every person who shall violate any of the provisions of §§ 20-4-11 to 20-4-13, inclusive, shall forfeit for the first offense the sum of fifty dollars ($50.00) and for every subsequent offense shall forfeit one hundred dollars ($100), and, in addition to the penalties therein provided, shall forfeit all the apparatus by him used in violation of the provisions of this section.

20-4-15. Taking of fish for culture or observation.-The division of fish and game may take fish from the fisheries hereinbefore referred to, for any purpose connected with fish culture or for scientific observation.

20-4-16. Entry of private property.-The chief of the division of fish and game and each duly authorized employee of said division may, in the discharge of his duties, enter upon and pass over private property without rendering himself liable in an action of trespass.

2017. Fresh-water hatchery.-Said division shall establish and maintain a fresh-water hatchery in this state for the purpose of providing trout, bass, and other fresh-water fish to be used by said division in the stocking of ponds and streams of the state; provided, however, that said division shall not stock any pond or stream unless the owner or owners of such portion or portions thereof as shall be stocked by said division shall first agree not to post said pond or stream against fishing for a period of at least three (3) years from date of such stocking. All the output of said hatchery not so used shall be sold at a fair market price and for cash only, and money received therefor shall be turned into the general treasury for the use of the state.

G. L., ch. 210, § 17, as enacted by P. L. 

1921, ch. 2060, § 1; G. L. 1923, ch. 238, § 17; G. L. 1938, ch. 240, § 15. 

20-4-18. Annual appropriations.-The general assembly shall annually appropriate such sum as it may deem necessary to carry into effect the provisions of §§ 20-4-5 to 20-4-19, inclusive.

20-4-19. Disposition of fines and forfeitures.-One-half of the fines and forfeitures recovered for violation of the provisions of §§ 20-4-5 to 20-4-18, inclusive, shall accrue to the complainant and one-half thereof to the use of the state.

201.. Fishing for which license required-Privileges not conferred by license. No male person above the age of fifteen (15) years shall catch or take any fish in any stream or fresh-water pond within this state, or place, or operate or superintend any device for the purpose of taking or catching fish in any of said waters, without having first obtained a license as hereinafter set forth; provided, however, that a license shall not be required of any citizen of this state or members of his immediate family to fish in any brook or pond running through or bordering upon land owned or leased by him and on which he is actually domiciled; and provided, further, that nothing in this chapter shall be construed as affecting in any way the provisions of the laws relating to trespass, nor as authorizing the placing, operating or superintending of any device for the purpose of taking or catching fish contrary to any laws now in force or which may be hereafter enacted, nor shall the possession of such license grant or confer any privilege not now enjoyed. 

P. L. 1921, ch. 2080, § 1; P. L. 1922, ch. 

2206, § 1; G. L. 1923, ch. 240, § 1; G. L. 

1938, ch. 231, § 1; P. L. 1946, ch. 1806, 

§1; P. L. 1947, ch. 1961, § 1. 

20-5-2. Issuance of fishing license.-The clerk of any city or town shall, upon the application of any person entitled to receive a license under this chapter, and upon payment of the license fee hereinafter 'specified, register such person and issue to him a license certificate in the form prescribed and upon a blank to be furnished by the division of fish and game which certificate shall bear the name, age, occupation, place of residence, signature and identifying description of the registrant, and shall authorize him to fish, subject to such conditions as are provided by law. The certificate shall be valid only until January first next following the date of issue, and shall not be transferable and shall be produced for examination upon the demand of any person. 

History of Section. 

P. L. 1921, ch. 2080, § 2; G. L. 1923, ch. 

240, § 2; G. L. 1938, ch. 231, § 2. 

20-5-3. Combination fishing and hunting license.-The clerk of any city or town shall upon application of any person entitled to receive a license under this chapter, grant a combination fishing and hunting license to any applicant who desires to receive the same in the form prescribed by the chief of the division of fish and game and upon payment of a fee of six dollars ($6.00) and a recording fee of twenty-five cents (25). Said combination license to fish. and hunt 23

shall expire on December 31 next succeeding the date of issue, shall not be transferable, shall be produced for examination upon demand of any person and may be revoked within the discretion of the chief of the division of fish and game.

§ 1. 

20-5-4. License fees.-Every person required to be licensed under the provisions of § 20-5-1 shall pay to the person from whom he procures his license, a fee therefor as follows: (1) Every citizen of the United States who has been a resident of this state for six (6) months prior to the date of his application for a license shall pay for such license a fee of three dollars twenty-five cents ($3.25); provided, however, that any such citizen who is under eighteen (18) years of age or over sixty-five (65) years of age may secure such license upon the payment of a fee of fifteen cents (15¢). (2) Every citizen of the United States not a resident of this state, or not having resided therein for a period of six (6) months preceding his application for a license, shall pay for such license a fee of five dollars and fifteen cents ($5.15); provided, however, that any such citizen of the United States may make application for a tourist's license and shall pay for such a license the fee of one dollar and fifty cents ($1.50) and shall be entitled to fish in Rhode Island waters for six (6) consecutive days. (3) Every person who is not a citizen of the United States but who has been a resident of this state for one (1) year prior to the date of his application for a license shall pay for such license a fee of five dollars and fifteen cents ($5.15). (4) Every person who is not a citizen of the United States and who has not resided in this state for a period of one (1) year prior to the date of his application for a license shall pay for such license a fee of seven dollars and fifteen cents ($7.15).

f 205. 

Extension of licenses of members of armed forces and merchant marine.-Every valid license to hunt or fish in this state, which is now held by any resident of this state upon joining the armed forces or the merchant marine of the United States, is hereby extended and shall be in force and valid until six (6) months following the termination of his service.

P. L. 1951, ch. 2703, § 1; P. L. 1956, ch. 3805, § 1. 

20-5-6. Issuance of licenses to members of armed forces and merchant marine.-Every member of the armed forces or of the merchant marine of the United States may hunt or fish in this state if such person procures a hunting or fishing license issued by the state of Rhode Island, the fee for which in such case shall be that charged for a resident civilian.

History of Section. 

P. L. 1951, ch. 2703, § 1; P. L. 1951, ch. 

2703, § 2, as enacted by P. L. 1956, ch. 3805, § 1. 

20-5-7. Free licenses to disabled veterans.-Every man or woman, who was a part of the armed forces of the United States government, and is now a one hundred per cent (100%) disabled veteran, may apply to the clerk of any city or town to receive a license to hunt and/or to fish in this state and upon the presentation of his or her necessary military discharge identification and/or disability papers shall, at the discretion of such city or town clerk, receive, without the payment of any license fee, a continuing special form of license. authorizing such man or woman to hunt and/or to fish in this state. for so long as he or she so desires; provided, however, that such man or woman, having once made application for such license, shall not again be required to appear before said licensing authority to present such papers.

209. Identifying device to be worn by licensees. In addition to either form of said certificate to be produced for examination upon the demand of any person, any such licensee, when fishing, shall wear upon his person while hunting such device as the chief of the division of fish and game shall prescribe, which shall be plainly visible, such device to be furnished by the division of fish and game at a minimum cost to the licensee.

20-5-10. Clerk's fee.-Out of the fees paid under the provisions of this chapter, the sum of fifteen cents (154) shall be retained by the clerk of the city or town in which the license is recorded.

20-5-11. Record of licenses issued-Accounting for fees.-Every city and town clerk shall record all licenses hereunder in books kept for that purpose, one (1) coupon of which shall be retained as his record. The said books shall be supplied by the division of fish and game, shall remain the property of the state, shall be open to public inspection during the usual office hours of the clerk, and shall be subject at all times to audit and inspection by the chief of said division, by the director of administration, or by their agents; and every such clerk shall, on the first Monday of every month, pay to the said division all moneys received by him for the said registrations issued during the month preceding, except the recording fee, together with a receipted bill for fees retained in accordance with § 20-5-10 and shall, within thirty (30) days succeeding January first of each year, return to the said division all registration books used during the year preceding, including all stubs and unused and void certificates. The chief of the division of fish and game shall pay to the general treasurer all money received by him for the said registrations issued during the previous month, and shall furnish the general treasurer with a list of the number and kind of registrations recorded by each city and town clerk during the said month.

20-5-14. False statements and violations-Cancelation of license. -Any person who wilfully makes a false representation as to birthplace or requirements of identification, or of other facts, or otherwise violates any provision of this chapter, or is any wise directly or indirectly a party to such violation, shall be punished by a fine of not more than twenty dollars ($20.00). The license of any person who shall be convicted of a violation of any of the laws relating to the fisheries of the state or of any provision of this chapter shall be void, and his license certificate shall be immediately surrendered to the clerk of the court in which conviction was entered and said clerk shall forthwith forward the same to the chief of the division of fish and game, who shall cancel the same and notify the clerk in whose city or town the certificate was recorded, of its cancelation; provided, however, that the license of any citizen of the state shall not be void because of failure to have his license certificate upon his person while fishing. No fee received for a certificate cancelled under the provisions of this section shall be returned. 

History of Section. 

P. L. 1921, ch. 2080, § 7; P. L. 1922, ch. 

2206, § 3; G. L. 1923, ch. 240, § 7: G. L. 

1938, ch. 231, § 7. 27

2015. Enforcement powers-Issuance of licenses by state or state agents. The chief of the division of fish and game shall have jurisdiction over all matters contained in this chapter and shall make all needful regulations for the purpose of enforcing the provisions thereof. Said chief may issue licenses hereunder in the same manner and under the same conditions as provided herein for city and town clerks. He may in his discretion appoint one or more responsible citizens of this state to act as his agents with authority to issue said licenses and may revoke the permits of said agents at his pleasure.

CHAPTER FISHING BY NONRESIDENTS SECTION.

20-6-1. Otter trawls and beam trawls.-No person, except he be a citizen of this state, shall take any fish with any.kind of beam or otter trawl or set any beam or otter trawl for the purpose of taking any fish therewith from the public waters of this state and it shall be unlawful for any person, firm or corporation to operate otter trawls or beam trawls in Sakonnet river, Mount Hope bay or Narragansett bay, or in Point Judith pond, so-called, in the towns of 

Narragansett and South Kingstown, or the Harbor of Refuge, so called, in the town of Narragansett, or in Potter pond, so-called, in the town of South Kingstown or in Great Salt pond, so-called, in the town of New Shoreham; provided, however, that it shall be lawful for any person who is a citizen of this state or corporation organized under the laws of this state to operate otter trawls or beam trawls in the area in Narragansett bay and in Sakonnet river south of a line extending from Quonset point, so-called, in the town of North Kingstown, thence running in the northeasterly direction to the southern tip of Hope island, thence in an easterly direction to Coggeshall point in the town of Portsmouth; thence south of a line extending from 

McCurry point, so-called, on the east side of the town of Portsmouth southeasterly in a line to Fogland point, so-called, in the town of Tiverton; and every person violating any provision of this section. shall be fined fifty dollars ($50.00) for each offense, one-half to use of the complainant and the other half to use of the state, or be imprisoned not exceeding twenty (20) days, or both, in the discretion of the court, and every firm or corporation violating any provision of this section shall be fined one hundred dollars ($100) for each offense, one-half to use of the complainant and the other half to use of the state.

G. L., ch. 207, § 46, as enacted by P. L. 

1915, ch. 1244, § 1; G. L. 1923, ch. 235, § 47; P. L. 1931, ch. 1692, § 1; G. L. 1938, ch. 245, § 4; P. L. 1939, ch. 737, § 1; P. L. 

1944, ch. 1528, § 1; P. L. 1953, ch. 3048, $1. 29

202. Carrying fish away in vessels.-Every person living without the state who shall take any lobsters, tautog, bass or other fish, within the harbors, rivers or waters of this state, for the purpose of carrying them thence in vessels or smacks, shall be fined ten dollars ($10.00) for every offense and shall forfeit all the fish or lobsters so taken.

G. L. 1896, ch. 171, § 26; G. L. 1909, ch. 207, § 32; G. L. 1923, ch. 235, § 37; 

G. L. 1938, ch. 245, § 1. 

20-6-3. Ponds on Connecticut boundary.-Any nonresident who shall hold a license to fish in the state of Connecticut may fish by angling in that portion of Beach pond which is located within the town of Exeter and of that portion of Killingly pond which is located within the town of Glocester without a license to fish by angling issued pursuant to the provisions of the statutes of this state; but such nonresident shall be subject to all of the provisions of the stat utes of this state relating to fishing in freshwater ponds and streams; provided that the state of Connecticut shall grant like privileges to persons holding a license to fish in Rhode Island. This section is reciprocal with chapter 88, acts of 1933, of the state of Connecticut.

CHAPTER 7 REGISTRATION AND REPORTS OF COMMERCIAL FISHERIES 

20-7-1. Registration of boats and devices. 

20-7-2. Monthly reports of commercial fisheries. 

20-7-3. Use of reports. 

20-7-4.  

20-7-1. Registration of boats and devices.-The owner of every boat, trap, seine, or other fishing gear commonly used in the commercial catching of marine fish, shellfish or lobsters shall cause such boat or device which is operating from any locality in the state of Rhode Island to be registered with the division of fish and game of the department of agriculture and conservation before such boat or fishing gear may be used for the commercial taking of marine fish, shellfish or lobsters. The chief of the division of fish and game shall issue a certificate of registration to each registrant.

P. L. 1953, ch. 3144, § 1; impl. am. P. 

L. 1954, ch. 3358, § 2. 

20-7-2. Monthly reports of commercial fisheries.-The owner of every boat, and the owner of every pound net, fish trap, weir, fyke net or similar contrivance, seine, drag or gill net, or other fishing gear, hereinafter referred to as devices, which are operating from any locality in the state of Rhode Island for commercial fishing purposes, shall monthly, on forms furnished by the division of fish and game, make a written report to the chief of said division, of the number of pounds of each kind of edible fish caught by him with such boat or such devices. The certificate of registration of any person who refuses, or knowingly or wilfully neglects to make the report required hereby or knowingly or wilfully makes a false report, may be suspended. Such suspension shall remain in force until the person affected shall comply with the provisions of this section. 

History of Section. 

P. L. 1953, ch. 3144, § 2; impl. am. P. 

L. 1954, ch. 3358, § 2. 31

203. Use of reports. Such reports shall not be made public and shall be kept only in the files of said division and used only for statistical purposes.

P. L. 1953, ch. 3144, § 3. 

20-7-4. -Whoever knowingly or wilfully violates any provision of this chapter may be punished by a fine of not less than ten dollars ($10.00) nor more than one hundred dollars ($100).

History of Section. 

P. L. 1953, ch. 3144, § 4. 32

201. Advisory council-Appointment of members.-Within the department of agriculture and conservation there shall be an advisory council on fish and game, consisting of seven (7) qualified electors of this state, who shall be appointed by the governor as herein provided. In the month of February in each year, the governor shall appoint a member or members of said council to hold office until the first day of March in the fifth year after such appointment and until his or their respective successor or successors are appointed and qualified to succeed the member or members whose term will next expire.

Any vacancy which may occur in said council shall be filled by appointment by the governor for the remainder of the unexpired term. The members of said council shall be chosen with due regard to their knowledge and interest in fish and game conservation. 

P. L. 1939, ch. 660, § 168; P. L. 1953, ch. 3157, § 1. 

20-1-2. Chairman of advisory council.-The members of said council shall elect one of their number as chairman upon the appointment of any new member for a full term and whenever the office may become vacant.

History of Section. 

P. L. 1939, ch. 660, § 168; P. L. 1953, ch. 3157, § 1. 

20-1-3. Secretarial service to council-Powers and duties.- 

Secretarial service shall be provided by the director of the department. Said council shall make suggestions to and advise the director of the department concerning the policies, rules and regulations of the department relating to fish and game restoration and conservation; provided, however, that said council shall have no administrative powers.

History of Section. 

P. L. 1939, ch. 660, § 168; P. L. 1953, ch. 3157, § 1. 

20-1-4. Hatcheries-Enforcement of fish and game laws.-The department and director of agriculture and conservation shall have charge of the Wickford hatchery and the Goose Nest Spring hatchery, and shall enforce the provisions of chapters 29 and 34 of this title and other provisions of law relating to birds, fish, and game. 

History of Section. 

P. L. 1939, ch. 660, § 163; P. L. 1939, ch. 718, § 11.

205. Office and facilities of division.-The department of ad ministration shall furnish the division of fish and game with suitable office and telephone service for the transaction of its business, where the maps, charts, books, leases, and the other property connected with said division shall be kept. 

G. L., ch. 137, § 39, as enacted by P.

20-1-6. Bond of division chief.-The chief of the division of fish and game, previous to entering upon the duties of his office, shall give a bond, with sureties satisfactory to the general treasurer, in the sum of one thousand dollars ($1,000), with condition to faithfully perform the duties of the office according to law.

P. L. 1901, ch. 853, § 2; G. L. 1909, ch. 203, § 2; G. L. 1923, ch. 230, § 2; G.

20-1-7. Control of fishing in Wallum Lake.-The chief of the division of fish and game in the department of agriculture and conservation may make suitable rules and regulations governing fishing in Wallum Lake, and for such purpose may confer with the division. of fisheries and game in the department of natural resources of the state of Massachusetts concerning fishing privileges to residents of 

Massachusetts in said Wallum Lake.

20-1-8. Fixing of seasons and bag limits.-Notwithstanding any provisions of the general laws and/or the public laws, the director of agriculture and conservation under the provisions of title 42, after holding a public hearing, notice of which shall be published twice a week for two (2) successive weeks in some daily newspaper published in the city of Providence, and with the approval of the governor, is hereby authorized and empowered to fix seasons and bag limits on any species of game, bird, fresh-water fish or fur-bearing animal in any specified locality or localities or the entire state when he shall find after investigation that such action is expedient. 

History of Section. 

P. L. 1949, ch. 2357, § 1.

209. Publication and effective period of seasons and bag limits. 

Notice of any such regulation so made shall appear in at least one (1) newspaper published in each county of the state once each week for two (2) consecutive weeks and such regulation shall become effective on a date to be specified in said regulation which date shall be subsequent to the date of the second publication. 

Any such regulation shall be and remain in effect for not longer than one (1) year from the effective date.

P. L. 1949, ch. 2357, § 1. 

20-1-10. Appointment and delegation of powers to conservation officers. The chief of the division of fish and game shall appoint such number of conservation officers as he may deem necessary, for the detection and prosecution of any violations of the laws of this state relating to fisheries, shellfisheries, wild birds and wild animals. 

Said chief may delegate any and all of his powers and duties to each of such conservation officers, who shall serve at his pleasure.

20-1-11. Records and reports of conservation officers.--Each conservation officer shall keep a record of his official acts, receipts, and expenditures and, when requested by the chief of the division of fish and game, shall make such report and in such form as the chief of the division of fish and game may prescribe. 

History of Section. 

G. L., ch. 230, § 3, as enacted by P. L. 

1954, ch. 3358, § 1. 

20-1-12. Enforcement powers of chief and conservation officers.

The chief of the division of fish and game, and each conservation officer, shall have the power: (1) To enforce all laws, rules and regulations relating to fish, game, fur-bearing animals, shellfish and lobsters, and to go on any property other than private dwellings, in the performance of his duties; (2) To execute all warrants and search warrants for the violation of laws, rules and regulations relating to fish, game, fur-bearing animals, shellfish and lobsters; 1 1

(3) To serve subpoenas issued for the trial of all offenses against the laws, rules and regulations relating to fish, game, fur-bearing animals, shellfish and lobsters; (4) To carry firearms or other weapons, concealed or otherwise, in the course of and in performance of the duties of such office; (5) To arrest without warrant and on view any person found violating any law, rule or regulation relating to fish, game, fur-bearing animals, shellfish and lobsters, take such person before a court having jurisdiction for trial, and detain such person in custody at the expense of the state until arraignment; (6) To seize and take possession of all fish, game, fur-bearing animals, shellfish or lobsters, which have been caught, taken, or killed, or had in possession, or under control of any person, or which have been shipped or are about to be shipped, at any time, in any manner, or for any purpose, contrary to the laws of this state; (7) To seize all fishing tackle, guns, shooting and hunting paraphernalia, hunting, fishing or trapping licenses, traps, decoys, tongs, bullrakes, dredges, or other implements or appliances used in violation of any law, rule or regulation relating to fish, game, furbearing animals, shellfish or lobsters when making an arrest, found in the execution of a search warrant, and hold the same at the owner's expense until the fine and costs imposed for the violation have been paid in full.

G. L., ch. 230, § 3, as enacted by P. L. 

1954, ch. 3358, § 1. or 

20-1-13. Deputy wardens.-The chief of the division of fish and game shall appoint such number of deputy wardens as he may deem. necessary. The deputy wardens shall not be authorized to carry pistols or revolvers but shall be authorized to detect violators of the laws of this state relating to fisheries, shellfisheries, wild birds and wild animals. Such detection shall be reported to the chief of the division of fish and game who may proceed with the prosecution. Deputy wardens shall be given an identification badge by said chief and shall serve at his pleasure.

History of Section. 

G. L., ch. 230, § 3, as enacted by P. L. 

1949, ch. 2301, § 1; P. L. 1954, ch. 3358, § 1. 

20-1-14. Oyster guards.-The said chief may also appoint such oyster guards as he may deem advisable for the detection and prosecution of any person committing depredations upon leased oyster

al ground. Each of said oyster guards shall be, by virtue of his office, a special constable, and as such oyster guard may, without warrant, arrest any person whom he may find taking oysters wrongfully from leased oyster ground and detain him for prosecution not exceeding forty-eight (48) hours, and may seize any boat or vessel used in such wrongful taking of oysters, together with her tackle, apparel and furniture and all implements belonging thereto. 

G. L., ch. 203, § 7, as enacted by P. L. 

1912, ch. 851, § 1; G. L. 1923, ch. 230, 

§7; G. L. 1938, ch. 230, § 4. 

20-1-15. Enforcement powers of division.-The chief of the divi sion of fish and game and his deputies and assistants shall protect the wild birds, wild animals, and shellfisheries throughout this state, and they shall enforce the law of this state and prosecute every person who shall violate any of the laws of this state relating to wild animals, wild birds, shellfisheries, fisheries, and the setting or attendance of snares and traps. They shall also have the power to execute all warrants and search warrants issued for the violation of the laws of this state with reference to wild birds or wild animals and the setting or attendance of snares or traps for wild birds or wild animals, and they shall have the power to arrest without warrant any person whom they find violating such laws; provided, that any person so arrested without warrant shall not be detained longer than twenty-four (24) hours, except as otherwise specifically provided by law.

20-1-16. Power to search without warrant.-The chief of the division of fish and game and his deputies and assistants shall have power to make search where they have reason to believe that wild animals, wild birds, shellfish, or fish, or any parts thereof, are possessed in violation of law; and without search warrant to examine the contents of any boat, car, wagon, or other vehicle, box, locker, basket, crate, game bag, or other package, or the contents of any building other than a dwelling house, to ascertain whether any of the provisions of the laws of this state relating to the protection of

wild animals, wild birds, shellfish, or fish, have been or are being violated, and to use such force as may be necessary for the purpose of such examination and inspection; and with a search warrant to search and examine the contents of any building or dwelling house; seize all wild animals, or wild birds, shellfish, or fish, or any parts thereof, possessed in violation of the law, or showing evidence of illegal taking, and hold the same subject to the order of the court issuing such search warrant. If any such wild birds or wild animals, shellfish, or fish shall be seized without search warrant under the provisions of this section, the same procedure shall be pursued as is hereinafter in this title provided for the forfeiture of wild birds and wild animals, shellfish, or fish taken under a search warrant. 

P. L. 1899, ch. 678, § 4; G. L. 1909, ch.

20-1-17. Regulations for enforcement of shellfishery laws-Appropriations. Said chief shall make all necessary regulations for enforcing the laws of the state relating to shellfisheries, and for executing the duties imposed upon him by law. 

The general assembly shall annually appropriate such sum as it may deem necessary for the purpose of enforcing said laws, and the state controller shall draw his orders on the general treasurer on receipt by him of proper vouchers, duly authenticated, and approved by the chief of said division of fish and game, and by the director of agriculture and conservation.

20-1-18. Operation of patrol boat.-The general assembly shall annually appropriate such sum as it may deem necessary to protect the oyster fisheries of the state, patrol and police the oyster grounds, to check licenses of "free fishermen," protect the scallop areas, collect egg lobsters, plant the fry and execute special work incidental to the lobster and other shellfisheries, said sum to be expended under the direction of the director of agriculture and conservation for the purpose of conditioning a patrol boat for continuous service, paying the salary of a man in charge of and to pilot said craft and providing fuel, coal and such other subsistence as may be necessary in said continuous patrol, police and other entailed services; and the

controller is hereby authorized and directed to draw his orders upon the general treasurer for the payment of such sum or sums, or so much thereof as may be required from time to time upon the receipt by him of proper vouchers approved by the director of agriculture and conservation.

G. L., ch. 230, § 4, as enacted by P. L. 

1940, ch. 908, § 1. 

20-1-19. Obligation of fishing vessels to heave to on command of patrol boat. Any fishing vessel or boat while operating in Rhode 

Island territorial waters shall immediately heave to upon command or signal of a marine patrol boat operated by the division of fish and game and carrying the identification of the state of Rhode Island. Said signal shall be a shouted command, or a white colored signal flare fired into the air. It shall be unlawful for the occupants of any fishing vessel or boat so commanded, or signaled by the division of fish and game marine patrol, to dump, destroy or throw anything from said vessel or boat. Any person violating the provisions of this section shall, upon conviction he fined twenty-five dollars ($25.00).

History of Section. 

G. L., ch. 230, §§ 11, 12, as enacted by 

P. L. 1954, ch. 3302, § 1. 

20-1-20. Prosecution of violations.-The chief of the division of fish and game and his deputies and assistants shall, by virtue of their respective offices, make complaints of any violations of the laws of this state relating to fisheries, shellfisheries, wild birds or wild animals, or relating to the setting or attendance of snares and traps, and they shall not be required, or any of them, to give recognizance or to furnish surety for costs or be liable for costs on such complaint. 

History of Section. 

P. L. 1899, ch. 678, § 2; P. L. 1901, ch. 853, §5; G. L. 1909, ch. 136, § 21;

1385, § 2; P. L. 1917, ch. 1535, § 1; G. L. 

1923, ch. 137, § 19; G. L. 1923, ch. 230, $6; G. L. 1938, ch. 230, § 5. 

20-1-21. Employees of division not entitled to share in fines. 

Any deputy in the division of fish and game who is employed by the said division, under pay, for a period exceeding two (2) consecutive days at a time, shall not be entitled to any portion of fines imposed, in case of arrests and convictions, during such period of employment, either under this chapter or under chapters 2, 24, 25, 27, 29, and 34 of this title. In such event the entire amount of fines imposed shall enure to the state.

P. L. 1909, ch. 381, § 9; G. L. 1923, ch. 139, § 9; G. L. 1938, ch. 254, § 9. 

20-1-22. Annual report of chief. The said chief shall make annual report to the general assembly, at its January session, of his doings and the condition of the division, including a detailed statement of all moneys received and expended on account thereof; and also including the names of all of the lessees of oyster ground, the number of acres leased each person, and the value thereof. 

History of Section. 

G. L. 1896, ch. 170, § 3; P. L. 1901, ch. 809, § 39; P. L. 1901, ch. 853, § 3; 

P. L. 1901, ch. 927, § 1; G. L. 1909, ch. 

203, § 4; G. L. 1923, ch. 230, § 4; G. L. 

1938, ch. 230, § 9. 

20-1-23. Statutory references to administrator or wardens. 

Where in any general or special law, reference is made to "administrator of fish and game" the same shall mean the chief of the division of fish and game in the department of agriculture and conservation and his conservation officers as the case may be. 

Where in any general or special law, reference is made to "fish and game warden" the same shall mean conservation officer.

History of Section. 

P. L. 1954, ch. 3358, § 2. 10

CHAPTER 2 CO-OPERATION IN FEDERAL PROJECTS SECTION. 

20-2-1. 

Federal fish-cultural work aythorized.

20-2-5. Appropriations for fish restoration projects.

20-2-1. Federal fish-cultural work authorized. The fish and wildlife service of the department of the interior, or its duly authorized agents, are hereby granted the right to conduct fish-cultural work in the waters of this state, and to do such other necessary operations connected therewith as may by them be considered proper. 

P. L. 1914, ch. 1104, § 1; G. L. 1923, ch. 

235, § 48; G. L. 1938, ch. 247, § 1. 

20-2-2. Assent to wildlife restoration projects.-The state of 

Rhode Island hereby assents to the provisions of the act of congress entitled "An act to provide that the United States shall aid the states in wildlife restoration projects, and for other purposes," approved September 2, 1937 (public, No. 415, seventy-fifth congress), and the division of fish and game, with the approval of the director of agriculture and conservation, is hereby authorized, empowered and directed to perform such acts as may be necessary to the conduct and establishment of co-operative wildlife restoration projects, as defined in said act of congress, in compliance with said act and with rules and regulations promulgated by the secretary of the interior thereunder; and no funds accruing to the state of Rhode Island from license fees paid by hunters shall be diverted for any other purpose than the administration of the division of fish and game of the department of agriculture and conservation. 

History of Section. 

P. L. 1938, ch. 2577, § 1; G. L. 1938, ch. 251, § 7. 

20-2-3. Disbursements for restoration projects.-The state controller is hereby authorized and directed to draw his orders upon the general treasurer for the payment of such sums as may be required for the purposes of $ 20-2-2, out of such funds as may, in accordance with the provisions hereof, at any time hereafter be 11

available therefor, upon receipt by him of proper vouchers duly authenticated and approved by the chief of the division of fish and game and by the director of agriculture and conservation. 

P. L. 1938, ch. 2577, § 3; G. L. 1938, ch. 

251, § 9; impl. am. P. L. 1939, ch. 660, 

§ 65. 

20-2-4. Assent to fish restoration and management projects.-The state of Rhode Island and Providence Plantations hereby assents to the provisions of the act of congress, entitled "An act to provide that the United States shall aid the states in fish restoration and management projects, and for other purposes," approved August 9, 1950 (public no. 681, 81st congress), and the division of fish and game, with the approval of the director of agriculture and conservation, is hereby authorized, empowered and directed to perform such acts as may be necessary to the conduct and establishment of fish restoration and management projects, as defined in said act of congress, in compliance with said act and with rules and regulations promulgated by the secretary of the interior thereunder, and no funds accruing to the state of Rhode Island from license fees paid by fishermen shall be diverted for any other purpose than the administration of the division of fish and game of the department of agriculture and conservation.

History of Section. 

P. L. 1951, ch. 2773, § 1. 

20-2-5. Appropriations for fish restoration projects.-The general assembly shall annually appropriate such sum as it may deem necessary to carry out the provisions of § 20-2-4, and the state controller is hereby authorized and directed to draw his orders upon the general treasurer for the payment of such sum, or so much thereof as may be required from time to time, upon receipt by him of properly authenticated vouchers.

History of Section. 

P. L. 1951, ch. 2773, § 3. 

20-2-6. Power of department and division preserved.-Nothing in §§ 20-2-2 to 20-2-5, inclusive, shall be construed to divest the department of agriculture and conservation or the division of fish and game of any power or authority now or heretofore vested in said department or division.

History of Section. 

P. L. 1938, ch. 2577, § 4; G. L. 1938, ch. 

251, § 10; P. L. 1951, ch. 2773, § 4. 12

CHAPTER 3 PROTECTION OF FISHING GROUNDS 

20-3-1. Depositing fish offal in tide waters.

20-3-1. Depositing fish offal in tide waters.-Every person who shall throw into or deposit in, or cause to be thrown into or to be deposited in, any of the public tidewaters of the state or upon the shores of any such tidewaters any fish offal or any water impregnated with fish, unless the same be filtered in such manner as may be determined by the town council of the town wherein such deposit shall be made, shall forfeit one hundred dollars ($100).

G. L. 1896, ch. 175, § 1; G. L. 1909, ch. 

211, § 1; P. L. 1920, ch. 1914, § 17; G. L. 

1923, ch. 239, § 1; G. L. 1938, ch. 246, § 1. 

20-3-2. Seizure and forfeiture of vessels violating chapter. 

Every vessel, craft, boat or floating apparatus employed in the procuring of fish oil, or in the dressing of bait for the mackerel fisheries, or the dressing of fish for other purposes, in violation of this chapter, shall be liable for any forfeiture and costs resulting from prosecution hereunder, and the same may be attached on the original writ and held as other personal property attached may be held, to secure any judgment which may be recovered in any action brought to enforce any such forfeiture; and any person upon view of any offense in violation of this chapter may seize and detain any vessel, craft, boat or floating apparatus, the same to be detained for a period not exceeding six (6) hours.

History of Section. 

G. L. 1896, ch. 175, § 2; G. L. 1909, ch. 

211, § 2; G. L. 1923, ch. 239, § 2; G. L. 

1938, ch. 246, § 2. 

20-3-3. Oil extraction on board vessels.-Every person who shall boil any menhaden fish or press any fish for the purpose of extracting oil therefrom, on board of any vessel on any of the public tidewaters, shall be fined not exceeding fifty dollars ($50.00). 

History of Section. 

G. L. 1896, ch. 175, § 3; G. L. 1909, ch, 211, § 3; G. L. 1923, ch. 239, § 3; G. L. 

1938, ch. 246, § 3. 13

204. Placement of brush or tree limbs in Charlestown pond.- 

Any person who shall wilfully place any brush, trees, or limbs of trees in any of the waters of Charlestown pond, shall be fined not more than twenty dollars ($20.00) nor less than five dollars ($5.00) for each offense, and all fines under this section shall enure one-half thereof to the use of the complainant and one-half thereof to the use of the town of Charlestown; provided, any lessee or lessees of oyster grounds in Charlestown pond may, upon obtaining permission of the town council of the town of Charlestown, place brush, shells, or limbs of trees on the grounds so leased, for the protection and cultivation of oysters.

201. Shellfish projects-Notice of land taken.-The chief of the division of fish and game may in his discretion, from time to time, make any experiments in planting, cultivating, propagating, managing and developing, any and all kinds of shellfish; and for the purpose of so doing, may from time to time, take, hold, and occupy as long as he shall deem necessary, to the exclusion of all persons, in one or more parcels, any portion of the shores of the public waters of the state, or land within the state covered by tidewater, at either high or low tide, not within any harbor line, and which is not at the time of such taking, under lease as a private and several oyster fishery. Said chief upon taking such land, shall forthwith place or cause to be placed such stakes, bounds, or buoys, with the words "Division of Fish and Game, State of Rhode Island and Providence Plantations," marked or painted thereon, as will definitely locate or designate said land so taken; upon taking such land he shall forthwith give public notice thereof by advertisement in some newspaper published in the county in which said land is situated, which advertisement shall describe and give approximate location of said land. Said chief may make rules and regulations necessary for the protection and management of the land so taken, together with all animal life or any property situated within the lines thereof.

G. L., ch. 174, § 2, as enacted by P. L.

20-4-2. Closure of land for shellfish projects.-The chief of the division of fish and game may in his discretion, from time to time, make any experiments in planting, cultivating, propagating, managing and developing any and all kinds of shellfish, upon the public fisheries (free and common), wherever he shall have planted any part, parcel or portion of any of the land upon the shores of the public waters of the state or any land within the state covered by tidewater, with any shellfish of any description; he may close, any land planted as aforesaid, to the use of the public, for such period of time as in his opinion may be necessary to complete the management undertaken. Said chief upon planting any land as aforesaid shall forthwith stake, buoy, or otherwise mark the bounds thereof with stakes, bounds or buoys, marked with the words "Division of 

Fish and Game, State of Rhode Island and Providence Plantations.”

20-4-3. -Any person violating any of the provisions of §§ 20-4-1 to 20-4-4, inclusive, or any of the regulations made by the chief of the division of fish and game under the provisions of said sections, shall, upon conviction therefor, be subject to a fine of twenty dollars ($20.00) and costs.

History of Section. 

P. L. 1917, ch. 1602, § 3; G. L. 1923, ch. 234, § 3; G. L. 1938, ch. 237, § 3. 

20-4-4. Enforcement of provisions.-It shall be the duty of the chief of the division of fish and game to enforce the provisions of §§ 20-4-1 to 20-4-4, inclusive, and to prosecute any and all persons who, in his opinion, are guilty of the violation of any of the provisions of said sections, and the said chief and his duly appointed deputies shall not be required to enter into recognizance or become liable for costs. It shall be the duty of the attorney-general to conduct the prosecution of all cases brought by said chief or his duly appointed deputies under the provisions of $$ 20-4-1 to 20-4-4, inclusive.

History of Section. 

P. L. 1917, ch. 1602, § 4; G. L. 1923, ch. 

234, 4; G. L. 1938, ch. 237, § 4. 

20-4-5. Cultivation of inland fish.-The division of fish and game shall introduce, protect, and cultivate fish in the inland waters of the state, and the chief of said division may make all needful regulations for the protection of such fish, and he shall prosecute for the violation of such regulations and of the laws of the state concerning inland fisheries.

20-4-6. Filing and publication of regulations.-The said chief shall cause a copy of any regulation made under the authority of § 20-4-5 to be filed in the office of the town clerk of any town in which any waters stocked with fish, or land occupied for experiments under the authority of § 20-4-1 and to which such regulations may apply, may be, and shall also cause a copy of such regulations to be advertised in some newspaper published in the same county.
