207. Co-operation with other states-Annual report-Prosecution of violations.-The chief of the division of fish and game may co-operate with the fish commissioners or other similar bodies or agencies of other states, and he shall make an annual report to the general assembly of his doings, with such facts and suggestions as he may deem proper. Said chief, whenever complaint is made by him for a violation of any regulation made by him, as aforesaid, or for violation of any of the provisions of this chapter or of chapters 3, 6, 12, 13, and 17 to 19, inclusive, of this title, shall not be required to enter into recognizance on such complaint or become liable for costs thereon.

G. L. 1896, ch. 174, § 2; P. L. 1901, ch. 

869, § 1; G. L. 1909, ch. 210, § 3; G. L. 

1923, ch. 238, § 3; G. L. 1938, ch. 240, § 1. 

20-4-8. Violations as to fish cultivation projects.-Every person who shall violate any of the regulations, made by the chief of the division of fish and game, under the authority of the provisions of §§ 20-4-5 and 20-4-6, or who shall take any fish, fish spawn, or any apparatus used in hatching or protecting fish, from any pond, lake, river, or stream stocked with or set apart by said chief, or by private parties for the protection and cultivation of fish with the consent of the town council of the town where such cultivation is carried on, without the consent of such chief or, if the cultivation of fish be carried on by a private party, without the consent of the person cultivating the same, or who shall trespass within the boundaries of any land which may be taken and occupied by said chief for his experiments in relation to shellfish, authorized by 20-4-1, shall be fined not exceeding three hundred dollars ($300) or be imprisoned not exceeding six (6) months or be both fined and imprisoned, in the discretion of the court before which the offender shall be tried.

History of Section.

1923, ch. 238, § 5; G. L. 1938, ch. 240, § 3. 

20-4-9. Acquisition of land for fish cultivation. For the purpose of protecting and propagating fish and providing public fishing preserves, said chief may, in the name and for the use of the state, acquire by gift, lease, or purchase any land, fresh-water stream, lake, pond, or part thereof or rights and interests therein, upon such conditions as may be agreed upon between the owner and said chief; provided, that before any such property, rights, or interests are accepted by said chief or any payment made therefor, the deed, lease, gift or condition shall be approved by the attorney-general, 18

and no agreement shall be made by said chief for the payment of a any sum in excess of the amount appropriated for the use of the chief, nor shall the state be obligated for future payments in excess of any amount that may be appropriated therefor. Said division shall have complete control over the land, water, rights and interests acquired as aforesaid and said chief may make such regulations for the protection of fish contained therein as he may deem best. Any person who shall violate any of the provisions of this section shall be fined not exceeding twenty dollars ($20.00) for each offense. 

G. L., ch. 238, § 18, as enacted by P. L. 

1927, ch. 991, § 1; G. L. 1938, ch. 240, § 4; G. L., ch. 240, § 25, as enacted by 

P. L. 1948, ch. 2118, § 1. 

20-4-10. Obstructions to fish culture.-The chief of the division of fish and game may personally or by deputy seize, and remove summarily if need be, all obstructions erected to hinder the passage of fish, or which are illegally erected to obstruct or in any way to impede the growth and culture of fish; and every person who shall rebuild or continue such obstruction after the same has been removed and notification thereof given by the chief of said division or his deputies, shall be fined fifty dollars ($50.00) for each offense.

20-4-11. Fishing in waters set aside for cultivation.-No person shall take or catch fish of any kind from any of the inland waters of the state, set apart by the division of fish and game for the cultiva tion of fish, except at such times and in such manner as is hereinafter provided.

History of Section,

20-4-12. Duration of prohibition of fishing-Ice fishing for black bass.-The prohibition of the catching of fish by hook and line, from fisheries stocked as hereinbefore provided, shall extend and be continued for and during the term of three (3) years from and after the time when such fishery was first established; provided, however, that fish may be caught through the ice only, and with hook and hand line only, in those ponds set apart for the cultivation of black bass, from the first day of December to the twentieth day of February until the expiration of the aforesaid term of three (3) years. 19

G. L. 1896, ch. 174, § 8; G. L. 1909, ch. 

210, § 9; G. L. 1923, ch. 238, § 9; G. L. 

1938, ch. 240, § 8. 

20-4-13. Open season in waters set aside for shad and salmon cultivation. After the expiration of said three (3) years no fish shall be taken by any person from any waters legally set apart by said division for the cultivation of shad or salmon or within one (1) mile of the outlet of the streams so set apart, except from and after the fifteenth day of April until the fifteenth day of July, or at any time except by hook and hand line or by not less than three-inch mesh nets or seines.

History of Section, 

G. L. 1896, ch. 174, § 10; G. L. 1909, ch. 

210, § 10; G. L. 1923, ch. 238, § 10; G. L. 

1938, ch. 240, § 9. 

20-4-14. Unlawful fishing in or near waters set aside.-Every person who shall catch any fish or shall use any seine for catching fish within half a mile from the mouth or outlet of any fishery set apart as is herein provided, and within any waters into which the waters of such fishery are let out, and every person who shall violate any of the provisions of §§ 20-4-11 to 20-4-13, inclusive, shall forfeit for the first offense the sum of fifty dollars ($50.00) and for every subsequent offense shall forfeit one hundred dollars ($100), and, in addition to the penalties therein provided, shall forfeit all the apparatus by him used in violation of the provisions of this section. 

History of Section, 

G. L. 1896, ch. 174, § 5; G. L. 1909, ch.

1938, ch. 240, § 5. 

20-4-15. Taking of fish for culture or observation.-The division of fish and game may take fish from the fisheries hereinbefore referred to, for any purpose connected with fish culture or for scientific observation.

20-4-16. Entry of private property.-The chief of the division of fish and game and each duly authorized employee of said division may, in the discharge of his duties, enter upon and pass over private property without rendering himself liable in an action of trespass.

2017. Fresh-water hatchery.-Said division shall establish and maintain a fresh-water hatchery in this state for the purpose of providing trout, bass, and other fresh-water fish to be used by said division in the stocking of ponds and streams of the state; provided, however, that said division shall not stock any pond or stream unless the owner or owners of such portion or portions thereof as shall be stocked by said division shall first agree not to post said pond or stream against fishing for a period of at least three (3) years from date of such stocking. All the output of said hatchery not so used shall be sold at a fair market price and for cash only, and money received therefor shall be turned into the general treasury for the use of the state.

G. L., ch. 210, § 17, as enacted by P. L. 

1921, ch. 2060, § 1; G. L. 1923, ch. 238, $17; G. L. 1938, ch. 240, § 15. 

20-4-18. Annual appropriations. The general assembly shall annually appropriate such sum as it may deem necessary to carry into effect the provisions of §§ 20-4-5 to 20-4-19, inclusive.

20-4-19. Disposition of fines and forfeitures.-One-half of the fines and forfeitures recovered for violation of the provisions of §§ 20-4-5 to 20-4-18, inclusive, shall accrue to the complainant and one-half thereof to the use of the state.

201.. Fishing for which license required-Privileges not conferred by license.-No male person above the age of fifteen (15) years shall catch or take any fish in any stream or fresh-water pond within this state, or place, or operate or superintend any device for the purpose of taking or catching fish in any of said waters, without having first obtained a license as hereinafter set forth; provided, however, that a license shall not be required of any citizen of this state or members of his immediate family to fish in any brook or pond running through or bordering upon land owned or leased by him and on which he is actually domiciled; and provided, further, that nothing in this chapter shall be construed as affecting in any way the provisions of the laws relating to trespass, nor as authorizing the placing, operating or superintending of any device for the purpose of taking or catching fish contrary to any laws now in force or which may be hereafter enacted, nor shall the possession of such license grant or confer any privilege not now enjoyed. 

P. L. 1921, ch. 2080, § 1; P. L. 1922, ch. 

2206, § 1; G. L. 1923, ch. 240, § 1; G. L. 

1938, ch. 231, § 1; P. L. 1946, ch. 1806, 

§ 1; P. L. 1947, ch. 1961, § 1. 

20-5-2. Issuance of fishing license. The clerk of any city or town shall, upon the application of any person entitled to receive a license under this chapter, and upon payment of the license fee hereinafter 'specified, register such person and issue to him a license certificate in the form prescribed and upon a blank to be furnished by the division of fish and game which certificate shall bear the name, age, occupation, place of residence, signature and identifying description of the registrant, and shall authorize him to fish, subject to such conditions as are provided by law. The certificate shall be valid only until January first next following the date of issue, and shall not be transferable and shall be produced for examination upon the demand of any person.

History of Section. 

P. L. 1921, ch. 2080, § 2; G. L. 1923, ch. 

240, § 2; G. L. 1938, ch. 231, § 2. 

20-5-3. Combination fishing and hunting license.-The clerk of any city or town shall upon application of any person entitled to receive a license under this chapter, grant a combination fishing and hunting license to any applicant who desires to receive the same in the form prescribed by the chief of the division of fish and game and upon payment of a fee of six dollars ($6.00) and a recording fee of twenty-five cents (254). Said combination license to fish. and hunt 23

shall expire on December 31 next succeeding the date of issue, shall not be transferable, shall be produced for examination upon demand of any person and may be revoked within the discretion of the chief of the division of fish and game.

§ 1. 

20-5-4. License fees. Every person required to be licensed under the provisions of § 20-5-1 shall pay to the person from whom he procures his license, a fee therefor as follows: (1) Every citizen of the United States who has been a resident of this state for six (6) months prior to the date of his application for a license shall pay for such license a fee of three dollars twenty-five cents ($3.25); provided, however, that any such citizen who is under eighteen (18) years of age or over sixty-five (65) years of age may secure such license upon the payment of a fee of fifteen cents (15¢). (2) Every citizen of the United States not a resident of this state, or not having resided therein for a period of six (6) months preceding his application for a license, shall pay for such license a fee of five dollars and fifteen cents ($5.15); provided, however, that any such citizen of the United States may make application for a tourist's license and shall pay for such a license the fee of one dollar and fifty cents ($1.50) and shall be entitled to fish in Rhode Island waters for six (6) consecutive days. (3) Every person who is not a citizen of the United States but who has been a resident of this state for one (1) year prior to the date of his application for a license shall pay for such license a fee of five dollars and fifteen cents ($5.15). (4) Every person who is not a citizen of the United States and who has not resided in this state for a period of one (1) year prior to the date of his application for a license shall pay for such license a fee of seven dollars and fifteen cents ($7.15).

History of Section. 

P. L. 1921, ch. 2080, § 3; P. L. 1922, ch.

205. Extension of licenses of members of armed forces and merchant marine.—Every valid license to hunt or fish in this state, which is now held by any resident of this state upon joining the armed forces or the merchant marine of the United States, is hereby extended and shall be in force and valid until six (6) months following the termination of his service.

P. L. 1951, ch. 2703, § 1; P. L. 1956, ch. 3805, § 1. 

20-5-6. Issuance of licenses to members of armed forces and merchant marine.-Every member of the armed forces or of the merchant marine of the United States may hunt or fish in this state if such person procures a hunting or fishing license issued by the state of Rhode Island, the fee for which in such case shall be that charged for a resident civilian.

History of Section. 

P. L. 1951, ch. 2703, § 1; P. L. 1951, ch. 

2703, § 2, as enacted by P. L. 1956, ch. 3805, § 1. 

20-5-7. Free licenses to disabled veterans.-Every man or woman, who was a part of the armed forces of the United States government, and is now a one hundred per cent (100%) disabled veteran, may apply to the clerk of any city or town to receive a license to hunt and/or to fish in this state and upon the presentation of his or her necessary military discharge identification and/or disability papers shall, at the discretion of such city or town clerk, receive, without the payment of any license fee, a continuing special form of license authorizing such man or woman to hunt and/or to fish in this state for so long as he or she so desires; provided, however, that such man or woman, having once made application for such license, shall not again be required to appear before said licensing authority to present such papers.

History of Section. 

P. L. 1948, ch. 2019, § 1. 

20-5-8. Replacement of lost or destroyed certificate.-Whoever loses, or by a mistake or accident destroys his certificate of registration may, upon application to the division of fish and game, accompanied by an affidavit fully setting forth the circumstances of the loss, receive without charge a duplicate certificate for the remainder of the year covered by the original certificate, History of Section. 

P. L. 1921, ch. 2080, § 5; G. L. 1923, ch. 

240, §5; G. L. 1938, ch. 231, § 5. 25

209. Identifying device to be worn by licensees. In addition to either form of said certificate to be produced for examination upon the demand of any person, any such licensee, when fishing, shall wear upon his person while hunting such device as the chief of the division of fish and game shall prescribe, which shall be plainly visible, such device to be furnished by the division of fish and game at a minimum cost to the licensee.

§ 1. 

20-5-10. Clerk's fee.-Out of the fees paid under the provisions of this chapter, the sum of fifteen cents (154) shall be retained by the clerk of the city or town in which the license is recorded. 

History of Section. 

P. L. 1921, ch. 2080, § 4; G. L. 1923, ch. 

240, § 4; G. L. 1938, ch. 231, § 4. 

20-5-11. Record of licenses issued-Accounting for fees.-Every city and town clerk shall record all licenses hereunder in books kept for that purpose, one (1) coupon of which shall be retained as his record. The said books shall be supplied by the division of fish and game, shall remain the property of the state, shall be open to public inspection during the usual office hours of the clerk, and shall be subject at all times to audit and inspection by the chief of said division, by the director of administration, or by their agents; and every such clerk shall, on the first Monday of every month, pay to the said division all moneys received by him for the said registrations issued during the month preceding, except the recording fee, together with a receipted bill for fees retained in accordance with § 20-5-10 and shall, within thirty (30) days succeeding January first of each year, return to the said division all registration books used during the year preceding, including all stubs and unused and void certificates. The chief of the division of fish and game shall pay to the general treasurer all money received by him for the said registrations issued during the previous month, and shall furnish the general treasurer with a list of the number and kind of registrations recorded by each city and town clerk during the said month.

History of Section.

and are hereby appropriated to said department for the protection and propagation of fish and game; and the state controller is hereby authorized and directed to draw his orders upon the general treasurer for the payment of such sum or sums as may be necessary from 0 time to time upon receipt by him of duly authenticated vouchers I presented by the chief of the division of fish and game; provided, however, nothing in this section shall be construed to deprive the city and town clerks of their recording fees.

Leasing of land and rights-of-way to streams and ponds to be stocked with fish by the division of fish and game and to be opened to public fishing. b. For stream improvement. C.

For the enforcement of fish and game laws. d. For the protection and propagation of fish and game.

20-5-14. False statements and violations-Cancelation of license. -Any person who wilfully makes a false representation as to birthplace or requirements of identification, or of other facts, or otherwise violates any provision of this chapter, or is any wise directly or indirectly a party to such violation, shall be punished by a fine of not more than twenty dollars ($20.00). The license of any person who shall be convicted of a violation of any of the laws relating to the fisheries of the state or of any provision of this chapter shall be void, and his license certificate shall be immediately surrendered to the clerk of the court in which conviction was entered and said clerk shall forthwith forward the same to the chief of the division of fish and game, who shall cancel the same and notify the clerk in whose city or town the certificate was recorded, of its cancelation; provided, however, that the license of any citizen of the state shall not be void because of failure to have his license certificate upon his person while fishing. No fee received for a certificate cancelled under the provisions of this section shall be returned.

20-6-1. Otter trawls and beam trawls.-No person, except he be a citizen of this state, shall take any fish with any. kind of beam or otter trawl or set any beam or otter trawl for the purpose of taking any fish therewith from the public waters of this state and it shall be unlawful for any person, firm or corporation to operate otter trawls or beam trawls in Sakonnet river, Mount Hope bay or Narragansett bay, or in Point Judith pond, so-called, in the towns of 

Narragansett and South Kingstown, or the Harbor of Refuge, so called, in the town of Narragansett, or in Potter pond, so-called, in the town of South Kingstown or in Great Salt pond, so-called, in the town of New Shoreham; provided, however, that it shall be lawful for any person who is a citizen of this state or corporation organized under the laws of this state to operate otter trawls or beam trawls in the area in Narragansett bay and in Sakonnet river south of a line extending from Quonset point, so-called, in the town of North Kingstown, thence running in the northeasterly direction to the southern tip of Hope island, thence in an easterly direction to Coggeshall point in the town of Portsmouth; thence south of a line extending from 

McCurry point, so-called, on the east side of the town of Portsmouth southeasterly in a line to Fogland point, so-called, in the town of Tiverton; and every person violating any provision of this section shall be fined fifty dollars ($50.00) for each offense, one-half to use of the complainant and the other half to use of the state, or be imprisoned not exceeding twenty (20) days, or both, in the discretion of the court, and every firm or corporation violating any provision of this section shall be fined one hundred dollars ($100) for each offense, one-half to use of the complainant and the other half to use of the state.

202. Carrying fish away in vessels.-Every person living without the state who shall take any lobsters, tautog, bass or other fish, within the harbors, rivers or waters of this state, for the purpose of carrying them thence in vessels or smacks, shall be fined ten dollars ($10.00) for every offense and shall forfeit all the fish or lobsters so taken.

20-6-3. Ponds on Connecticut boundary.-Any nonresident who shall hold a license to fish in the state of Connecticut may fish by angling in that portion of Beach pond which is located within the town of Exeter and of that portion of Killingly pond which is located within the town of Glocester without a license to fish by angling issued pursuant to the provisions of the statutes of this state; but such nonresident shall be subject to all of the provisions of the stat utes of this state relating to fishing in freshwater ponds and streams; provided that the state of Connecticut shall grant like privileges to persons holding a license to fish in Rhode Island. This section is reciprocal with chapter 88, acts of 1933, of the state of Connecticut.

CHAPTER 7 

REGISTRATION AND REPORTS OF COMMERCIAL FISHERIES 

20-7-1. Registration of boats and devices. 

20-7-2. Monthly reports of commercial fisheries.

20-7-4.  

20-7-1. Registration of boats and devices.-The owner of every boat, trap, seine, or other fishing gear commonly used in the commercial catching of marine fish, shellfish or lobsters shall cause such boat or device which is operating from any locality in the state of Rhode Island to be registered with the division of fish and game of the department of agriculture and conservation before such boat or fishing gear may be used for the commercial taking of marine fish, shellfish or lobsters. The chief of the division of fish and game shall issue a certificate of registration to each registrant.

P. L. 1953, ch. 3144, § 1; impl. am. P. 

L. 1954, ch. 3358, § 2. 

20-7-2. Monthly reports of commercial fisheries.-The owner of every boat, and the owner of every pound net, fish trap, weir, fyke net or similar contrivance, seine, drag or gill net, or other fishing gear, hereinafter referred to as devices, which are operating from any locality in the state of Rhode Island for commercial fishing purposes, shall monthly, on forms furnished by the division of fish and game, make a written report to the chief of said division, of the number of pounds of each kind of edible fish caught by him with such boat or such devices. The certificate of registration of any person who refuses, or knowingly or wilfully neglects to make the report required hereby or knowingly or wilfully makes a false report, may be suspended. Such suspension shall remain in force until the person affected shall comply with the provisions of this section. 

History of Section. 

P. L. 1953, ch. 3144, § 2; impl. am. P. 

L. 1954, ch. 3358, § 2. 31

P. L. 1953, ch. 3144, § 3.

CHAPTER ATLANTIC STATES MARINE FISHERIES COMPACT SECTION.

201. Entry into compact authorized-Text.-The governor of this state is hereby authorized and directed to execute a compact on behalf of the state of Rhode Island with any one or more of the states of Maine, New Hampshire, Massachusetts, Connecticut, New York, New Jersey, Delaware, Maryland, Virginia, North Carolina, South

Carolina, Georgia and Florida and with such other states as may enter into the compact, legally joining therein in the form substantially as follows: 

ATLANTIC STATES MARINE FISHERIES 

COMPACT The contracting states solemnly agree:

ARTICLE I 

The purpose of this compact is to promote the better utilization of the fisheries, marine, shell and anadromous, of the Atlantic seaboard by the development of a joint program for the promotion and protection of such fisheries, and by the prevention of the physical waste of the fisheries from any cause. It is not the purpose of this compact to authorize the states joining herein to limit the production of fish or fish products for the purpose of establishing or fixing the price thereof, or creating and perpetuating monopoly. 

ARTICLE II 

This agreement shall become operative immediately as to those states executing it whenever any two or more of the states of Maine, New Hampshire, Massachusetts, Rhode Island, Connecticut, New 

York, New Jersey, Delaware, Maryland, Virginia, North Carolina,

South Carolina, Georgia and Florida have executed it in the form that is in accordance with the laws of the executing state and the congress has given its consent. Any state contiguous with any of the aforementioned states and riparian upon waters frequented by anadromous fish, flowing into waters under the jurisdiction of any of the aforementioned states, may become a party hereto as hereinafter provided.

ARTICLE III 

Each state joining herein shall appoint three (3) representatives to a commission hereby constituted and designated as the Atlantic states marine fisheries commission. One shall be the executive officer of the administrative agency of such state charged with the conservation of the fisheries resources to which this compact pertains or, if there be more than one (1) officer or agency, the official of that state named by the governor thereof The second shall be a member of the legislature of such state designated by the commission or 34

committee on interstate co-operation of such state, or if there be none, or if said commission on interstate co-operation cannot constitutionally designate the said member, such legislator shall be designated by the governor thereof; provided, that if it is constitutionally impossible to appoint a legislator as a commissioner from such state, the second member shall be appointed by the governor of said state in his discretion. The third shall be a citizen who shall have a knowledge of and interest in the marine fisheries problem to be appointed by the governor. This commission shall be a body corporate with the powers and duties set forth herein. 

ARTICLE IV 

The duty of the said commission shall be to make inquiry and ascertain from time to time such methods, practices, circumstances and conditions as may be disclosed for bringing about the conserva- I tion and the prevention of the depletion and physical waste of the fisheries, marine, shell and anadromous, of the Atlantic seaboard. 

The commission shall have power to recommend the co-ordination of the exercise of the police powers of the several states within their respective jurisdictions to promote the preservation of those fisheries and their protection against overfishing, waste, depletion or any abuse whatsoever and to assure a continuing yield from the fisheries resources of the aforementioned states. 1 To that end the commission shall draft and, after consultation with the advisory committee hereinafter authorized, recommend to the governors and legislatures of the various signatory states legislation dealing with the conservation of the marine, shell and anadromous fisheries of the Atlantic seaboard. The commission shall, more than one (1) month prior to any regular meeting of the legislature in any signatory state, present to the governor of the state its recommendations relating to enactments to be made by the legislature of that state in furthering the intents and purposes of this compact.

The commission shall consult with and advise the pertinent administrative agencies in the states party hereto with regard to problems connected with the fisheries and recommend the adoption of such regulations as it deems advisable. 

The commission shall have power to recommend to the states party hereto the stocking of the waters of such states with fish and fish eggs or joint stocking by some or all of the states party hereto and when two or more of the states shall jointly stock waters the commission shall act as the co-ordinating agency for such stocking. 3'5

ARTICLE V 

The commission shall elect from its number a chairman and a vice chairman and shall appoint and at its pleasure remove or discharge such officers and employees as may be required to carry the provisions of this compact into effect and shall fix and determine their duties, qualifications and compensation. Said commission shall adopt rules and regulations for the conduct of its business. It may establish and maintain one or more offices for the transaction of its business and may meet at any time or place but must meet at least once a year.

ARTICLE VI 

No action shall be taken by the commission in regard to its general affairs except by the affirmative vote of a majority of the whole number of compacting states present at any meeting. No recommendation shall be made by the commission in regard to any species of fish except by the affirmative vote of a majority of the compacting states which have an interest in such species. The commission shall define what shall be an interest.

ARTICLE VII 

The fish and wildlife service of the department of the interior of the government of the United States shall act as the primary research agency of the Atlantic states marine fisheries commission co-operating with the research agencies in each state for that purpose. Representatives of the said fish and wildlife service shall attend the meetings of the commission.

An advisory committee to be representative of the commercial fishermen and the salt-water anglers and such other interests of each state as the commission deems advisable shall be established by the commission as soon as practicable for the purpose of advising the commission upon such recommendations as it may desire to make, ARTICLE VIII 

When any state other than those named specifically in article II of this compact shall become a party thereto for the purpose of conserving its anadromous fish in accordance with the provisions of article II the participation of such state in the action of the commission shall be limited to such species of anadromous fish. 

ARTICLE IX 

Nothing in this compact shall be construed to limit the powers of any signatory state or to repeal or prevent the enactment of any legislation or the enforcement of any requirement by any signatory state imposing additional conditions and restrictions to conserve its fisheries. 36

2. 2. 

ARTICLEX 

Continued absence of representation or of any representative on the commission from any state party hereto shall be brought to the attention of the governor thereof.

ARTICLE XI 

The states party hereto agree to make annual appropriations to the support of the commission in proportion to the primary market value of the products of their fisheries, exclusive of cod and haddock, as recorded in the most recent published reports of the fish and wildlife service of the United States department of the interior, provided, no state shall contribute less than two hundred dollars ($200) per annum and the annual contribution of each state above the minimum shall be figured to the nearest one hundred dollars ($100).

The compacting states agree to appropriate initially the annual amounts scheduled below, which amounts are calculated in the manner set forth herein, on the basis of the catch record of 1938. Subsequent budgets shall be recommended by a majority of the commission. and the cost thereof allocated equitably among the states in accordance with their respective interests and submitted to the compacting states.

Schedule of Initial Annual State Contributions.

South Carolina Georgia Florida $700 200 2300 300 400 1300 800 200 700 1300 600 200 200 1500

ARTICLE XII 

This compact shall continue in force and remain binding upon each compacting state until renounced by it. Renunciation of this compact must be preceded by sending six (6) months' notice in writing of intention to withdraw from the compact to the other states party hereto. 37

P. L 1941, ch. 1021, § 1. 

20-8-2. Amendment of compact.-The state of Rhode Island here by enters into an amendment of the Atlantic states marine fisheries compact with any one or more of the states of Maine, New Hampshire, Massachusetts, Connecticut, New York, New Jersey, Pennsylvania, Delaware, Maryland, Virginia, South Carolina, Georgia, and

Florida or such other states as may become party to that compact for the purpose of permitting the states that ratify this amendment to establish joint regulation of specific fisheries common to those states through the Atlantic states marine fisheries commission and their representatives on that body. Notice of intention to withdraw from this amendment shall be executed and transmitted by the governor and shall be in accordance with article XII of the Atlantic states marine fisheries compact and shall be effective as to this state with those states which similarly ratify this amendment. This amendment shall take effect as to this state with respect to such other of the aforesaid states as take similar action. 

AMENDMENT No. 1 OF THE ATLANTIC STATES MARINE FISHERIES COMPACT 

The states consenting to this amendment agree that any two or more of them may designate the Atlantic states marine fisheries commission as a joint regulatory agency with such powers as they may jointly confer from time to time for the regulation of the fishing operations of the citizens and vessels of such designating states with respect to specific fisheries in which such states have a common interest. The representatives of such states on the Atlantic states marine fisheries commission shall constitute a separate section of such commission for the exercise of the additional powers so granted provided that the states so acting shall appropriate additional funds for this purpose. The creation of such section as a joint regulatory agency shall not deprive the states participating therein of any of their privileges or powers or responsibilities in the Atlantic states marine fisheries commission under the general compact. 

History of Section. 

P. L. 1949, ch. 2360, § 1. 

20-8-3. Rhode Island representatives on interstate commission- 

Ex officio members.-In pursuance of article III of said compact there shall be three (3) members, hereinafter called commissioners, of the Atlantic states marine fisheries commission, hereinafter called commission, from the state of Rhode Island. The first commissioner from the state of Rhode Island shall be the director of agriculture 38

and conservation of the state of Rhode Island, ex officio, and the term of any such ex officio commissioner shall terminate at the time he ceases to hold said office of director of agriculture and conservation and his successor as commissioner shall be his successor as director of agriculture and conservation. The second commissioner from the state of Rhode Island shall be a legislator and member of the commission on interstate co-operation of the state of Rhode Island, ex officio, designated by said commission on interstate cooperation, and the term of any such ex officio commissioner shall terminate at the time he ceases to hold said legislative office or said office as commissioner on interstate co-operation, and his successor as commissioner shall be named in like manner. 

P. L. 1941, ch. 1021, § 2. 

20-8-4. Appointive commissioner. The governor, by and with the advice and consent of the senate, shall appoint a citizen as a third commissioner who shall have a knowledge of and interest in the marine fisheries problem. The term of said commissioner shall be three (3) years and he shall hold office until his successor shall be appointed and qualified. Vacancies occurring in the office of such commissioner from any reason or cause shall be filled by appointment by the governor, by and with the advice and consent of the senate, for the unexpired term.

History of Section. 

P. L. 1941, ch. 1021, § 2.

Powers of commission Co-operation and information furnished by state agencies.-There is hereby granted to the commission and the commissioners thereof all the powers provided for in the said compact and all the powers necessary or incidental to the carrying out of said compact in every particular. all things falling within their respective provinces and jurisdiction necessary or incidental to the carrying out of said compact in every particular; it being hereby declared to be the policy of the state of 

Rhode Island to perform and carry out the said compact and to accomplish the purposes thereof. All officers, bureaus, departments and persons of and in the state government or administration of the state of Rhode Island are hereby authorized and directed at convenient times and upon request of the said commission to furnish the said commission with information and data possessed by them or any of them and to aid said commission by loan of personnel or other means lying within their legal rights respectively. 

P. L. 1941, ch. 1021, § 3. 

20-8-8. Powers granted supplemental. Any powers herein granted to the commission shall be regarded as in aid of and supplemental to and in no case a limitation upon any of the powers vested in said commission by other laws of the state of Rhode Island or by the laws of the states of Maine, New Hampshire, Massachusetts, Connecticut, New York, New Jersey, Delaware, Maryland, Virginia, North Carolina, South Carolina, Georgia and Florida or by the congress or the terms of said compact.

20-8-9. Accounts and reports Legislative recommendations.

The commission shall keep accurate accounts of all receipts and disbursements and shall report to the governor and the legislature of the state of Rhode Island on or before the tenth day of December in each year, setting forth in detail the transactions conducted by it during the twelve (12) months preceding December 1st of that year and shall make recommendations for any legislative action deemed by it advisable, including amendments to the statutes of the state of Rhode Island which may be necessary to carry out the intent and purposes of the compact between the signatory states.

20-8-12. Severability of provisions. If any provision of this chapter or the application thereof to any person or circumstances is held invalid, such invalidity shall not affect other provisions or applications of the chapter which can be given effect without the invalid provision or application, and to this end the provisions of this chapter are declared to be severable.

201. Oyster season.-No person shall take any oysters from the free and common oyster fisheries northerly of Field's Point and Kettle Point in this state between the first day of June in any year and the first day of April in the following year, or from the free and common oyster fisheries in any of the other waters of this state between the fifteenth day of May and the fifteenth day of September of any year. Any person who shall take any oysters or expose any oysters for sale taken from the free and common fisheries aforesaid in violation of the foregoing provisions shall be fined twenty dollars ($20.00) and costs for each offense.

P. L. 1912, ch. 852, § 1; G. L. 1923, ch. 

229, § 1; G. L. 1938, ch. 232, § 1. 

20-9-2. License for oysters, clams, or quahaugs.-The chief of the division of fish and game may, from time to time, issue a license to any person a resident of this state, permitting him to take oysters, clams or quahaugs from the free and common fisheries, subject to the provisions of this chapter, and may also issue a registration to the owner of any boat used in taking oysters, clams or quahaugs from the free and common fisheries, and may order the holder of any license, issued as aforesaid, to appear before him to answer to any charges by whomsoever preferred, of violations of the laws of this state, or of the rules and regulations of said chief, relating to the shellfisheries, and if said chief deems such charges to be substantiated, he may revoke and terminate said license. Said chief may make such rules and regulations, from time to time, as he may deem expedient for the issuing of said licenses, but no license issued under this chapter shall be valid, however, before the licensee shall endorse his name in his own handwriting thereon, and no such license shall be transferable; provided, however, that once having issued any such license to any person, said chief shall not refuse to renew the same except for cause shown.

History of Section. 

P. L. 1912, ch. 852, § 2; G. L. 1923, ch.

§ 1. 

20-9-3. License fees. Each person shall pay the said division of fish and game the sum of twenty-five dollars ($25.00) for a license to fish from the free and common fisheries; provided however, that any resident of this state over sixty-five (65) years of age shall pay a fee of one dollar ($1.00) for such license; and the owner of each 43

boat used in fishing for oysters, clams or quahaugs from the free and common fisheries shall pay to the said division the sum of one dollar ($1.00) for a registration to so use said boat.

P. L. 1912, ch. 852, § 4; G. L. 1923, ch. 

229, § 4; G. L. 1938, ch. 232, § 4; P. L. 

1956, ch. 3813, § 1. 

20-9-4. Nonresident license.-Every nonresident not a real estate taxpayer may obtain a special license to take in any one (1) day not more than one (1) bushel, each of oysters, clams or quahaugs, the fee for which shall be five dollars ($5.00) plus a twenty-five cents (254) recording fee.

History of Section. 

G. L., ch. 232, § 4, as enacted by P. L. 

1956, ch. 3813, § 1. 

20-9-5. Appointment of agents-Rules as to nonresident licenses.- 

The chief of the division of fish and game may appoint such agents. as deemed necessary and may make rules and regulations for the issuance of nonresident shellfish licenses as provided in § 20-9-4. 

History of Section. 

G. L., ch. 232, § 14, as enacted by P. L. 

1956, ch. 3813, § 1. 

20-9-6. Taking of shellfish without license.-No person shall take any oysters, clams or quahaugs, from the free and common fisheries of this state, unless he shall, at the time, have a license from the chief of the division of fish and game permitting him so to do; provided, however, every person a resident or real estate taxpayer of this state, may take in any one (1) day, without a license, not more than one (1) bushel, each of oysters, clams or quahaugs, and said oysters, clams or quahaugs not to be sold or offered for sale.

20-9-7. Penalty for exceeding allowance without license.-Every person who shall take, in any one (1) day, more than one (1) bushel, each, of oysters, clams or quahaugs from the free and common fisheries of this state without a license as aforesaid, shall be fined twenty dollars ($20.00) and costs, or be imprisoned not more than thirty (30) days, or both.

Possession and inspection of license.-Every person holding a license as provided by this chapter shall have the same in his possession at all times while fishing from the free and common fisheries, and he shall present the same for inspection on demand to any person; and any person refusing to so present said license for inspection upon demand shall be fined not less than five dollars ($5.00) nor more than twenty dollars ($20.00) for each offense.

P. L. 1912, ch. 852, § 5; G. L. 1923, ch.

1956, ch. 3813, § 1. 

20-9-9. Numbering of licensed boats.-The chief of the division of fish and game shall provide a number for each boat licensed by him and the same shall be displayed upon all boats licensed, in such manner as the said chief may direct.

History of Section. 

P. L. 1912, ch. 852, § 7; G. L. 1923, ch.

20-9-10. Use of dredges.-No licensed person shall cast, haul or have overboard any dredge, while fishing for oysters, clams or quahaugs from the free and common fisheries, nor shall any licensed boat be used for fishing for oysters, clams or quahaugs with dredges, except as hereinafter provided; provided, however, that any person. having a license for the taking of scallops, as provided by chapter 13 of this title, may use a dredge or dredges, not exceeding six (6) in number nor exceeding twenty-eight (28) inches each in width, for such purpose; and provided, further, that said licensee shall immediately return all oysters or quahaugs caught by him to the waters from which they were taken and that any licensed boat may be used in dredging for mussels by the licensee of said boat, said licensee having first obtained a permit from the chief of the division of fish and game allowing him so to do, and that the said licensee, while dredging for mussels under the permit granted him by said chief, shall immediately return all oysters, scallops or quahaugs caught by him to the waters from which they were taken, and that the fact of any such licensed person being found with oysters or quahaugs in his passession while dredging for mussels shall be prima facie evidence that such person was fishing in violation of the provisions of this chapter and shall be subject to the penalties and fines imposed thereby; and further provided, that any resident dredging for sea clams, or skimmers, so-called, shall not be in violation of this section if such dredging is done southerly of the coastline of Little Compton, southerly of a line extending from Church point, in the town of 45

Little Compton, to Flint point on Aquidneck island, southerly of the coastline of Aquidneck island, southerly of a line extending from Castle Hill point, on Aquidneck island, to Southwest point on Conanicut island, southerly of the coastline of Conanicut island, southerly of a line extending from Beavertail point, of Conanicut island to Bonnet point, Narragansett, easterly of the coastline of the town of Narragansett, southerly of the coastline of the towns of Narragansett, South Kingstown, Charlestown, and Westerly to the Connecticut line, For the purpose of this section coastline refers to the land facing the open sea.

P. L. 1912, ch. 852, § 8; G. L. 1923, ch.

20-9-11. Use of boat at night prohibited.-No person shall use any boat for the purpose of taking or catching any oysters, quahaugs or scallops from any of the free and common fisheries of this state, except between sunrise and sunset on each day.

History of Section. 

P. L. 1912, ch. 852, § 9; G. L. 1923, ch. 

229, § 9; G. L. 1938, ch. 232, § 9. 

20-9-12. Number and size of fish taken by licensees.-No licensed person shall take more than twenty (20) bushels of oysters, clams or quahaugs, including shells in either case, during any one (1) day from the free and common fisheries, and any licensed person taking more than twenty (20) bushels of oysters, clams or quahaugs, during any one (1) day from the free and common fisheries, shall be fined twenty dollars ($20.00) for every bushel so taken over and above said twenty (20) bushels, or be imprisoned not more than thirty (30) days, or both; provided, however, that from the waters of Charlestown pond in the town of Charlestown no licensed person may take more than five (5) bushels of oysters in any one (1) day and any boat used in the taking of oysters from said Charlestown pond shall not be used by more than one (1) licensed person in any one (1) day; and further provided, that no person shall take any oysters from Charlestown (Ninigret) pond in the town of Charlestown and Green Hill pond in the town of South Kingstown, such as are commonly called seed oysters, for the purpose of transplanting same to other waters regardless of whether such oysters are single or attached to shells, stones or other material; except such seed oysters as are attached to the shells of oysters which are being taken for immediate consumption and which cannot be detached without injury or destruction. 46

am ar For the purpose of this chapter, a seed oyster shall be any oyster that is less than two and one quarter (24) inches in its longest dimension. 

P. L. 1912, ch. 852, § 10; G. L. 1923, ch. 229, § 10; G. L. 1938, ch. 232, § 10; 

P. L. 1944, ch. 1416, § 1; P. L. 1947, ch. 

1938, § 1; P. L. 1953, ch. 3134, § 1. 

20-9-13. Transplantation of seed oysters.-The chief of the division of fish and game in order to preserve and insure the future growth of oysters in Charlestown (Ninigret) pond in the town of Charlestown and Green Hill pond in the town of South Kingstown shall be responsible for transplanting seed oysters to more favorable locations; provided, however, that not more than twenty-five per cent (25%) of seed oysters to be transplanted shall be taken to other waters by the state.

History of Section. 

G. L., ch. 232, § 10, as enacted by P. L.

20-9-14. Duration of revocation or cancelation of license.-Any person, whose license has been revoked or cancelled, shall not receive another license to fish for oysters, clams or quahaugs upon the public fisheries of the state, or to use a boat therefor, for a period not to exceed one (1) year from the date of such revocation or cancelation. 

History of Section.

20-9-15. Disposition of moneys received under chapter. All moneys derived from payments received for fishing licenses in accordance with the provisions of this chapter shall be deposited with the general treasurer and are hereby appropriated to said department for the protection, cultivation, research and management of shellfish; and the state controller is hereby authorized and directed to draw his orders upon the general treasurer for the payment of such sum or sums as may be necessary from time to time upon receipt by him of duly authenticated vouchers presented by the chief of the division of fish and game; provided, however, nothing in this section shall be construed to deprive the issuing agents of their recording fees.

20-9-16. Enforcement of chapter.-The conservation officers, ap pointed under § 20-1-10, are hereby required to enforce the provisions.

47

of this chapter. Each of said conservation officers appointed as aforesaid shall be by virtue of his office a special constable and as such conservation officer may, without warrant, arrest any person found violating any of the provisions of this chapter and detain him for prosecution not exceeding forty-eight (48) hours. The chief of the division of fish and game and his duly appointed conservation officers may without warrant go upon any boat or vessel that they believe is used in the illegal taking or transporting of oysters, clams or quahaugs and may seize and remove such oysters, clams or quahaugs held or offered for sale in violation of the provisions of this chapter.

20-9-17. Methods of taking blue or sand crabs-Nonresidents. 

No person shall take or attempt to take any blue or sand crabs from any of the waters in this state except by a scoop, or crab net, trot or hand line. No nonresident of this state shall take or attempt to take at any time any blue or sand crabs from the waters of this state.

20-9-18. Designation of waters for taking of blue and sand crabs. -The chief of the division of fish and game may designate waters in any creek, cove, inlet, river or bay from which such crabs shall not be taken.

History of Section. 

G. L., ch. 229, § 19, as enacted by P. L. 

1931, ch. 1722, § 1; G. L. 1938, ch. 232, $19. 

20-9-19. Seasons and hours for taking of blue crabs.-No person shall take any blue crabs from the waters of this state between the first day of December in any year and the thirtieth day of April in the following year, both dates inclusive, and no person shall take any blue crabs from the waters of this state at any time between the hours of sunset and sunrise.

History of Section. 

G. L., ch. 229, § 20, as enacted by P. L. 

1931, ch. 1722, § 1; G. L. 1938, ch. 232, § 20. 

20-9-20. Egg-bearing blue crabs-Minimum size. No person shall take, offer for sale or possess at any time any female blue crab bearing eggs visible thereon, or from which the egg pouch or bunion shall have been removed. No person shall take, buy or sell or give away or expose for sale or possess any blue crab measuring less than 48

for four and one-eighth (4%) inches across the shell from tip to tip of spike; provided, however, that shedder and soft shell crabs, socalled, may be taken, bought, sold, given away or exposed for sale.

20-9-21. Violations as to blue and sand crabs.-Any person violating any of the provisions of $ 20-9-17, 20-9-19 and 20-9-20 shall be fined twenty dollars ($20.00) and costs for each offense, and any person convicted a second time of a violation of any of the provisions of $$ 20-9-17, 20-9-19 and 20-9-20, shall, in addition to the penalties before mentioned, be deprived of the privilege of fishing for blue or sand crabs within the state for three (3) years thereafter, under a penalty of thirty (30) days' imprisonment for each offense.

20-9-22. Penalty for taking shellfish at night.-Notwithstanding the provisions of any general or public law to the contrary any person who shall take any oysters, clams, quahaugs, scallops, mussels or blue crabs from any of the waters of this state during the hours between sundown and sunrise, shall, upon conviction, be punished by a fine of not more than one thousand dollars ($1,000) or by imprisonment for not more than three (3) years, or by both such fine and imprisonment.

20-9-23. General penalty clause.-Any person violating any of the provisions of this chapter shall be fined twenty dollars ($20.00) and costs for each offense unless a specific penalty is provided herein for such violation.

Deprivation of privileges on second conviction.-Any person convicted a second time of a violation of any of the provisions of this chapter shall, in addition to the penalties before mentioned, be deprived of the privilege of fishing for shellfish within the state for three (3) years thereafter under a penalty of thirty (30) days imprisonment for each offense. 49

20-10-1. Leases authorized—General terms.-The chief of the di vision of fish and game may, unless otherwise by statute prohibited, agree to lease in the name of the state, by public auction or otherwise, to any suitable person, being an inhabitant of this state, or to any corporation chartered under the laws of this state, any piece of land within the state, covered by four (4) feet of tidewater at mean low tide, as delineated upon the plats in the office of the said chief, and not within any harbor line, for the purpose of oyster culture and the oyster business, to be used as a private and several oyster fishery for the planting and cultivation of oysters thereon, upon such terms and conditions as he may deem proper, but not for a longer term than ten (10) years nor for a shorter term than five (5) years, nor for a rent of less than five dollars ($5.00) per annum for every acre to be leased where the water is of the depth of less than twelve (12) feet at mean low water, as shown on the plats in the office of the division of fish and game, and not agreeing to lease more than one (1) acre at a time in one (1) lot or parcel to one (1) person, firm or corporation; but in drawing such leases said chief may include in the instrument of lease one or more acres of land so leased by him, and all such leases shall be made and executed free of expense to the lessee; provided, however, that in Little Narragansett bay, and in 

Pawcatuck river, so-called, the said chief may let such land on terms as to time and rentals as may seem to him best,

20-10-3. Deep water leases.-The said chief may let and lease any lands within the state covered by tidewater where the said water is of the depth of at least twelve (12) feet according to the plats in the office of the division of fish and game at the average low water, for the purpose of having the said land used in planting and cultivating oysters in the deep waters of Narragansett bay and tributaries, at an annual rent of not less than two dollars and fifty cents ($2.50) per acre, for a term of not exceeding ten (10) years from such letting.

20-10-4. Place of business of corporations-Exports-Ceasing to carry on business.-Any corporation to which a lease is made pursuant to § 20-10-1 shall have its principal place of business within this state for the opening, shipping and selling of oysters grown on the ground leased to it within this state. Said corporation shall not ship out of this state any oysters in cargo lots direct from any of the oyster grounds leased to it. If at any time hereafter said corporation shall practically cease to carry on actively the business aforesaid, then and thereupon said chief may cancel said lease or leases to said corporation.

20-10-5. Renewal of leases.-Any lessee or holder of oyster ground, on the expiration of any lease thereof which is now or which may hereafter be granted, shall upon application to the chief of the division of fish and game, have the preference in the reletting of said ground for a like term to that granted in the original lease, unless said applicant, at the time for granting said application, shall be in arrear for rent on said original lease of said ground; and said application for such renewal or further lease shall be granted without notice or advertisement of the pendency thereof; provided, however, that no renewal or further lease of said ground shall be granted when the chief of the division of fish and game shall for cause cease to 53

lease said ground for oyster culture. And such letting shall not be subject to any provisions for letting by public auction.

2074, § 1; G. L. 1923, ch. 230, § 8; G. L. 

1938, ch. 233, § 1. 

20-10-6. Interest of chief prohibited.-Said chief shall not at any time be interested in any lease of ground for planting oysters, or in the cultivation or product thereof.

History of Section. 

P. L. 1901, ch. 853, § 7; P. L. 1903, ch.

20-10-7. Soft bottom leases.-Said chief may agree to lease any land or lands within the state, not under lease on February 15, 1939, for the purpose of having the said lands prepared and made suitable for the planting and cultivating of oysters or other shellfish, which is found upon examination by said division to be soft or "mud" bottom, so-called, upon such terms and for such length of time as he may deem best, but in no case shall the lease be for a period exceeding ten (10) years from the date thereof. The rental per acre for such ground leased shall be one dollar ($1.00) per acre per annum during the term of the lease.

Upon the termination of such lease the renewal thereof shall be made in accordance with the provisions of $ 20-10-1, the rental of the ground contained therein shall be no less than ten dollars ($10.00) per acre per annum for every acre leased where the water is less than twelve (12) and more than four (4) feet in depth at mean low water, nor less than five dollars ($5.00) per acre in water, the depth of which is more than twelve (12) feet at mean low water, according to the plats in the office of the said division of fish and game.

History of Section. 

G. L., ch. 203, § 36, as enacted by P. L. 

1917, ch. 1524, § 1; G. L. 1923, ch. 230, § 36; G. L. 1938, ch. 233, § 30. 20-10-8. Adjustment of rent on grounds found to have soft bottom -Reports-Renewal.-Whenever the lessee of any lease granted under the provisions of this chapter shall make application to said division, setting forth that the land or some portion thereof leased by him consists of soft or "mud" bottom, and requesting therein that rental of said land shall be fixed at one dollar ($1.00) per acre per annum, for the unexpired term which said lease has to run, the said 54

chief shall make an examination of said land and if said chief shall find the same to be soft or "mud" bottom, shall fix the rental of that portion of the land leased which is found to be soft or "mud" bottom, at one dollar ($1.00) per acre per annum for the unexpired term which said lease has to run.

The lessee of each and every parcel of land leased as aforesaid shall during the month of November in each and every year during the term of such lease make a return, in writing, to the said chief of the division of fish and game, of the condition of said land, and the amount of work performed thereon. The failure on the part of the lessee to make such return shall be sufficient cause for the chief of the division of fish and game to terminate said lease, without recourse upon the part of the said lessee. 

Upon the termination of such lease, the renewal thereof shall be made in accordance with the provisions of § 20-10-1, the rental of the ground contained therein shall be not less than ten dollars ($10.00) per acre per annum for every acre leased where the water is less than twelve (12) and more than four (4) feet in depth at mean low water, nor less than five dollars ($5.00) per acre in water, the depth of which is more than twelve (12) feet at mean low water, according to the plats in the office of said division of fish and game.

20-10-9. Wrongful claim to oyster grounds.-Any person who shall wrongfully make claim to any public oyster ground, of which he has no lease or title from the state, by erecting bounds or monuments thereon of any description, or otherwise claiming title to such land, shall for the first offense pay a fine of twenty dollars ($20.00) and costs, and for every subsequent offense pay a fine of fifty dollars ($50.00) and costs, one-half thereof to the use of the state and the other half to the complainant.

History of Section. 

P. L. 1901, ch. 853, § 9; G. L. 1909, ch. 

203, 8:10; G. L. 1923, ch. 230, § 10; G. L. 

1938, ch. 233, § 3. 

20-10-10. Survey and platting of lands-Triangulation stations and reference points.-The said chief shall cause the original surveying and platting of all lands for the planting and cultivating of oysters under provisions of this chapter to be done at the expense of the state and without charge to the lessee. 

He shall have power and authority to go upon the shores adjoining Narragansett bay and its tributaries and to establish stations 55

or points known as triangulation stations or reference points. Such stations or points shall be established in such way or manner as the chief may direct, and he shall have full control and charge of the same with authority to elect an engineer, prescribe his duties and to fix the amount of his salary. Such stations shall be the property of the state of Rhode Island and any person who shall injure, deface, destroy, or remove or cause to be removed, injured, defaced or destroyed any mark or marks thereon, shall be fined twenty dollars ($20.00) and costs for each offense; one-half thereof to the use of the complainant and one-half thereof to the use of the state.

§ 4. 

20-10-11. Cancelation or modification of leases.-The said chief may at the request of the lessee, for cause shown, cancel or modify any lease, or he may remit or abate the rent received therein, if it shall be made to appear to the satisfaction of the said chief that it would be equitable so to do; provided, that in all cases where leases are cancelled, the said chief shall give notice of such action within five (5) days by publication twice a week for two (2) successive weeks, in some daily newspaper published in the city of Providence.

20-10-12. Grounds not subject to lease-Special rules in Brightman's or Babcock's pond.-The said chief shall not let any land north of a line extended across Providence river, bearing S 40° 30' W, true meridian, from the copper bolt set in the rock near the end of Kettle Point, to Field's Point, or let any lands west of a line drawn from Warwick Neck Light bearing S 55° 16′ 02″ W, true meridian, to

20-10-13. Notice of applications for leases.-The said chief shall give notice of every application for a lease of land for the planting of oysters by publication twice a week for two (2) successive weeks in some daily newspaper published in the city of Providence, and also once a week for two (2) successive weeks in some newspaper published in the county nearest to which the ground is located, describing the land therein applied for and giving the name and residence of the applicant and the day, hour, and place where the land will be let; which day shall in all cases, where the first hearing upon such an application is to be had, be upon the first or third Friday of the month, and the chief may give such further notice of such application as he may deem to be necessary to inform persons interested of the pendency of such application, and the actual costs of publishing said notices shall be paid by the applicants, History of Section. 

P. L. 1901, ch. 853, § 13; G. L. 1909, ch.

20-10-14. Conduct of hearings-Effect of decision.-Said chief may adjourn such hearing from time to time, and may issue process to compel the attendance of witnesses for either party, and shall give notice to all parties who have appeared before him upon any application of the time and place when his decision will be given; and such decision shall be final, unless appellate proceedings are taken and prosecuted as hereinafter provided, 57

20-10-15. Right of appeal.-Any person aggrieved by the decision ⚫ of the said chief, upon any application for a private or several oyster ground or oyster fishery, may petition the supreme court for a reversal or modification of such decision.

20-10-16. Filing of appeal and bond.-Application for citation in such case shall be made to the clerk of said court within five (5) days from the day such decision shall have been made, and the petitioner shall, at or before the time for filing his petition, file with said clerk a copy of the proceedings before the said chief, and a bond, signed by him or by some one in his behalf, with sufficient surety, in the sum of fifty dollars ($50.00) payable to said clerk for the use of the state, with condition to prosecute such petition to final judgment and to pay such witness fees and the costs of summons incurred by any party opposing such petition as the court shall award, in case the decision of the said chief shall not be reversed.

20-10-17. Hearing and decision on appeal.-Such case shall be heard and tried in the same manner as other cases entered upon the docket of said court, and the judgment of the court, which shall be entered immediately upon the rendition of decision, shall be conclusive upon the question whether said land shall or shall not be leased, and the said chief shall grant or refuse a lease accordingly.

Covenants and 20-10-18. Execution and recording of leases rights of reentry and termination.-Such leases shall be executed by such lessee, as well as by said chief, in two (2) parts, one (1) part thereof to be delivered to such lessee and the other part thereof to be retained by said chief and recorded in a book kept for that purpose, and shall contain proper covenants for the payment of rent and the performance of the conditions and observance of the restrictions 58

therein set forth, with proper clauses reserving to said chief a right to reenter on behalf of the state and to terminate said lease for breach of any of said covenants.

P. L. 1901, ch. 853, § 18; G. L. 1909, ch.

20-10-19. Bounding of leased grounds-Stakes and buoys.-Said chief shall, before granting any such lease, cause the land to be leased as aforesaid, to be surveyed and platted, and shall in all cases cause such land to be marked with proper bounds, stakes or buoys to define the limits thereof, with such marks thereon as he may direct. Such bounds, stakes or buoys, with the marks thereon, shall be renewed or removed whenever the chief shall so direct.

All buoys used in connection with bounding or subdividing shellfish grounds or for any purpose whatsoever in connection with the enjoyment of the rights and privileges granted by the leasing of shellfish grounds, shall be under the supervision and care of the said division.

Said chief may designate the kind and style of stake or buoy which shall be used for the purposes of marking shellfish grounds and make any suitable regulations in reference to the same; provided, however, that driven stakes shall not be used as bounds or other marks on such shellfish grounds, except on the inside or shoreward boundaries of said grounds, and in no case in more than six (6) feet of water at mean low tide.

Said chief is hereby empowered to investigate all complaints brought to his notice and, in his discretion, to remove or cause to be removed any stake or buoy located on any leased shellfish ground and used for the purpose of bounding, subdividing or otherwise marking said ground, which in his judgment, is in an improper position or condition or does not conform to any regulation which he may make.

Also, said chief may, in his discretion, remove or cause to be removed all such stakes or buoys which may be on shellfish ground where the lease of the same has terminated. 

Also, said chief may remove or cause to be removed any stake or buoy used for the purpose of marking or bounding shellfish beds which may be improperly located on land not leased. 

In case the chief removes or causes to be removed any stake or buoy from leased ground, the cost of removal shall be collected from the lessee. Such cost shall become a charge against said lessee and sub- 59

ject to collection in the same manner as is the yearly rent under the lease for said ground.

Any person wilfully violating the requirements, orders or regulations respecting bounds, stakes or buoys as determined by said chief shall, for the first offense, be fined not more than twenty dollars ($20.00), and for each subsequent offense shall be fined not more than one hundred dollars ($100).

P. L. 1901, ch. 853, § 19; G. L. 1909, ch. 

203, § 20; P. L. 1911, ch. 703, § 1; G. L. 

1923, ch. 230, § 20; G. L. 1938, ch. 233, $14. 

20-10-20. Surveying-Setting of stakes and buoys.-The drawing and executing of such leases, the original surveying and platting, shall be done by said chief without expense to the lessees. The setting up of the bounds, stakes, or buoys shall in all cases be done by the lessee under the direction of the chief.

20-10-21. Injury to or misuse of markers.-Every person who shall wilfully injure, deface, destroy or remove such marks or bounds, or deface any mark thereon, or shall tie or fasten any boat or vessel to any such stake or buoy, shall be fined twenty dollars ($20.00) for each offense, one-half thereof to the use of the state and one-half thereof to the use of the complainant. Every such person shall, in addition thereto, be liable in an action of the case to pay double damages and costs to the person who shall be injured by having the marks and bounds, stakes, or buoys of their said lots injured, defaced, removed or destroyed as aforesaid.

History of Section. 

P. L. 1901, ch. 853, § 21; G. L. 1909, ch.

20-10-22. Oysters as property of lessee. The oysters planted or growing in any private oyster ground leased as aforesaid shall, during the continuance of the lease, be the personal property of the lessee of such oyster ground.

History of Section. 

P. L. 1901, ch. 853, § 22; G. L. 1909, ch.

20-10-23. Unauthorized use of instruments on leased grounds. 

Every person who shall work a dredge, pair of oyster tongs or rakes, or any other implement for the taking of shellfish of any description, 60

upon any private and several oyster ground or bed without the consent of the lessee or owner thereof, or who shall, while upon or sailing over any such ground or bed, cast, haul, or have overboard any such dredge, tongs, rake, or other implement for the taking of shellfish d of any description, under any pretense or for any purpose whatever, without the consent of such lessee or owner, shall for the first offense be fined not exceeding twenty dollars ($20.00) or be imprisoned not exceeding thirty (30) days, and for every subsequent offense shall be fined not exceeding one hundred dollars ($100) or be imprisoned not exceeding six (6) months.

20-10-24. Termination of lease on breach of terms.-Said chief

De shall from time to time diligently inspect and ascertain whether or not the terms and restrictions of the leases are kept and performed in a just and proper manner, and whether or not the rents are punctually paid; and in case said terms and restrictions are not kept and performed, or said rents are not punctually paid, the said chief shall forthwith enter upon the land so leased and terminate the lease.

20-10-25. Collection of rent-Sale of terminated lease.-Said chief may, in the name of the state, institute any legal proceedings that may be necessary for the collection of such rent. Said chief may take possession of any lot leased, upon which the rent shall not have been paid, and may dispose of said lot with all the oysters thereon at public auction to the highest bidder, first giving notice of the time and place of sale by publishing the same at least once each week for two (2) successive weeks in some newspaper published in the city of Providence, with power to adjourn such sale from time to time, giving like notice of such adjournment; to make and execute to the purchaser at such sale a good and sufficient conveyance of all the right title and interest of said lessee in and to the lot leased, together with the oysters thereon; and to receive the proceeds of such sale, and from said proceeds to retain all sums due and owing the state for rent as aforesaid, together with all expenses incident to such sale, rendering and paying the surplus of said proceeds of sale, if any there be over and above the amounts so to be retained as aforesaid, to said lessee, his heirs, executors, administrators or assigns. 61

20-10-26. Taking of oysters at night.-Every person who shall take oysters from any private and several oyster bed, except between the hours of sunrise and sunset, shall be fined twenty dollars ($20.00) for each offense, one-half thereof to the use of the state and one-half thereof to the use of the complainant; and every boat or vessel used or in any way employed in so doing shall, together with its tackle, apparel, furniture and implements on board, be forfeited. 

History of Section. 

P. L. 1901, ch. 853, § 26; G. L. 1909, ch.

20-10-27. Unlawful taking of oysters from private bed.-Every person who shall wrongfully take and carry away oysters from a private oyster bed shall for the first offense be fined fifty dollars ($50.00) and be imprisoned for thirty (30) days, and for every subsequent offense shall be fined one hundred dollars ($100) and be imprisoned for six (6) months.

History of Section. 

P. L. 1901, ch. 853, § 27; G. L. 1909, ch. 

203, § 28; G. L. 1923, ch. 230, § 28; G. L. 

1938, ch. 233, § 22. 

20-10-28. Penalty for injuring private beds.-Every person who shall wilfully break up, damage or injure any bed of oysters, or any tract of land leased from the state for an oyster bed, by depositing thereon earth or stones, or dredgings or scoopings from the river or docks, or in any other manner, shall be fined not exceeding five hundred dollars ($500), one-half thereof to the use of the state and one-half thereof to the use of the complainant; and shall forfeit his boat or vessel, with her tackle, apparel and furniture, and all the implements by him used in injuring such oyster bed. 

History of Section. 

P. L. 1901, ch. 853, § 29; G. L. 1909, ch. 

203, § 30; G. L. 1923, ch. 230, § 30; G. L. 

1938, ch. 233, § 24. 

20-10-29. Deprivation of fishing privileges for second violation.—

Every person convicted a second time of a violation of any of the provisions of this chapter shall, in addition to the penalties hereinbefore mentioned, be deprived of the privilege of fishing for oysters in the waters of the state for the space of three (3) years thereafter, under, penalty of thirty (30) days imprisonment for each offense. 62

20-10-30. Maximum take in Trustan pond.-Every person who shall take more than two (2) bushels of oysters during any one day from Trustan pond, in South Kingstown, shall be fined not less than five dollars ($5.00) nor more than twenty-five dollars ($25.00) for every bushel so taken above two (2) bushels.

20-10-31. Power of constables to arrest without warrant.-Any police constable may in view of the commission of any offense against the provisions of this chapter upon any of the public waters of the state arrest the offender without warrant and detain him for prosecution not exceeding twenty-four (24) hours.

20-10-32. Arrest, seizure, and prosecution of violations.-The chief of said division and his deputies and assistants shall be, by virtue of their respective offices, special constables, and, as such, may arrest any person found violating any of the provisions of this chapter, and may seize any boat or. vessel, with her tackle, apparel and furniture, and all implements belonging thereto, when employed in taking oysters or in injuring any oyster bed in violation of the provisions of this chapter, and shall make complaint when called upon to do so for all such violations, and in any such complaint they shall not be required at the time of complaint or thereafter to enter into recognizance or in any way to become liable for the costs that may accrue thereon; and the attorney-general shall, when notified to do so by the complainant, prosecute all such complaints in the court where the same shall be made or be pending; and all cases of appeal thereof from the sentence of a district court, and all questions arising under the same, or under any complaint and warrant made under the provisions of this chapter, shall be conducted by said attorney-general.

201. Taking of undersize quahaugs.-Any person who takes any quahaugs less than one and one-half (12) inches in diameter, from any of the shores or waters of this state, shall be fined five dollars ($5.00) for each and every quart; provided, however, that such penalty shall not attach if said person promptly returns the quahaugs unmutilated to the same immediate water or shore area from which the quahaugs were first taken.

§ 1; P. L. 1951, ch. 2719, § 1. 

20-11-2. Possession of undersize quahaugs.-Every person, not affected by the provisions of § 20-11-1, who has in his or her custody or possession any quahaugs less than one and one-half (12) inches in diameter, taking the smallest diameter, shall be fined five dollars ($5.00) for each and every quart.

20-11-3. Use of dredges, rakes, and power apparatus restricted to licensees.-No person shall take any quahaugs or clams from the waters of this state by dredges, rakes or other apparatus operated by mechanical power or hauled by power boats; provided, however, that persons duly licensed may take quahaugs as hereinafter provided.
