20-11-4. License for dredging of quahaugs.-A citizen of this state who has resided within the state for at least two (2) years next preceding the date of application, may make application to the division of fish and game for a license, the fee for which shall be ten dollars ($10.00), to take quahaugs by dredges hauled by power boat. The chief of the division of fish and game may upon application of any person entitled to apply for a license under this chapter upon payment of the license fee herein prescribed, register this 66

met T: person and take affidavit, above signature, as to name, age, occupation, place of residence and term of residence within the state and may issue to such person a license to take quahaugs by dredges hauled by power boat. License shall expire June 30 annually. Every person so licensed shall keep his license with him at all times when engaged in the taking of quahaugs and in going to or returning from the fishing grounds and, on demand, shall show it to any conservation officer. 

G. L., ch. 232, § 3, as enacted by P. L. 

1927, ch. 989, § 1; P. L. 1935, ch. 2215, § 1; G. L. 1938, ch. 236, § 3; P. L. 1947, ch. 1936, § 1; G. L., ch. 236, § 4, as enacted by P. L. 1956, ch. 3818, § 2. 

20-11-5. Registration of boats used in quahaug dredging.-Any boat used in the taking of quahaugs by dredges must be registered with the chief of the division of fish and game who shall record the ownership, type of boat, number and name of same, length and motive power. Upon the payment of a fee of twenty-five dollars ($25.00) he may issue a certificate of registration for the boat provided it is owned by a resident of this state and shall assign a number to be of size and design prescribed by the director of agriculture and conservation. The registration shall be kept on the boat at all times and the number shall be displayed on a flat surface in a conspicuous place upon the port side while said boat is engaged in taking quahaugs. License shall expire June 30 annually.

20-11-7. Maximum daily take.-A boat registered as prescribed in this chapter may take and possess between sunrise and sunset of any one (1) day thirty (30) bushels of quahaugs. The person in charge of any boat which takes or possesses more than thirty (30) bushels of quahaugs during any one (1) day shall be fined twenty dollars ($20.00) for every bushel so taken or possessed over and above said thirty (30) bushels, or be imprisoned for thirty (30) days, or both. 67

20-11-8. Opening of areas for quahaug dredging-Areas permanently closed.-Pursuant to good conservation practices, and after a hearing called for that purpose, the director of agriculture and conservation shall be authorized to open between December 1 and March 31 inclusive of any year, areas of the public waters for taking quahaugs under the license issued pursuant to this chapter and shall be authorized to close at any time any area in danger of depletion of quahaugs or when flagrant violations of this chapter occur; provided, however, that no area shall be open in the Sakonnet river from a line running two thousand (2,000) feet southerly from the northernmost point of East Power street dock in the town of Portsmouth, county of Newport, northeasterly to the westerly end of the Stone bridge, and from the easterly end of said Stone bridge in the town of Tiverton, southerly following the shore line across the mouth of Nannaquaket pond, to Fishing rock, in Tiverton, to buoy No. 10 thence to Sapowet point in Tiverton, southerly in a straight line to Corey wharf in Tiverton, nor north of a line commencing at the dock at Homestead on the eastern shore of Prudence island, running northeasterly to nun-buoy N-18 lying southwest of 

Hog island, thence easterly to the Hog island shoal light, thence again northerly to the north abutment of Mount Hope bridge at 

Bristol point and following the shore line of Bristol to Mount Hope point thence easterly to the Rhode Island-Massachusetts state line, nor in that area between the towns of Tiverton and Portsmouth lying south of the railroad bridge and north of the Stone bridge, nor south of a line running from the Navy dock at the south end of Prudence island to the dock at North Chimney, so-called in the town of Middletown.

20-11-9. Carrying of power apparatus during closed season.-No person shall have on board any boat commonly used by fishermen, any dredge, rake or other apparatus operated by mechanical process, or hauled by power boats, which dredge, rake or other apparatus shall be capable of taking quahaugs during the period when such taking shall not be permitted pursuant to this chapter. 68

20-11-10. Disciplinary suspension of boat registration.-Upon vi olation of any provision of this chapter the director of agriculture and conservation may suspend the registration of any boat so regis- E tered for a period not less than fifteen (15) days nor more than thirty (30) days for the first offense and for a period of not less than thirty (30) days nor more than sixty (60) days for the second or any succeeding offense.

1956, ch. 3818, § 3. 

20-11-11. Suspension of license.-Upon violation of any provision of this chapter the director of agriculture and conservation may suspend the license of any person so licensed for a period not less than fifteen (15) days nor more than thirty (30) days for the first offense and for a period of not less than thirty (30) days nor more than sixty (60) days for the second offense, and said director may suspend the license of said person for a period not to exceed one (1) year for a third or any succeeding offense; provided, however, that once having issued any such license to any person, said director shall not refuse to renew the same except for cause shown.

20-11-12. Penalties for violations-Impoundment of boats.-Any person who shall without a license take quahaugs in the manner described in this chapter, or any person who shall violate any suspension of the director of agriculture and conservation made under the authority of this chapter, or who shall violate any provision of this chapter, for which a penalty is not otherwise provided, shall upon conviction for the first offense be fined two hundred fifty dollars ($250) and said director may seize, hold and impound in one (1) of three (3) commercial shipyards submitting the lowest bid, for a period of not less than thirty (30) nor more than sixty (60) days, any power boat used in any such violation of this section. together with its dredges, rakes and equipment. Any person convicted of a subsequent violation or violations of the provisions of this section shall be imprisoned for thirty (30) days and the director of agriculture and conservation may seize, hold and impound, in a commercial shipyard for a period of not less than ninety (90) nor more than one hundred and twenty (120) days, any power boat 69

used in any such violation of this chapter together with its dredges, rakes and equipment.

20-11-13. Costs of seizure and impounding.-Any costs incurred by the seizure and impounding of said power boats by authority of § 20-11-12 shall be assessed against the owner or owners of said boats and no boats, so seized and impounded shall be released to any claim; ant unless and until any costs incurred by reason of such seizure and impounding shall have been paid.

20-11-14. Transfer of quahaugs from Providence river.-The director of the department of agriculture and conservation is authorized and directed to permit, after requiring all necessary safeguards in the best practice of other states, the transfer of quahaugs from the polluted waters of the Providence river to approved areas in East Greenwich bay and in the Sakonnet river north of Stone Bridge or in Mount Hope bay as he may select.

20-11-17. Soft-shelled clams-Methods of taking-Minimum size. -No -No person, either as principal, agent or employee, shall dig or take by any method other than by forks, rakes, hoes, or by tongs operated by hand from any of the waters of this state any clam, that marine mollusk of the species Mya arenaria, commonly called the softshelled clam; nor shall any person dig or take by any method whatsoever any such soft-shell clam less than one and one-half (1) 70

inches in longest diameter, to the amount of more than five per cent (5%) of any batch. Violation of the provisions of this section shall be punishable by a fine of not less than ten dollars ($10.00) nor more than fifty dollars ($50.00).

20-11-18. Violation of the provisions of this section shall be punishable by a fine of not less than ten dollars ($10.00) nor more than fifty dollars ($50.00).

201 License required for taking of lobsters-Penalty.-No person, either as principal, agent or servant, shall, at any time, catch or take any lobster from any of the waters in the jurisdiction of this state, or place, set, keep, maintain, supervise, lift, raise or draw in or from any of said waters, or cause to be placed, set, kept, maintained, supervised, lifted, raised or drawn in or from any of said waters, any pot or other contrivance, designed or adapted for the catching or taking of lobsters, unless licensed so to do as hereinafter provided. Every person who shall violate any of the provisions of this section shall be fined twenty dollars ($20.00) or be imprisoned not more than thirty (30) days, or both, for each such offense. 

P. L. 1909, ch. 437, § 1; G. L. 1923, ch. 

235, §7; G. L. 1938, ch. 238, § 1. 

20-12-2. Issuance of license and badge-Expiration-Fee-An nual report. The chief of the division of fish and game may grant or refuse to grant licenses to catch and take lobsters from the waters within the jurisdiction of this state (in the manner, at the times, and subject to the regulations provided in this chapter) to such citizens of this state as have resided in this state for at least one (1) year next preceding the granting of such license as he may think proper. Whenever any such license shall be granted the same shall be granted to expire on the fifteenth day of November next succeeding the granting of the same, unless sooner revoked as hereinafter provided, and each person to whom such license shall be granted shall, for each license, pay to said division the sum of five dollars ($5.00) for the use of the state. The chief of said division, in his annual report to the general assembly, shall state the number of licenses granted, with the names of the persons licensed and the amicunt of money received therefor. Said chief shall issue to each person licensed as aforesaid a certificate stating the name of the person to whom such license has been granted and the date of expiration of such license, and shall also issue to each person so licensed a metal badge in such form and bearing such inscription as said chief shall determine.

History of Section. 

P. L. 1909, ch. 437, § 2; P. L. 1911, ch. 

711, § 1; G. L. 1923, ch. 235, § 8; G. L. 

1938, ch. 238, § 2. 

20-12-3. Use of license by agent or employee.-If any person to whom such license shall be granted shall be incapacitated for any reason from using said license, said person may permit his agent or employee, if a citizen of the United States, to perform such duties 73

under, the license, as may be necessary during the period of his incapacity; provided, that said agent or employee shall when performing said duties be required to wear the license badge of the holder of said license.

P. L. 1909, ch. 437, § 2, as enacted by 

F. L. 1911, ch. 711, § 1; G. L. 1923, ch. 

235, § 8; G. L. 1938, ch. 238, § 2. 

20-12-4. Revocation of license.-If any person licensed as aforesaid shall, at any time, be adjudged guilty of any violation of any of the provisions of this chapter, after full hearing by said chief, the said chief shall revoke the license issued to such person, and such person shall thereupon cease to have any authority thereunder. 

History of Section. 

P. L. 1909, ch. 437, § 2; P. L. 1911, ch. 

711, §1; G. L. 1923, ch. 235, § 8; G. L. 

1938, ch. 238, § 2. 

20-12-5. Wearing and exhibition of badge and certificate.-Each person, licensed under the provisions of this chapter, shall, at all times, while engaged in the pursuit so licensed, wear upon his person the badge issued to him as provided in § 20-12-2, and shall, upon demand of the chief of said division, or any of his deputies, exhibit said badge and the certificate issued to him as provided in § 20-12-2. 

Every person violating any of the provisions of this section shall, for each offense, be fined five dollars ($5.00).

History of Section. 

P. L. 1909, ch. 437, § 3; G. L. 1923, ch. 

235, § 9; G. L. 1938, ch. 238, § 3. 

20-12-6. Reports of lobsters taken.-For the purpose of determining whether the number of lobsters caught in the waters of this state are increasing or decreasing during any period, every person licensed to catch and take lobsters as aforesaid shall, upon request of the chief of the division of fish and game, report upon blanks, furnished by the said division, a record of the number and pounds of lobsters taken by such person from the waters of this state during the period for which such record is requested; provided, however, such returns from any person shall not be made public, shall be kept only in the files of said division and used only for statistical purposes.

74

207. Minimum size of lobsters taken-Egg-bearing females.—

No person shall catch or take from any of the waters within the jurisdiction of this state, or have in his possession within this state any lobster, cooked or uncooked, which is less than four and oneeighth (4%) inches in length, measured from the forward end of the bone projecting from the head to the rear end of the body shell, or any lobster, alive or dead, which measures less than three and onesixteenth (3 1/16) inches, measured from the rear of the eye socket along a line parallel to the center line of the body shell to the rear end of the body shell. No person shall have in his possession within this state any female lobster bearing eggs, or from which the eggs have been brushed or removed. Every person violating any of the provisions of this section shall be fined five dollars ($5.00) for each such lobster; except that any person licensed under this chapter catching and taking any such lobster and immediately returning the same alive to the water from which it was taken shall not be subject to such fine. The possession of any such lobster, cooked or uncooked, shall be prima facie evidence that the same was caught and taken in violation of this section. 

P. L. 1909, ch. 437, § 5; G. L. 1923, ch. 

235, § 11; P. L. 1936, ch. 2323, § 1; G. L. 

1938, ch. 238, § 6. 

20-12-8. Buoying of pots.-Each and every "lobster pot," socalled, set, kept or maintained, or caused to be set, kept or maintained in any of the waters in the jurisdiction of this state, by any person licensed under this chapter, shall be separately and plainly buoyed; except that in cases where natural conditions render it impracticable to separately buoy each pot the chief of said division may, upon application from any person licensed under this chapter grant permission to otherwise buoy such pots, and each and every permit so granted shall set forth the name of the person to whom the same is granted, the number of pots to be buoyed in a special manner, the manner of buoying the same, the place or places where the same are to be located, and the period of time during which such permit shall extend. Every person violating any of the provisions of this section shall be fined twenty dollars ($20.00), or be imprisoned not more than thirty (30) days for each offense, or both.

History of Section. 

P. L. 1909, ch. 437, § 6; P. L. 1911, ch. 

712, § 1; G. L. 1923, ch. 235, § 14; G. L. 

1938, ch. 238, § 9. 75

209. Marking of buoys, pots, and contrivances.-No person licensed under this chapter shall use any pots for catching, or cars or other contrivance for keeping, lobsters unless the same and the buoys attached thereto are plainly marked with the name or names of the owners thereof, or the person or persons using the same, and the license number or numbers of such person or persons. Every person violating the provisions of this section shall be fined twenty dollars ($20.00) or be imprisoned not more than thirty (30) days, or both, for each such offense, and all pots, cars and other contrivances used contrary to the provisions of this and other sections of this chapter shall be seized by any officer engaged in the enforcement of this chapter, and said property shall be forfeited. 

P. L. 1909, ch. 437, § 7; G. L. 1923, ch. 

235, § 15; G. L. 1938, ch. 238, § 10. 

20-12-10. Unauthorized raising of pots.-No person except the chief of the division of fish and game and his deputies shall lift or raise any pot, belonging to any person licensed under this chapter, set for the catching or taking of lobsters, except with the permission of the owner or owners thereof and licensed so to do under this chapter. Every person violating any of the provisions of this section shall be fined ten dollars ($10.00) for each such offense. 

History of Section. 

P. L. 1909, ch. 437, § 9; G. L. 1923, ch. 

235, § 17; G. L. 1933, ch. 238, § 12. 

20-12-11. Mulation of uncooked lobsters.-No person shall mu tilate any uncooked lobster by severing its tail from its body, or have in his possession any part or parts of any uncooked lobster so mutilated. Every person violating any of the provisions of this section shall be fined five dollars ($5.00) for each such offense, and in any and all prosecutions under this section the possession of any part or parts of any uncooked lobster, so mutilated, shall be prima facie evidence sufficient to convict.

History of Section. 

F. L. 1909, ch. 437, § 10; G. L. 1923, ch. 

235, § 18; G. L. 1938, ch. 238, § 13. 

20-12-12. Deputies for enforcement of chapter-Immunity from costs. The chief of the division of fish and game shall appoint deputies, whose duties shall be the enforcing of the provisions of this chapter relative to the protection of lobsters. Each of said deputies. appointed as aforesaid shall be, by virtue of his office, a special constable, and as such deputy may, without warrant, arrest any person found violating any of the provisions of this chapter, relative to the 76

​ייד -ti protection of lobsters, and detain such person for prosecution not exceeding twenty-four (24) hours. Neither said chief nor said deputies shall be required to enter into recognizance or become liable for ar costs.

P. L. 1909, ch. 437, § 11; G. L. 1923, ch. 

235, § 19; G. L. 1938, ch. 238, § 14. 

20-12-13. Search and seizure.-For the purpose of enforcing the provisions relative to the protection of lobsters, the chief and his appointed deputies may search in suspected places, or upon any boat or vessel that they may believe is used in the catching or transporting of lobsters, and may seize and remove lobsters taken, held, or offered for sale in violation of the provisions of this chapter. 

History of Section. 

P. L. 1909, ch. 437, § 12; G. L. 1923, ch. 

235, § 20; G. L. 1938, ch. 238, § 15. 

20-12-14. Jurisdiction of courts-Appeal. The several district courts shall have concurrent jurisdiction with the superior court over all offenses under this chapter so far as the same relate to the protection of lobsters, and to the full extent of the penalties therein specified; parties defendant, however, having the same right to appeal from the sentences of said district courts as is now provided by law in other criminal cases.

History of Section. 

P. L. 1909, ch. 437, § 14; G. L. 1923, ch. 

235, § 22; G. L. 1938, ch. 238, § 17. 

20-12-15. Negative allegations not required-License as defense. -No negative allegations of any kind need be averred or proved in any prosecution brought under this chapter, but the respondent in any such action may show his license by way of defense.

History of Section. 

P. L. 1909, ch. 437, § 4; G. L. 1923, ch. 

235, § 10; G. L. 1938, ch. 238, § 5. 

20-12-16. Disposition of fines.-Fines incurred under any of the provisions of this chapter shall enure one-half thereof to the use of the complainant and one-half thereof to the use of the state. 

History of Section. 

P. L. 1909, ch. 437, § 13; G. L. 1923, ch. 

235, § 21; G. L. 1938, ch. 238, § 16. 77

201. Taking scallops at night.-Every person who shall take any scallops from any of the waters of the state between the hours of sunset and sunrise shall be fined not more than twenty dollars ($20.00) or imprisoned not more than thirty (30) days, or both, for each such offense.

P. L. 1901, ch. 833, § 1; G. L. 1909, ch. 

209, § 1; G. L. 1923, ch. 237, § 1; G. L. 

1938, ch. 239, § 1; P. L. 1946, ch. 1720, 

§ 1; P. L. 1953, ch. 3131, § 1. 

20-13-2. Taking restricted to residents and taxpayers.-Every person who shall take any scallops from any of the waters of the state unless he shall at the time have been a resident, or real estate taxpayer in the state for one (1) year next preceding, shall be fined not more than twenty dollars ($20.00) or imprisoned not more than thirty (30) days, or both, for each such offense.

History of Section.

20-13-3. Daily take without license-Open season.-Every resident and real estate taxpayer of this state may take without a license. one (1) bushel of scallops per day between the fifteenth day of September at sunrise and the last day of December at sunset next following.

History of Section.

20-13-4. Issuance of license.-Every person must have his home and legal residence in the state for a period of one (1) year, next preceding, before engaging in the taking of more than one (1) bushel of scallops per day, and he shall obtain from the division of fish and game a license, the fee for which shall be fifteen dollars ($15.00). The chief of the division of fish and game shall, upon application of any person entitled to receive a license under this chapter upon payment of the license fees herein prescribed, register this person and take affidavit, above signature, as to name, age, occupation, place of residence and term of residence within the state and shall issue to such a person a license permitting him to take in any 79

one day a maximum of ten (10) bushels of scallops including the shells from the waters of the state. Said division of fish and game shall furnish a copy of the scallop laws with each license issued under this chapter.

20-13-5. Closed season-Scallops stranded on shore.-Every person who shall take any scallops from any of the waters of the state by dredging or by any other means, at any time between sunset the last day in December and sunrise the following September fifteenth, unless otherwise provided, shall be fined not more than twenty dol lars ($20.00) or imprisoned not more than thirty (30) days, or both, for each such offense; provided, however, it shall be lawful for any person at any time to take adult or seed scallops found, carried by storm or tides, from their natural beds and deposited on beaches or exposed flats not covered by water. Such scallops shall be taken by hand for personal or family use and shall not be sold or offered for sale.

20-13-6. Taking and possession of seed scallops.-Every person who shall take any seed scallops from any of the waters of this state by dredging or by any other means, or shall have in his possession any seed scallops and fail to restore them as soon as possible to their natural beds in the water from which taken, shall be fined not more than twenty dollars ($20.00) or be imprisoned not more than thirty (30) days, or both, for each such offense; provided, however, it shall be lawful to sell or have in possession scallops other than adult scallops unavoidably left in a catch after it has been culled, to the amount of not more than five per cent (5%) of the total catch remaining. For the purpose of this chapter a seed scallop shall be a scallop with a bright, thin, slightly curved shell with no foreign ad- 80

di herent, the shell having no well defined raised annual growth line and the scallop being less than one (1) year old.

20-13-7. Sale of scallops during closed season.-Any person who shall offer for sale any scallops between the fifteenth day of January and the fifteenth day of September shall be fined not more than twenty dollars ($20.00) or be imprisoned for thirty (30) days, or both, for each such offense; provided, however, it shall be lawful to have in possession or offer for sale frozen scallops taken legally.

20-13-8. Registration and numbering of boats.-Every person, who obtains a license for the purpose of taking more than one (1) bushel of scallops in any one (1) day from any of the waters of the state, shall register said boat with the chief of the division of fish and game who shall record the ownership, type of boat, number or name of the same, length, motive power, and provide a number for each boat so registered, said number to be black, eight (8) inches in height upon a white surface; said number shall be exposed in a conspicuous place upon the port side of said registered boat while engaged in the taking of scallops and shall not be transferable, and the deputies in said division of fish and game shall have a record of such registered boat in a book kept for such purpose.

20-13-9. Operation of boats-Expiration of license.-Every such registered boat while in use for the taking of scallops shall be pro- 8 pelled by its own power and shall have on board, while thus engaged, the licensed person to whom the boat was registered, and such 81

registered boat, no matter how many persons are on board, shall not be used in any one (1) day for the taking of scallops by other than the licensed person to whom it was registered, nor shall any boat have on board at any time, or be used for taking of in any one day, more than ten (10) bushels of scallops, including the shells. All licenses shall expire the last day of December at midnight. For the purpose of this section this license shall be known as a commercial license.

20-13-10. Towing of dredge during closed season.-Every person who shall tow a scallop dredge in the waters of the state for any purpose between sunset the last day of December and the following 

September fifteenth at sunrise shall be fined not more than twenty dollars ($20.00) or imprisoned not more than thirty (30) days, or both, for each such offense; provided, however, the chief of the division of fish and game may upon request direct his deputies to provide so-called testing service for information of the industry.

20-13-11. Number, size, and hauling of dredges.-No person engaged in taking scallops shall use more than six (6) single dredges, the blades of which shall not be more than twenty-eight (28) inches in width and the bag to be used shall not be more than thirty-six (36) inches in length. Every single dredge shall be towed and hauled aboard the said registered boat individually.

20-13-12. Opening scallops on water-Star fish.-Every person, who shall open any scallops while catching them or throw the shells of opened scallops onto the scallop beds, and every person who shall throw back into the water any star fish, shall be fined not more than twenty dollars ($20.00) or be imprisoned not more than thirty (30) days, or both, for each such offense. 82

20-13-13. Appliances used in south shore ponds.-No person shall use a dredge or other appliance drawn or manipulated by other than hand power for the purpose of taking scallops from the waters of Brightman pond, so-called, in the town of Westerly, nor from Charlestown pond, so-called, in the town of Charlestown, nor from Potter pond, so-called, in the town of South Kingstown, south of a line drawn from the entrance of Potter pond channel to Meadow point, so-called, thence to the north end of Palmer island and continuing in said direction to the west shore of the pond.

20-13-14. General penalty provision.-Every person who shall violate any of the provisions of this chapter unless otherwise provided shall be fined not more than twenty dollars ($20.00) or imprisoned not more than thirty (30) days, or both, for each such ọffense.

20-13-15. Deputies to chief-Enforcement powers-Disposition of fines. The chief of the division of fish and game shall appoint deputies, whose duties shall be the enforcing and carrying out of the provisions of this chapter. Each of said deputies, appointed as aforesaid, shall by virtue of his office be a special constable, and as such deputy may without warrant arrest any person found violating any of the provisions of this chapter and detain him for prosecution not exceeding twenty-four (24) hours. The chief of said division and his duly appointed deputies may search in suspected places, or go upon any boat or vessel that they may believe is used in illegal taking or transporting of scallops, and may seize and remove scallops taken and held, or offered for.sale in violation of the provisions of this chapter. Neither said chief nor said deputies shall be required to enter into recognizance, or become liable for costs. Fines incurred under any of the provisions of this chapter shall inure to the state, 83

20-13-16. Jurisdiction of district courts-Appeals.-The several district courts shall have original jurisdiction over all offenses under this chapter, and to the full extent of the penalties therein specified, parties defendant, however, having the same right to appeal from sentences of said district courts as is now provided by law in other criminal cases.

History of Section. 

P. L. 1901, ch. 833, § 12; C. P. A. 1905, § 1216; G. L. 1909, ch. 209, § 12; G. L.

239, § 15, as enacted by P. L. 1953, ch. 3131, § 1. 

20-13-17. Transplanting of seed scallops.-The chief of the division of fish and game in the fall of each year, in order to preserve and insure the future of the scallop industry, shall be responsible for transplanting large sets of seed scallops from the plats and endangered area in the waters of the state, where they may be exposed at extreme low tides and frozen during severe cold weather, to more favorable locations; provided, however, that not more than twentyfive per cent (25%) of the seed scallops to be transplanted shall be taken to other waters of the state.

SECTION. 

CHAPTER 14 STRIPED BASS SECTION. 

20-14-1. Minimum size of bass taken. 20-14-2. 

Possession or sale of undersize 20-14-3. 20-14-4. bass-Interstate commerce. 

Enforcement of chapter. 

T 20-14-1. Minimum size of bass taken.-No person, firm or corporation shall take in any manner whatsoever from the public waters of this state, including the waters of the Atlantic Ocean within three (3) nautical miles of the coast line, or have in possession, any striped bass measuring less than sixteen (16) inches in length. Striped bass shall be measured from the tip of the snout to the fork of the tail. 

P. L. 1941, ch. 1019, § 1. 

20-14-2. Possession or sale of undersize bass-Interstate commerce. No person, firm or corporation shall take, possess, sell, possess for sale, or offer for sale, any striped bass measuring less than sixteen (16) inches in length, whether caught within the jurisdictional limits of this state or otherwise; provided, however, that this chapter shall not apply to striped bass, packed and in transportation in unbroken packages and coming from any other state or country; provided, such packages shall be clearly marked by stencil, tag or otherwise showing the true origin of the shipment and its destination beyond the limits of the state of Rhode Island. 

History of Section. 

P. L. 1941, ch. 1019, § 2. 

20-14-3. Penalty for violations.-Any person, firm or corporation shall be fined not more than twenty dollars ($20.00) for each striped bass taken, possessed, sold, possessed for sale or offered for sale by him or it in violation of the provisions of this chapter. 

History of Section. 

P. L. 1941, ch. 1019, § 3. 

20-14-4. Enforcement of chapter. The department of agriculture and conservation shall enforce the provisions of this chapter.

History of Section. 

F. L. 1941, ch. 1019, § 4. 85

CHAPTER 15 MENHADEN 

License for fishing in Narragansett bay and inlets. 

20-15-2. License not transferable-Revocation. 

20-15-3.  

20-15-1. License for fishing in Narragansett bay and inlets.—It shall be unlawful for any person, partnership, firm, association, or corporation to catch or take, or to attempt to catch or take, with purse net or seine any fish commonly known as menhaden from the public waters of this state north of a line commencing at Point Judith Light and running approximately in a northeasterly direction to Brenton Reef Lightship, thence continuing to Sakonnet Light; provided, that any legal resident of the state, or any partnership, firm, or association composed entirely of legal residents of this state, or any Rhode Island corporation fifty-one per cent (51%) of whose stock is owned by legal residents of this state, may, upon application to the chief of the division of fish and game, annually obtain a license to take menhaden from the waters of this state from July 1 to October 31 north of the line described; provided, however, that any equipment used by any licensee for the catching of menhaden shall be registered with said chief in accordance with such regulations as he may promulgate; and provided, further, that fifty-one per cent (51%) of the crew manning the boats used by any licensee for the catching of menhaden, shall be legal residents of this state. Said chief shall determine the eligibility of any person, partnership, firm, association, or corporation to take menhaden from the waters of this state under this chapter.

P. L. 1948, ch. 2120, § 1; impl. am. P. 

L. 1954, ch. 3358, § 2. 

20-15-2. License not transferable-Revocation.-Any license is sued under $ 20-15-1 shall not be transferable. If the chief of the division of fish and game shall determine that any licensee has obtained his or its license by fraud or deception, or has violated the provisions of § 20-15-1, said chief shall revoke his or its license.

History of Section.

20-15-3. Penalty for violations.-Any person, partnership, firm, association, or corporation violating the provisions of § 20-15-1, upon conviction thereof, shall be fined not less than five hundred dollars ($500) nor more than seven hundred fifty dollars ($750)

The town council of the town of New Shoreham is hereby authorized to regulate the shell fisheries in Great Salt pond in New Shoreham, by leasing in the name and for the benefit of said town, by public auction or otherwise, in lots of one (1) acre each, to any suitable person being an inhabitant of this state, any of the shore and bottom of the said Great Salt pond, subject to the rights of the riparian owners therein, as a private or several shellfish ground, to be used for the protecting, cultivating, producing, planting, growing, digging and taking of clams, quahaugs, oysters, mussels, scallops or other shellfish therein or thereon, in such manner and upon such terms and conditions as they may deem proper, but not for a longer term than ten (10) years nor for a shorter term than one (1) year. 88

201. Definition of terms.-The term fish traps as used in this chapter shall be taken to include stub and floating traps, weirs, pounds, nets, or any other equivalent contrivance or thing set on, in or anchored to the bottom or shore used for the purpose of catching fish.

The term suitable person as used in this chapter shall be taken to include any inhabitant of the state or any corporation incorporated under the laws of the state. 

The license of a location for a fish trap shall be construed to carry with it the exclusive right of the licensee to operate or maintain a fish trap at the place defined in the license. 

P. L. 1928, ch. 1167, § 14; G. L. 1938, ch. 241, § 14. 

20-18-2. Waters within which license required.-It shall be unlawful for any person, without first obtaining a license as hereinafter provided, to erect or maintain any fish trap of either floating, weir, pound, stub or stake type in those portions of the public waters of the state designated by the engineer's office of the department of the army of the United States as available areas for the purposes of fishing which are included in the following boundaries, viz.: commencing at Point Judith and thence extending in a northeasterly direction to Sakonnet point, thence in a northwesterly direction to Sachuest point, thence following the southerly shore line of the island of Rhode island to Brenton's point, thence westerly in a straight line to Beaver Tail lighthouse, thence in a northwesterly direction to

Bonnet point, thence following the easterly shore line of the town of 

Narragansett to Point Judith, History of Section. 

P. L. 1928, ch. 1167, § 1; G. L. 1938, ph, 241, § 1. 

20-18-3. Persons to whom licenses issuable.-The division of fish and game may issue licenses duly signed and under its seal, to set or erect and maintain fish traps, as provided in this chapter, to any inhabitants of this state, or to any corporations incorporated in the state.

History of Section. 

P. L. 1928, ch. 1167, § 2; G. L. 1938, ch. 241, § 2. 

20-18-4. Application for license.-Any person desiring a license for a location for erecting a fish trap, shall make application in writing to said division of fish and game in such form and giving such information as the chief of said division may require. 

Each application for a location for a fish trap shall fix definitely 91

the position of the desired location with reference to prominent points on shore, and shall also show the dimensions of the trap and of the leader to be set in the desired location, and any other details the chief of said division may require. 

P. L. 1928, ch. 1167, § 3; G. L. 1938, ch. 

241, § 3. 

20-18-5. Distance between traps-Length of trap and leader. 

Except as provided in $ 20-18-6 no license shall be granted for the erection within the areas described in § 20-18-2 of a fish trap any part of which is less than three thousand (3,000) feet distant from an adjoining trap, measured parallel with the general coastline at such point as determined by the division of fish and game, or for a greater length of trap and leader than two hundred eighty (280) fathoms; provided that the division of fish and game may grant a license for the erection of a fish trap within a lesser distance of three thousand (3,000) feet from an adjoining trap if the parties interested agree in writing to. such lesser distance.

History of Section. 

P. L. 1928, ch. 1167, § 4; P. L. 1932, ch. 

1890, § 1; G. L. 1938, ch. 241, § 4. 

20-18-6. Restricted locations off Rhode island and Sakonnet river. -No license shall be granted for the erection of a fish trap within that portion of the public waters of the state southerly of the island of Rhode island, designated by the engineer's office of the department of the army as available fishing areas as aforesaid, lying easterly of Brenton's reef and southerly of a line drawn from Coggeshall's point to Sakonnet light, except as follows: Licenses may be granted for the erection of traps (1) southeasterly from Seal Rock, so-called, in a line on range of White Church in Saunderstown over Seal Rock to the offshore limits of the outer area designated by the engineer's office of the department of the army as aforesaid, provided that all traps on this line shall be of equal length including leaders and shall be so limited in length that permits for eight (8) traps may be granted; (2) southeasterly from Flat Rock, so-called, on the easterly side of Coggeshall's point, in a line to the offshore limits of the inner or "summer" area designated by the engineer's office of the department of the army as aforesaid; (3) in a line from the westerly side of Price's neck, so-called, southwesterly for a distance of not exceeding one thousand five hundred (1,500) feet, provided that not more than two (2) traps shall be licensed in this line; (4) in a line from the easterly side of Price's neck, so-called, south-southeasterly for a distance of not exceeding three thousand three hundred (3,300) 92

feet; (5) southeasterly from Coggeshall's point in a line to the eastern limits of the outer area designated by the engineer's office of the department of the army as aforesaid; (6) southerly from Cormorant 

Rock, so-called, in a line for a distance of not exceeding seven thousand (7,000) feet; (7) for two (2) traps only on the line constituting the offshore limits of the outer area designated by the engineer's office of the department of the army as aforesaid at a point five thousand (5,000) feet east-southeasterly of the southeastern end of the line from Seal Rock described in (1) of this section; (8) for two (2) traps only beginning at a point ten thousand (10,000) feet east-southeasterly of the southeastern end of the line from Seal Rock described in (1) of this section; provided, that the traps and leaders described in (7) and (8) of this section shall extend parallel with the aforesaid Seal Rock line. The provisions of § 20-18-5 shall not apply to licenses for the erection of traps granted under this section with the exception of the provision in said § 20-18-5 that no license shall be granted for a greater length of trap and leader than two hundred eighty (280) fathoms, which provision shall apply to this section. 

P. L. 1928, ch. 1167, § 4, as enacted by 

P. L. 1932, ch. 1890, § 1; P. L. 1932, ch. 

1955, § 1; G. L. 1938, ch. 241, § 4. 

20-18-7. Continuity of traps.-(a) No more than two (2) traps shall be set together in any continuous string or line, and there shall be a space of one hundred fifty (150) fathoms between such two (2) trap sets within which no trap, leader, or other twine shall be placed. (b) No more than three (3) traps shall be set, erected or maintained by the holder of any license issued hereunder in such a manner as to be consecutively arranged in any one (1) of the lines described in § 20-18-6; provided that this paragraph shall not be deemed to limit the total number of traps that may be set, erected or maintained in any such line by any such holder. 

History of Section. 

P. L. 1928, ch. 1167, § 4; P. L. 1932, ch. 

1890, § 1; G. L. 1938, ch. 241, § 4. 

20-18-8. Hook-backs in leaders.-Each leader shall be set as nearly as may be in a straight line and, with the exception of the traps permitted by (7) and (8) of 20-18-6, no' bend or angle in such leader known commonly as a "hook-back" shall be permitted.

History of Section. 

P.. L. 1928, ch. 1167, § 4, as enacted by 

P. L. 1932, ch. 1890, § 1; G. L. 1938, ch. 

241, § 4. 93

209. Preference of prior occupants in licensing.-The chief of the division of fish and game shall, where there are two or more applicants for licenses for the same location or for locations that are less than three thousand (3,000) feet apart, determine which applicant has continuously during the fishing season occupied said location for the longer period of years, and the applicant who shall establish a priority of location shall, if otherwise properly qualified, be entitled to receive a license for such location. 

P. L. 1928, ch. 1167, § 5; G. L. 1938, ch. 

241, § 5. 

20-18-10. Expiration and renewal of licenses.-All licenses issued before the last day of December, A.D., 1958, shall expire on that date and all licenses shall be for a three (3) year period, or, for that portion thereof, expiring on the last day of December of each third year thereafter.

On termination of any license the division may renew the same by issuing a new license in place of such license, provided application for renewal is made within ten (10) days before said termination in manner similar to that required for application for a license in § 20-18-4. Failure to apply for renewal as aforesaid shall operate to leave the location available for an application by any suitable person as defined in § 20-18-1.

History of Section. 

P. L. 1928, ch. 1167, § 6; G. L. 1938, ch. 

241, § 6. 

20-18-11. Exclusive right of licensee-Assignment of license. 

The holder of any license, his heirs, executors, administrators, successors or assigns shall have the exclusive right to fish such location. by means of a fish trap, as is defined in § 20-18-1. 

Any holder of a license may, with the approval of the chief of the division of fish and game, transfer the same to any suitable person. 

Said approval shall be endorsed on said license in order to become effective. 

History of Section. 

P. L. 1928, ch. 1167, § 7; G. L. 1938, ch. 

241, § 7. 

20-18-12. Inspection of traps. The chief of the division of fish and game shall cause an inspection or inspections of the fish traps licensed hereby to be made at a proper time or times in each year for the purpose of determining whether such traps are located in accordance with the license therefor, and whether the licensee is observing the rules and regulations fixed by the said chief. 94

י: 

P. L. 1928, ch. 1167, § 8; G. L. 1938, ch. 

241, § 8. 

20-18-13. Suspension or revocation of license.-If upon inspection it shall appear that any trap is improperly located or is otherwise being used in violation of its rules and regulations, the chief of the division of fish and game shall forthwith cause notice to be given to the holder of any license, and if such facts are established at a hearing thereon, the chief may suspend or revoke any such license. 

History of Section. 

P. L. 1928, ch. 1167, § 8; G. L. 1938, ch. 

241, § 8. 

20-18-14. Report as to traps placed-Failure to occupy location- 

Removal of licensee from state.-On or before the tenth day of January in each year every person holding a license as aforesaid shall notify the division of fish and game, in writing, under oath, what traps were placed under said license during the preceding calendar year. Failure to furnish such information shall make the license null and void after that date. Failure to place a fish trap on a licensed location for a part of one (1) calendar year may, in the discretion of the chief of said division, make the license therefor null and void, and the location will thereupon be available for any suitable applicant. A license for a fish trap location shall become null and void upon the removal of the holder thereof from the state. 

History of Section. 

P. L. 1928, ch. 1167, § 9; P. L. 1932, ch. 

1890, § 2; G. L. 1938, ch. 241, § 9 

20-18-15. Removal of trap on cessation of use-Closed season 

Damaged or dilapidated traps.-Upon ceasing to use any fish trap as hereby authorized, such structure shall at once be removed by the owner thereof at his own expense and to the satisfaction of the chief of the division of fish and game. Failure to remove the same shall be considered sufficient grounds for prosecution of the owner for maintaining a public nuisance. All fish traps herein authorized shall be completely removed by or before the last day of December of each year, and no fish trap shall be reset before the first day of the following March. All submerged or broken stakes must be promptly removed. Any fish trap damaged or allowed to get into a dilapidated condition will be regarded as abandoned unless promptly removed or rebuilt. Failure to do this will subject the owner to prosecution and forfeiture of the trap, as herein provided. 

History of Section. 

P. L. 1928, ch. 1167, § 10; G. L. 1938, ch. 241, § 10. 95

2016. Seizure of abandoned or unlawful traps-Destruction or sale. The said division may take possession of any abandoned fish trap, and appurtenances; or any fish trap and appurtenances placed in a location for which no license has been obtained as herein provided; or any fish trap, and appurtenances for which a licensed location has been obtained, but which the owner thereof may wilfully maintain in a wrong position or location; and may destroy the same or may dispose of it at public auction to the highest bidder, first giving notice of the time and place of sale by publishing the same at least three (3) times each week for two (2) successive weeks in a newspaper of general circulation with power to adjourn such sale from time to time, giving like notice of such adjournment; and make and execute to the purchaser at such sale a good and sufficient conveyance of all right, title and interest in and to said fish trap and appurtenances; and to receive the proceeds of such sale and pay the same into the treasury of the state; and the state controller shall draw his orders upon the general treasurer for the payment of the expenses of taking possession and disposing of any such fish trap as aforesaid, upon receipt by him of proper vouchers, approved by the chief of said division, and by the director of agriculture and conservation, and the general treasurer shall pay said orders out of moneys appropriated therefor. 

P. L. 1928, ch. 1167, § 13; G. L. 1938, ch. 241, § 13; impl. am. P. L. 1939, ch. 

660, § 65. 

20-18-17. Rules and regulations.-The chief of the division of fish and game may fix such rules and regulations in reference to fish traps, licensed hereunder, as may appear to him necessary for the public safety and for the proper execution of this chapter. The chief may modify and change such rules from time to time to properly meet such conditions as may appear.

History of Section. 

P. L. 1928, ch. 1167, § 11; G. L. 1938, ch. 241, § 11. 

20-18-18. Judicial enforcement of chapter and orders.-The su perior court, upon the application of the chief of the division of fish. and game, shall have jurisdiction in equity to enjoin the violation of any of the provisions of this chapter and to enforce the orders of said chief provided for in this chapter.

2019. Wilful injury to traps.-Any person who shall wilfully or maliciously cut, remove, displace, tamper with or in any way damage any trap, leader or pound set by virtue of the license provided for by this chapter shall be imprisoned not exceeding two (2) years or be fined not exceeding one thousand dollars ($1,000) or punished by both such fine and imprisonment. 

P. L. 1928, ch. 1167, § 17, as enacted by 

P. L. 1932, ch. 1890, § 4; P. L. 1932 ch. 

1955, § 2; G. L. 1938, ch. 241, § 16. 

20-18-20. Penalty for violations generally-Prosecution.-Every person or corporation wilfully. violating any of the provisions of this chapter, or any rule or regulation made by the chief of the division of fish and game, as provided in § 20-18-17, shall be punished by a fine not exceeding five hundred dollars ($500) nor less than one hundred dollars ($100), or imprisoned (in the case of a natural per-son) not exceeding one (1) year, or by both such punishments, in the discretion of the court. Proper proceedings for violation of any of the provisions of this chapter shall be instituted by the attorneygeneral.

History of Section. 

P. L. 1928, ch. 1167, § 12; G. L. 1938, gh. 241, § 12. 

20-18-21. Appropriations and disbursements.-The general assembly shall annually appropriate such sums as it may deem sufficient to be expended by the division of fish and game under the provisions of this chapter; and the state controller is hereby authorized and directed to draw his orders upon the general treasurer for so much thereof as may from time to time be required, upon receipt by him of proper vouchers approved by the chief of said division, and by the director of agriculture and conservation.

History of Section. 

P. L. 1928, ch. 1167, § 15; G. L. 1938, ch. 241, § 15; impl. am. P. L. 1939, ch. 

660, § 65. 

20-18-22. Severability of provisions.-Each section of this chapter and each part of each section is hereby declared to be an independent section, and the holding of any section or sections or part or parts thereof to be void, ineffective or unconstitutional for any cause, shall not be deemed to affect any other section or part thereof.

201. Barrington river.-Every person who shall set or draw any seine in any part of the river running from Warren river through the town of Barrington, except that part lying north of the Congregational church building in the said town of Barrington, shall be fined twenty dollars ($20.00).

G. L. 1896, ch. 171, § 1; G. L. 1909, ch.

20-19-27. Supervision by division of fish and game-Interstate cooperation-Annual reports.-The chief of the division of fish and game shall have general supervision of all matters relating to the subjects contained in this chapter, and may make all needful regulations to carry out the provisions of this chapter, and shall from time to time examine all the weirs, traps or other contrivances, with a view of carrying out such regulations as are most beneficial to the people of the state and shall prosecute for the violation of such regulations or for the infringement of the provisions of this chapter. He may co-operate with the fish commissioners or other similar bodies or agencies of other states, and shall make an annual report to the general assembly of his doings, with such facts and suggestions in relation to the object for which he is appointed as he may deem proper. 105

SECTION. 

SECTION. 

CHAPTER 20 ERADICATION OF STAR FISH 

20-20-1. Appropriations for bounties. 

20-20-2. Designation of zones for taking of star fish. 

20-20-3. Permits for dredging. 

20-20-4. Weighing stations-Bounty. Restrictions. 20-20-5. 

20-20-6. Rules and regulations-Payment of expenses and bounties. 

20-20-7.  

20-20-1. Appropriations for bounties.-The general assembly shall annually appropriate such sum as it may deem necessary, out of any money in the treasury not otherwise appropriated, to be expended under the direction of the director of agriculture and conservation, in the administration of the provisions of this chapter, to eradicate star fish from the waters of Rhode Island, and pay a suitable bounty for such star fish as hereinafter provided; and the controller is hereby authorized and directed to draw his orders upon the general treasurer for the payment of such sum, or so much thereof as may be required from time to time, upon the receipt by him of proper vouchers approved by the director of agriculture and conservation.

P. L. 1941, ch. 984, § 1. 

20-20-2. Designation of zones for taking of star fish.-The said director shall designate zones in Rhode Island waters from which star fish may be taken, so strategically located in the opinion of said director as to break up effectively the spawning beds of such star fish, which said zones shall not include leased grounds. 

History of Section. 

P. L. 1941, ch. 984, § 2. 

20-20-3. Permits for dredging. The department of agriculture and conservation shall issue a permit to the owner or operator of each boat which is to be used in dredging star fish, which permits may be revoked by said department for noncompliance with the terms of this chapter, or for violation of any of the statutes of this state in relation to fish and game.

History of Section. 

P. L. 1941, ch. 984, § 3. 

20-20-4. Weighing stations-Bounty-Said department may designate the location for three (3) weighing stations in the state, to any one of which a free fisherman, duly authorized by said permit to dredge for star fish, may bring his catch of star fish to be weighed 107

by the duly authorized representative of said department. Said fisherman shall be paid for his load of star fish a bounty in amount of seventy-five cents (754) per one hundred (100) pounds of star fish for each one hundred (100) pounds of catch; provided, however, said star fish must be landed on the dock of one of the official weighing stations by the fisherman himself; and provided, further, that the bounty per one hundred (100) may be changed as the star fish become less plentiful, but in no case shall the bounty be more than one dollar ($1.00) per one hundred (100) pounds, and the price shall be fixed at the direction of the director of agriculture and conservation. 

P. L. 1941, ch. 984, § 4. 

20-20-5. Restrictions.-The following restrictions in relation to the taking of star fish shall be enforced by the department of agriculture and conservation and shall rigidly apply: (a) No boat shall be used for the taking or dredging of star fish until the owner or operator thereof has complied with the provisions of § 20-20-3 in relation to permits. (b) No bounty shall be paid for star fish taken from leased grounds or for star fish dredged by oyster companies. (c) No boat shall take star fish except between sunrise and sunset of each week day, and no star fish shall be taken on the first day of the week. (d) No otter trawls may be used in taking such star fish, and the only dredges permitted to be used shall receive the approval of the department of agriculture and conservation. 

History of Section. 

P. L. 1941, ch. 984, § 5. 

20-20-6. Rules and regulations-Payment of expenses and bounties. The director of agriculture and conservation may make the necessary rules and regulations to carry out the provisions of this chapter most expeditiously for the purpose of eradicating the star fish from the waters of the state. Said director is hereby authorized and directed to spend whatever sum may be necessary from such appropriation for the administering of this chapter, and the balance of said appropriation shall be paid out in bounties for star fish as provided in this chapter.

History of Section. 

P. L. 1941, ch. 984, § 6. 

20-20-7. -Any person violating any of the provisions of this chapter shall, upon conviction therefor, be subject to a fine of not less than one hundred dollars ($100) and costs. 

History of Section. 

P. L. 1941, ch. 984, § 7.

201. Closed season.-No person shall take any species of fish from any of the waters in the jurisdiction of this state between 

February 21 and the Friday before the third Saturday of April, inclusive, said waters being closed to all fishing. 

G. L., ch. 240, § 10, as enacted by P. L. 

1949, ch. 2230, § 1; P. L. 1954, ch. 3264, $1. 

20-21-2. Closed season on black bass-Minimum length-Maxi. mum daily catch.-Between the twentieth day of February and the twentieth day of June next succeeding no person shall take from any of the waters in the jurisdiction of this state or have in his possession within this state any black bass. No person shall take or have in his possession within this state at any time of the year any black bass less than ten (10) inches in length nor shall any person catch or take from the waters of this state in any one (1) day more than six (6) black bass; provided, however, that, during the open season as provided in this section, such length restriction and bag limit shall not apply to the taking of black bass from the waters of Stafford pond in the town of Tiverton.

20-21-5. Sale of black bass.-It shall be unlawful for any person, firm or corporation to sell or offer to sell, purchase or offer to purchase at any time any largemouth or smallmouth black bass regardless of whether said bass are caught in the waters of the state, or the waters of any other state, district, territory, or country and brought into the state of Rhode Island. Any person violating the provisions of this section shall be fined not exceeding one hundred dollars ($100).

20-21-6. Closed season on trout.-No person shall take any trout from any of the waters in the jurisdiction of this state or have in his possession within this state any trout so taken between the third 

Saturday of October and the third Saturday of April next suc ceeding.

20-21-7. Minimum size of trout-Maximum daily catch.-No person shall take or have in his possession within this state at any time of year any trout less than seven (7) inches in length nor shall any person catch or take from the waters of this state in any one (1) day more than ten (10) trout in number.

20-21-8. -Every person violating any of the provisions of §§ 20-21-6 to 20-21-9, inclusive, shall be fined not 111

more than twenty dollars ($20.00) for each and every trout found in his possession contrary to the provisions of said sections; but any person catching or taking any trout less than the prescribed length from any of the waters of this state and immediately returning the same alive to the waters from which taken shall not be subject to such fine.

20-21-9. Artificially cultivated trout.-Nothing contained in §§ 20-21-6 to 20-21-8, inclusive, shall be so construed as to prohibit the taking and sale of trout artificially cultivated in private ponds at any season of the year; provided, that all persons raising brook trout artificially in private ponds shall use the initials of their names as a brand, which brand shall be put on every box or container of trout shipped or put on the market by them between the fifteenth day of July in each year and the fifteenth day of April next succeeding. All persons raising and disposing of trout as aforesaid shall cause their brand, required herein, to be registered by the chief of the division of fish and game.

20-21-10. Closed season on pickerel-Minimum size-Maximum daily catch.-Every person who shall take any pickerel between the twentieth day of February and the third Saturday in April, and every person who shall take or have in his possession any pickerel less than twelve (12) inches in length shall be fined ten dollars ($10.00) for each and every pickerel taken or found in his possession. No person shall catch or take from the waters of this state in any one (1) day more than ten (10) pickerel; but any person catching or taking any pickerel less than twelve (12) inches in length and immediately returning the same alive to the water shall not be subject to fine.

20-11. Minimum size of perch-Maximum daily catch.-No person shall catch or take from any fresh-water stream or pond in this state or have in his possession within this state any yellow or striped perch or white perch, so taken, less than six (6) inches in. length, nor shall any person catch or take from any fresh-water stream or pond in this state in any one (1) day or have in his possession within this state more than twenty in number of white perch or thirty in number of yellow or striped perch so taken. Any person violating any of the provisions of this section shall be fined ten dollars ($10.00) for each offense, but any person catching or taking from any of the waters of this state any such fish less than the prescribed length or more than the prescribed number and immediately returning the same alive to the waters from which taken shall not be subject to such fine.

20-21-12. Sale of trout, bass, pickerel, or perch.-No person shall sell or offer for sale within this state any trout, black bass, pickerel or yellow perch taken from any fresh-water streams or ponds in the jurisdiction of this state excepting such trout, black bass, pickerel or yellow perch as are artificially cultivated in private ponds as authorized by law. Any person violating the provisions of this section shall be fined twenty dollars ($20.00) for each offense.

20-21-13. Stocking of streams and ponds.-No person shall stock any fresh-water stream or pond within this state with any species of fish or place or liberate any fish in any such fresh-water stream or pond, unless the same is privately owned, without first securing permission from the division of fish and game. Any person who shall violate any of the provisions of this section shall be fined not exceeding twenty dollars ($20:00) for each offense.

20-21-15. Fishing in artificially stocked waters.-No person shall place, operate or superintend any device intended for the purpose of taking or catching fish in any pond or stream stocked with fish at the expense of the state within three (3) years after such stream or pond has been stocked and a copy of the regulations of the chief of the division of fish and game, for the protection thereof, has been filed in the office of the town clerk in which such stream or pond is situated and advertised as provided in § 20-4-6, or, at any time, in any private pond, brook, stream, preserve, or any other place made, constricted or used for the purpose of breeding or growing fish therein, without the consent of the proprietor or lessee thereof so to do.

20-21-16. Hand devices to be used-Ice fishing.-No person shall place, operate or superintend any device intended for the purpose of taking or catching fish, excepting single lines, with not more than two (2) hooks upon each, held in and operated by hand or upon poles or rods designed to be held in the hands, in any stream or fresh-water pond within this state, except such streams and ponds as are wholly upon his own land; provided, that for the purpose of fishing through ice upon the surface of any stream or fresh-water pond, other than private streams and ponds, and streams and ponds stocked by the state, within three (3) years after the same are stocked and notice given as aforesaid, any person may place, operate, or superintend five (5) lines with a single hook upon each and held by any device designed for such purpose; provided, however, that every such line for the purpose of fishing through the ice shall be attended by a person licensed to catch fish who shall have such license in his or her possession at all times when so attending such lines.

20-21-20. Ice fishing at night.-During the period of time from one (1) hour after sunset until one (1) hour before sunrise next succeeding, no person shall place, operate, or superintend any device for the purpose of catching or taking fish through the ice upon the surface of any fresh-water pond or stream within this state, nor shall any person at any time use any such device for said purpose unless the same shall be personally attended. Every person violating any of the provisions of this section shall, for each offense, be fined not more than twenty dollars ($20.00), or be imprisoned not more than thirty (30) days or both.

20-21-21. Disposition of fines and forfeitures.-One-half of the fines and forfeitures recovered for violation of the provisions of this chapter shall accrue to the complainant and one-half thereof to the use of the state.

CHAPTER 22 MINNOWS 

SECTION. 

SECTION. 

20-22-1. 20-22-2. 

Definition of terms.

20-22-3. Issuance of license-Fee-Ex- 

20-22-4. piration. 

Authorized seines and nets.

20-22-6. Regulations as to taking of minnows. 

20-22-7. Penalties for violations. 

20-22-8. Enforcement of chapter. 

20-22-1. Definition of terms.-The term "person" as used in this chapter shall be construed to mean and include any person, firm or corporation, and each employee of such person, firm or corporation. 

The term "fresh-water minnow" as used in this chapter shall be construed to mean and include all minnows and the young of all species of fresh-water fish, which are not protected by law. 

P. L. 1954, ch. 3356, § 2. 

20-22-2. License required for minnow business-Possession without license. It shall be unlawful for any person to possess, in this state, at any one time, more than one hundred (100) live fresh-water minnows without first procuring a license therefor issued by the division of fish and game of the state department of agriculture and conservation. It shall be unlawful for any person to sell or barter or engage in the business of taking or catching for the purpose of selling or bartering fresh-water live minnows for bait without first procuring a license therefor, from the division of fish and game. 

History of Section. 

P. L. 1954, ch. 3356, § 1. 

20-22-3. Issuance of license-Fee-Expiration.-Any person who desires to possess more than one hundred (100) fresh-water minnows at any one (1) time, or to engage in or to continue to engage in the business of taking, catching, selling or bartering live freshwater minnows for bait shall file an application with the division of fish and game for a license to do so. Upon receipt of the application, the division of fish and game shall have power to either issue or refuse a license to the applicant; except that when said applicant is engaged in hatching and raising his own stock such permit shall be issued. Such application and license shall be on such forms as shall be prescribed by the division of fish and game, 116

and said division is hereby empowered to charge an annual fee for such a license in the sum of fifteen dollars ($15.00) and to designate the waters from which fresh-water minnows can be taken. Licenses shall expire December 31 of each year.

P. L. 1954, ch. 3356, § 3. 

20-22-4. Authorized seines and nets.-Fresh-water minnows, not including the young of any species of game fish, may be lawfully taken for bait with minnow traps, dip nets and seines of the dimensions not more than eight (8) feet in length and four (4) feet in depth and having a mesh not larger than one-quarter of an inch in any waters of this state. Possession and use of minnow seines larger than the dimensions specified in this section, unless otherwise provided, shall be unlawful.

History of Section. 

P. L. 1954, ch. 3356, § 4. 

20-22-5. Larger seines used by licensees.-Minnow seines of greater dimensions than authorized in § 20-22-4 may be lawfully pos sessed and used by a person licensed under this chapter, in the waters specified by the chief of the division of fish and game; provided, that no minnow seines so licensed shall exceed fifty (50) feet in length and eight (8) feet in depth and having a mesh not larger than one-quarter of an inch; and provided further, however, that upon payment of an additional ten dollars ($10.00) for each twenty-five (25) feet, a seine over fifty (50) feet in length and up to one hundred (100) feet can be used.

History of Section. 

P. L. 1954, ch. 3356, § 5. 

20-22-6. Regulations as to taking of minnows.-The chief of the division of fish and game is hereby empowered to make such regulations as to the method of taking, time of taking, numbers to be taken, and season to take fresh-water minnows in this state, History of Section. 

P. L. 1954, ch. 3356, § 6. 

20-22-7. Penalties for violations.-Any person violating any provision of this chapter shall be deemed guilty of a misdemeanor and upon conviction shall be punished, for the first offense, by imprisonment for not more than thirty (30) days or by a fine of not less than ten dollars ($10.00) or more than fifty dollars ($50.00), or both such fine and imprisonment and for any subsequent offense he shall be punished by imprisonment for not more than sixty (60) days or by a fine of not less than twenty-five dollars ($25.00) or more than one hundred dollars ($100) or both such fine and imprisonment. 

History of Section. 

P. L. 1954, ch. 3356, § 7. 117

208. Enforcement of chapter.-The chief of the division of fish and game and his deputies shall enforce the provisions of this chapter. 

P. L. 1954, ch. 3356, § 8. 118

CHAPTER 23 RECIPROCAL ENFORCEMENT OF FISHING LAWS SECTION. 

20-23-1. 

Powers of enforcement officers in waters between states. 

20-23-1. Powers of enforcement officers in waters between states. -If and when the states of New York and Connecticut and the commonwealth of Massachusetts or any of them shall enact similar laws for the arrest and punishment of violations of the conservation or fish laws of this state, or the state so enacting such similar law, committed or attempted to be committed by any person or persons fishing in waters lying between such states, any game protector, fish warden or other person of either state who is authorized to make arrests for violations of the conservation or fish laws of such state, shall have power and authority to make arrest on any part of such waters between such states or the shores thereof and to take any person or persons so arrested for trial to the state in which the violation was committed and there to prosecute such person or persons according to the laws of such state. 

P. L. 1945, ch. 1611, § 1. 119

CHAPTER 24 STATE RESERVATIONS 

20-24-1. Lease or control of land for protection of birds and animals-Posting, 20-24-2. Hunting on reservation prohibited-Predatory animals, 20-24-3.

20-24-4. Law as to public lands unaffected.

20-24-7.  

20-24-1. Lease or control of land for protection of birds and animals-Posting.-For the purpose of protecting any species of useful wild birds or wild quadrupeds, and for the propagation thereof, the chief of the division of fish and game may lease, or with the consent of the owner, may control any land deemed suitable in character and situation for such purpose. Such land shall be conspicuously posted and designated as a state reservation.

P. L. 1918, ch. 1663, § 1; G. L. 1923, ch. 138, § 1; G. L. 1938, ch. 251, § 1. 

20-24-2. Hunting on reservation prohibited-Predatory animals. -No person shall at any time hunt, pursue, take, kill or in any manner molest or destroy any wild bird, the nest or eggs thereof, or any wild quadruped, on land leased or controlled by the division of fish and game as a state reservation, except that the chief of said division may, in his discretion, and with such limitations as he may deem advisable, authorize in writing any deputy, the owner or the occupant of such land, to hunt, pursue, take, kill or destroy any predatory birds, the nest or eggs thereof, or any quadruped harmful to useful birds or to agriculture. Such authority shall be revocable at the pleasure of the said chief.

History of Section. 

P. L. 1918, ch. 1663, § 2; G. L, 1923, ch. 

138, § 2; G. L. 1938, ch. 251, § 2. 

20-24-3. Arrest of violators-Penalty.-It shall be the duty of the chief of said division and his deputies to arrest for violations of this chapter, and persons convicted of such violation shall be punished by a fine not exceeding twenty dollars ($20.00).

History of Section. 

P. L. 1918, ch. 1663, § 2; G. L, 1923, ch.

20-24-5. Hunting on public lands.-No person shall hunt, pursue, take, kill or in any manner molest or destroy any wild birds or game within the exterior boundaries of any state reservation, park or common, or any land held in trust for public use, except that the authorities or persons having the control and charge of such reservations, parks, commons, or other lands may in their discretion, and with such limitations as they may deem advisable, authorize persons to hunt, take or kill within said boundaries any wild birds, or animals which are not now protected by law. Such authorizations shall be by written license, and the license shall be revocable at the pleasure of the authorities or persons granting it; provided, however, that said authorities shall designate and set apart certain areas in which hunting will be permitted. All persons hunting in said designated areas shall comply with all laws as to the licensing of hunters, open and close seasons, bag limits and all other laws relating to birds and animals. The division of fish and game shall co-operate with the authorities and improve these areas for better wildlife management and shall stock birds and animals, therein.

CHAPTER 25 ARTIFICIAL PROPAGATION OF GAME 

20-25- 1. License for propagation and live sale of birds and animals.

20-25-11. Laws as to wild birds and animals unaffected. 

20-25-1. License for propagation and live sale of birds and animals. Upon written application to the director of agriculture and conservation, a license may be granted in the discretion of said director, without charge, to any person to engage in the rearing within an enclosure, of any wild birds or game quadrupeds, and to dispose of the same alive for purposes of propagation, under such rules and regulations as may be made from time to time by said director; provided, however, that any person or persons engaging in the commercial raising or selling of any wild birds, game quadrupeds or domestic game birds shall pay to the general treasurer an annual license fee of five dollars ($5.00). This license shall be issued by the director of agriculture and conservation and he shall keep a record of same. Such artificially propagated wild birds, game quadrupeds or domestic game birds, if alive, may be bought, sold, and had in possession, at any season of the year for purposes of propagation.

P. L. 1917, ch. 1513, § 1; G. L. 1923, ch. 

138, §7; P. L. 1932, ch. 1883, § 1; G. L. 

1938, ch. 252, § 1; P. L. 1939, ch. 736, § 1;

P. L. 1940, ch. 911, § 1. 

122

202: License to raise game for liberation. The director of agriculture and conservation may, in his discretion, issue a license without charge to any person to engage in the rearing within an enclosure of domestic game, and to dispose of the same alive for liberation at field trials or upon game preserves. Such artificially propagated domestic game shall be tagged for identification with tags or seals supplied to the licensee by the department of agriculture and conservation, for which the department shall be paid five cents (54) each, before being disposed of by the licensee. Game so tagged may be had in possession, transported, bought and sold at any season of the year.

G. L., ch. 252, § 1, as enacted by P. L. 

1948, ch. 2156, § 3. 

20-25-3. License to sell carcasses of game birds.-Any person or persons engaging in the commercial rearing of domestic game birds and duly licensed by said director as herein before provided, upon written application to said director and the payment to the general treasurer of an additional annual license fee of five dollars ($5.00), may be granted a license, which shall expire on the last day of December in each year at midnight, to sell, the unplucked carcasses of domestic game birds to which have been attached a tag or seal provided by the department, which tag or seal shall remain intact until the bird is used. For each tag or seal there shall be paid to the department of agriculture and conservation a fee of five cents (5¢), which moneys shall be paid by said director to the general treasurer. Said director may from time to time make such rules and regulations as he may deem necessary pertaining to the use of said tags or seals and the operation of the business of the sale of said domestic game birds.

20-25-5. Counterfeiting or misuse of tags or seals.-No person shall counterfeit any tag or seal issued by the department of agriculture and conservation in accordance with the provisions of this 123

chapter and no tag or seal so issued shall be affixed to the carcass of any game other than domestic game birds.

20-25-6. Labeling of domestic game birds for shipment by common carrier. Common carriers may receive and transport at any time carcasses of domestic game birds tagged as aforesaid, but to every package containing such carcasses shall be affixed a tag or label upon which shall be plainly printed or written the name, address and number of license of the person to whom such license was issued, the name or names of the person or persons to whom such domestic game birds are to be transported, the number and kind of carcasses contained in the package and the signed statement that said package contains domestic game birds.
