201. License required-Hunting on own land-Privileges not granted. No person shall hunt, pursue, take or kill any wild bird or animal, in this state, without having first obtained a license as hereinafter provided; provided, that nothing in this chapter shall be construed as affecting the right of a bona fide resident of this state or his immediate family to hunt, without such license, on land owned by him, or on land leased by him and on which he is actually domiciled, and which land is used exclusively for agricultural purposes, and not for club shooting purposes, nor as affecting in any way the provisions of the laws relating to trespass, nor as authorizing the pursuit, taking, wounding or killing, or the possession of wild birds or animals contrary to any laws now in force or which may be hereafter enacted, nor shall the possession of such license grant or confer any privilege not enjoyed prior to April 6, 1909. 

P. L. 1909, ch. 381, § 1; G. L. 1923, ch. 139, § 1; G. L. 1938, ch. 254, § 1. 

20-27-2. Safety instruction-Certificate of competency.-The division of fish and game shall formulate a course of instruction in safe hunting practices and the handling and use of firearms for such persons as are applying for a license for the first time and shall designate one or more competent persons or organizations to give such instruction. Any person or organization so designated shall give such instruction without charge to any person requesting the same and shall, upon the successful completion thereof, issue a certificate of competency to such person. The division of fish and game may designate the police authorities of the several cities and towns to give such instruction and issue such certificates.

History of Section. 

G. L., ch. 254, § 12, as enacted by P. 

L. 1956, ch. 3770, § 1. 

20-27-3. Appeal of refusal of certificate.-Any person who has been refused a certificate of competency under the provisions of § 20-27-2 may appeal from such refusal to the director of agriculture and conservation who shall make the final determination of the competency of such applicant.

History of Section. 

G. L., ch. 254, § 12, as enacted by P. 

L. 1956, ch. 3770, § 1. 

20-27-4. Certificate of competency required for initial license.—

No license to hunt shall be issued to any person unless he has held such a license in a prior year or unless he presents to the licensing agent a certificate of competency issued under § 20-27-2. Each agent shall transmit all such certificates presented to him to the division 128

F of fish and game in connection with his report to said division pursuant to § 20-27-16.

G. L., ch. 254, § 12, as enacted by P. 

L. 1956, ch. 3770, § 1. 

20-27-5. Members and veterans of armed forces exempt.-Any person who is serving or has served in the army, navy, air force, marine corps or coast guard or any woman's auxiliary branch thereof shall not be required to obtain a certificate of competency under § 20-27-2. 

History of Section. 

G. L., ch. 254, § 12, as enacted by P. 

L. 1956, ch. 3770, § 1. 

20-27-6. False information or fraudulent certificate.-Any person who obtains a hunting license by giving false information or by presenting a fraudulent certificate shall be fined not more than twentyfive dollars ($25.00), and such license shall be revoked and shall not be reissued for one (1) year.

History of Section. 

G. L., ch. 254, § 12, as enacted by P. 

L. 1956, ch. 3770, § 1. 

20-27-7. Issuance of license.-The clerk of every town or city shall, upon the application of any person and the payment of the license fee, hereafter provided, issue to such person a license to pursue, hunt and kill game in the state of Rhode Island during the open season; provided, however, that no license shall be granted to any person under fifteen (15) years of age. Every such license shall be printed or stamped upon some durable and waterproof material and shall bear the name, age, occupation, place of residence and an identifying description of the licensee, and shall expire on the thirty-first day of 

December next succeeding the date of issue. Every such license shall plainly and conspicuously indicate upon its face the year of issuance and shall be in the form prescribed by the chief of the division of fish and game.

History of Section. 

P. L. 1909, ch. 381, § 2; P. L. 1910, ch. 

529, § 1; P. L. 1911, ch. 690, § 1; G. L. 

1923, ch. 139, § 2; P. L. 1923, ch. 428, 

§ 1; G. L. 1938, ch. 254, § 2. 

20-27-8. Combination fishing and hunting license. The clerk of any city or town shall, upon the application of any person entitled to receive a license under this chapter, grant a combination fishing and hunting license to any applicant who desires to receive the same in the form prescribed by the chief of the division of fish and game and upon the payment of a combination license fee, equal the usual 129

fee for a license to fish and to hunt although the clerk of the city or town in which the license is recorded may retain out of such license fee not more than the usual sum for recording either a license to fish or a license to hunt. Said combination license to fish and to hunt shall be valid only until January first next following the date of issue, shall not be transferable, shall be produced for examination upon the demand of any person, and may be revoked within the discretion of the chief of the division of fish and game.

G. L., ch. 254, § 2, as enacted by P. 

L. 1948, ch. 2056, § 2; impl. am. P. L. 

1954, ch. 3358, § 2. 

20-27-9. Device to be worn by hunters. In addition to either form of said certificate to be produced for examination upon the demand of any person, any such licensee, when hunting, shall wear upon his person while hunting such device as the chief of the division. of fish and game shall prescribe which shall be plainly visible, such device to be furnished by the division of fish and game at a minimum cost to the licensee.

History of Section. 

G. L., ch. 254, § 2, as enacted by P. 

L. 1948, ch. 2056, § 2; impl. am. P. L.

20-27-10. License fees.-Every bona fide resident of the state, who is a citizen of the United States, shall pay for such hunting license a fee of two dollars ($2.00) and a recording fee of twenty-five cents (254); provided, however, that any such resident who is over sixtyfive (65) years of age may secure such license upon the payment of the recording fee. Every nonresident of this state, who is a citizen of the United States, shall pay for such license a fee of ten dollars ($10.00) and a recording fee of twenty-five cents (254). Every unnaturalized, foreign-born person shall pay for such license a fee of fifteen dollars ($15.00) and a recording fee of twenty-five cents (254); provided, that any nonresident, who is a citizen of the United 

States and who owns real estate in Rhode Island assessed for taxation at a valuation of not less than five hundred dollars ($500); and any nonresident, who is a citizen of the United States, and who is a member of a club or association incorporated for the purpose of shooting or fishing, and incorporated prior to January 1st 1909, which club or association owns real estate in Rhode Island assessed for taxation at a valuation of not less than one thousand dollars ($1.000); and any nonresident citizen of the United States, who, on the written invitation of a member of an association incorporated under the laws of this state, attends a meeting of the association for the purpose of 130

hunting foxes only, during a period not exceeding six (6) days in any one (1) year, may procure a special license upon the payment of a license fee of two dollars ($2.00) and a recording fee of twenty-five cents (25¢).

P. L. 1909, ch. 381, § 3; P. L. 1920, ch. 1898, § 1; G. L. 1923, ch. 139, § 3; 

P. L. 1927, ch. 959, § 1; G. L. 1938, ch. 

254, § 3; P. L. 1939, ch. 738, § 1. 

20-27-11. Export privileges of nonresident licensees.-Each nonresident hunting license except the special license issued for the purpose of hunting foxes, shall enable the licensee to carry from the state not more than ten (10) wild fowl or birds, the exportation of which is prohibited by law, in any one (1) calendar year; provided, that the owner thereof shall carry them open to view for inspection. 

History of Section. 

P. L. 1909, ch. 381, § 3; P. L. 1920, ch. 1898, § 1; G. L. 1923, ch. 139, § 3; 

P. L. 1927, ch. 959, § 1; G. L. 1938, ch. 

254, § 3; P. L. 1939, ch. 738, § 1. 

20-27-12. Application for license.-Every person who may desire such license shall file an application with the clerk of the town or city wherein he resides, if a resident; and with the clerk of any town or city in this state, if a nonresident, properly sworn to, stating the name, age, occupation, place of residence, nationality, weight, height, and color of hair and eyes of the applicant for whom such license is wanted, and pay the fees as provided in this chapter. 

History of Section. 

P. L. 1909, ch. 381, § 4; G. L. 1923, ch. 139, § 4; G. L. 1938, ch. 254, § 4. 

20-27-13. Issuance of licenses by state division or agents.-The chief of the division of fish and game may issue licenses in the same manner and under the same conditions as provided herein for city and town clerks. He may in his discretion appoint responsible citizens or corporations of this state engaged in operating sporting goods stores to act as his agents with authority to issue said licenses in the same manner and under the same conditions as provided herein for city and town clerks and may revoke the permits of said agents at his pleasure.

History of Section. 

G. L., ch. 254, § 11, as enacted by P. 

L. 1943, ch. 1299, § 1. 20-27-14.

Possession and exhibition of license-Endorsement- Transfer or loan.-Every person holding a license provided by this chapter shall have the same in his possession at all times while hunting in this state, and shall present the same for inspection to any 131

person demanding the same; and any person refusing to so present said license for inspection upon demand shall be liable to the same punishment as if he were hunting without such license. A license shall not be valid before the licensee shall endorse his name in his own handwriting thereupon, and shall be good only for the person to whom it is issued, and any transfer or loan of such license shall work a forfeiture of the same. 

P. L. 1909, ch. 381, §5; G. L. 1923, ch. 139, §5; G. L. 1938, ch. 254, § 5. 

20-27-15. Penalty for violations-Forfeiture of license.-Every person who shall violate any of the provisions of this chapter shall be punished by a fine of not less than twenty dollars ($20.00) nor more than fifty dollars ($50.00), or by imprisonment not exceeding thirty (30) days, or by both fine and imprisonment; and the license of any person convicted of violating any of the provisions of this chapter shall be forfeited, and no license shall be granted such person for a period of one (1) year after such conviction; and the same penalties shall be imposed upon any person who shall be convicted of hunting, pursuing or killing any wild bird or animal on a license which has been issued in the name of another person. 

History of Section. 

P. L. 1909, ch. 381, § 6; G. L. 1923, ch. 139, § 6; G. L. 1938, ch. 254, § 6. 

20-27-16. Records and accounting for licenses, license books, and fees. The clerk of every town or city shall record all such licenses to be issued by him in books kept therefor, to be supplied by the chief of the division of fish and game, one (1) coupon of which shall be retained by the town or city clerk as his record, which record shall be open to inspection by all officers authorized to make arrest and by the general treasurer or the state controller or their agents, and by the chief of the division of fish and game and his deputies, and such clerk shall, on the first Monday of every month, furnish to the general treasurer and to the chief of the division of fish and game a list of the number and kind of licenses issued by said clerk during the previous month and shall pay to the general treasurer all moneys, except recording fees, received by him for such licenses issued during the month preceding. Every such clerk shall also, on the first Monday of every month, return to the chief of the division of fish and game all license books in which no more licenses remain to be issued. 

A record shall be kept by the chief of the division of fish and game of all books containing license blanks issued to each town or city clerk and said chief shall furnish the general treasurer with a list 132

of the license books suppplied by him to each town or city clerk during the month next preceding, and every such clerk shall, within thirty (30) days succeeding January first of each year, return to the chief of the division of fish and game all license books used during the year preceding, including all stubs and unused and void certificates.

P. L. 1909, ch. 381, § 7; P. L. 1910, ch. 

529, § 2; G. L. 1923, ch. 139, §7; P. L. 

1923, ch. 428, § 2; P. L. 1928, ch. 1225, § 1; P. L. 1932, ch. 1873, § 1; G. L. 1938, ch. 254, §7; impl. am. P. L. 1939, ch. 

660, § 65. 

20-27-17. Enforcement of chapter-Disposition of fines.-It shall be the duty of the chief of the division of fish and game and his deputies to enforce the provisions of this chapter, and to prosecute every person who shall violate the same. They shall have power to arrest, without warrant, every person whom they shall find killing, pursuing, or taking any wild animal or bird contrary to the provisions of this chapter; provided, that any person so arrested without warrant shall not be detained longer than twenty-four (24) hours. Whenever complaint is made by said chief, or by his deputies, of any violation of the provisions of this chapter, he shall not be required, either by himself or by his deputies, to furnish surety for costs, or be liable for costs on such complaint. All fines recovered by virtue of this chapter shall enure one-half thereof to the use of the state and one-half thereof to the use of the complainant, and all prosecutions thereof shall be commenced within ninety (90) days after the commission of the offense, and not afterwards. 

History of Section. 

P. L. 1909, ch. 381, § 8; G. L. 1923, ch. 

139, § 8; P. L. 1926, ch. 787, § 1; G. L. 

1938, ch. 254, § 8. 

20-27-18. Forfeiture of license on conviction.—The license to pursue, hunt and kill game in the state of Rhode Island during the open season issued, under the provisions of this chapter, to any person who shall hereafter be convicted of violating any of the provisions of chapters 24 to 34, inclusive, of this title, or of §§ 11-44-4 and 11-44-5, shall be forfeited, and no such license shall be granted to such person for a period of one (1) year after such conviction.

History of Section. 

P. L. 1921, ch. 2093, § 1; G. L. 1923, ch. 

139, § 10; G. L. 1938, ch. 254, § 10. 133

SECTION. 

CHAPTER 28 HUNTING SAFETY 

20-28-1. Shooting in proximity of occupied buildings. 

20-28-2. Hunting along or across highways. 

20-28-3. Loaded weapons in vehicles. 

20-28-4. Carrying of weapon while under influence of liquor or drugs. 

20-28-5. Maximum size of hunting parties. 

20-28-6. Reports of hunting injuries. 

20-28-7.  

20-28-8. Enforcement of provisions. 

20-28-9. Restriction on size of weapons and ammunition. 

20-28-1. Shooting in proximity of occupied buildings.-It is unlawful for any person while hunting or pursuing wild birds or wild animals in this state to shoot or discharge any firearm or other deadly weapon within one hundred and twenty-five (125) yards of any occupied dwelling house, residence or other building occupied by human beings, or any barn, stable or other building used in connection therewith, without the specific permission of the owner or tenant thereof.

P. L. 1954, ch. 3351, § 1. 

20-28-2. Hunting along or across highways.-It is unlawful for any person to hunt, pursue, kill or shoot at any bird or animal along, upon or across any public highway in this state. 

History of Section. 

P. L. 1954, ch. 3351, § 2. 

20-28-3. Loaded weapons in vehicles.-It is unlawful for any person to have in his possession a loaded rifle or loaded shotgun or a rifle or shotgun from the magazine of which all shells and cartridges have not been removed, in or on any vehicle or conveyance or its attachments while standing upon or along, or being driven upon or along any highway, road, lane or trail within this state; provided, however, that the provisions of this section shall not apply to law enforcement officers while upon official duty. 

History of Section. 

P. L. 1954, ch. 3351, § 3. 

20-28-4. Carrying of weapon while under influence of liquor or drugs. It is unlawful to hunt anywhere in this state, or to enter any of the fields or woods, or to enter upon any lands or waters within or 

134

bordering this state while possessed of a firearm or other weapon for the purpose of hunting, when intoxicated or under the influence of intoxicating liquor or narcotic drugs. 

P. L. 1954, ch. 3351, § 4. 

20-28-5. Maximum size of hunting parties.-It is unlawful for more than five (5) persons to hunt in unison, or to co-operate in any manner with each other to hunt, for wild birds or wild animals of any kind.

History of Section. 

P. L. 1954, ch. 3351, § 5. 

20-23-6. Reports of hunting injuries.-Every person who shall cause an injury to a human being by gunfire while hunting or shall have inflicted an injury upon himself with firearms while hunting or shall have inflicted an injury upon himself while trapping shall render a report to the division of fish and game within seventy-two (72) hours after such injury, unless such person be physically incapable of making the required report, in which event it shall be the duty of such person to designate some other person to file the report within the specified time.

History of Section. 

P. L. 1954, ch. 3351, § 6. 

20-28-7. -Every person violating any of the provisions of §§ 20-28-1 to 20-28-6, inclusive, upon conviction shall be sentenced to pay a fine of not exceeding twenty dollars ($20.00) and shall forfeit his hunting license and be deprived of the privilege of hunting in this state for one (1) year.

History of Section. 

P. L. 1954, ch. 3351, § 7. 

20-28-8. Enforcement of provisions.-The chief of the division of fish and game and his conservation, officers, members of the state police and town and city policemen shall have the power to enforce the provisions of §§ 20-28-1 to 20-28-7, inclusive.

History of Section. 

P. L. 1954, ch. 3351, § 8; impl. am. P. 

L. 1954, ch. 3358, § 2. 

20-28-9. Restriction on size of weapons and ammunition.-Whoever shall use or have in his possession while hunting in this state any rifle other than twenty-two (22) caliber rim fire, or shotgun and shells loaded with ball, bullets, slug or shot larger than No. 2 shot, shall be fined not more than twenty dollars ($20.00); provided, however, that from April 1 to September 30, both dates inclusive, twenty-two (22) caliber rifle's may be used during daylight hours and, provided, further, that this section shall not apply to persons killing 135

or pursuing deer under authority and in accordance with the provisions of § 20-30-2. The chief of the division of fish and game and his conservation officers shall enforce the provisions of this section and shall prosecute every person who shall violate the same and they shall have power to arrest without warrant every person whom they shall find violating the provisions of this section; provided that any person so arrested shall not be detained longer than twenty-four (24) hours.

G. L., ch. 137, § 37, as enacted by P. 

L. 1928, ch. 1201, § 5; P. L. 1931, ch. 

1750, § 1; G. L. 1938, ch. 248, § 25; P. 

L. 1954, ch. 3352, § 1; impl. am. P. L. 

1954, ch. 3358, § 2. 136

201. Birds protected.-Every person who shall take, kill, destroy, buy, sell or offer for sale, or have in his possession any wild bird, or birds, including grebes, loons, gulls, terns, bitterns, herons, cranes, doves, woodpeckers, nighthawks, meadowlarks, commonly called "marsh quail," and blue jays at any season of the year, except as hereinafter provided, shall be fined not exceeding twenty dollars ($20.00) for each of such birds.

20-29-2. Penalty for hunting birds.-Every person who shall pursue with intent to kill any wild bird, except as hereinafter provided, shall be fined twenty dollars ($20.00) for each offense.

History of Section. 

G. L. 1896, ch. 112, § 2; P. L. 1900, ch.

20-29-3. Game birds not protected.-Sections 20-29-1 and 20-29-2 shall not apply to the shooting of anatidae, including brant, wild ducks and wild geese, between the first day of October and the fifteenth day of January, next following, inclusive; rails between the first day of September and the thirtieth day of November, inclusive; 

Wilson snipe commonly called "jack snipe," between the first day of 

October and the thirtieth day of November; woodcock between the first day of November and the thirtieth day of November; ruffed grouse, commonly known as "partridge," quail, commonly called "bobwhite," or cock pheasants, between the first day of November and the thirty-first day of December, inclusive; provided, however, that this section shall not be construed as permitting the shooting of hen pheasants at any season of the year, and provided also, that this section shall not be construed as affecting the closed season for wood duck, as provided for in § 20-29-8.

204. Penalty for destruction of nest or eggs.-Every person who shall wilfully disturb or destroy the nest or eggs of any wild bird, except as hereinafter provided, shall be fined twenty dollars ($20.00) for each offense.

G. L. 1896, ch. 112, § 3; P. L. 1900, ch. 746, § 3; G. L. 1909, ch. 136, § 3; G.

20-29-5. Nests not protected-Protection of poultry.-Sections 

20-29-1, 20-29-2 and 20-29-4 shall not apply to the killing of, or the destruction of the nests or eggs of, English or European house sparrows (passer domesticus), European starlings (sturnus vulgaris), or crows (genus corvus); nor shall they be deemed to make it unlawful for the owner or occupant of land to kill hawks or owls when in the act of destroying poultry, the property of said owner or occupant, on the land owned or occupied by said owner or occupant.

20-29-6. Closed season on game birds-Cock pheasants in New 

Shoreham and Jamestown.-Every person who shall take, kill or pursue with intent to kill, any of the anatidae, commonly known as "waterfowl," including any brant, wild duck or goose between the fifteenth day of January and the first day of October next following; any rail between the thirtieth day of November and the first day of 

September following; Wilson snipe between the thirtieth day of 

November and the first day of October following; woodcock between the thirtieth day of November and the first day of November following; ruffed grouse, commonly known as "partridge," quail, commonly known as "bobwhite," or cock pheasant, between the thirty-first day of December and the first day of November following, or hen pheasant at any season of the year, shall be fined not exceeding twenty dollars ($20.00) for each offense; provided, that every person who shall take, kill or pursue with intent to kill within the town of New 

Shoreham at any time except on the first or third Wednesday in 

November, or the first or third Wednesday in December in any year any cock pheasant shall be fined not exceeding twenty dollars ($20.00) for each offense; and every person who shall take or kill within the town of New Shoreham on any such Wednesday more than two (2) cock pheasant, shall be fined not exceeding twenty dollars 139

($20.00) for each bird which such person shall take or kill in excess of such two (2) birds; and provided, further, that every person who shall take, kill or pursue with intent to kill within the town of Jamestown at any time except on each Tuesday in the months of November and December in any year any cock pheasant shall be fined not exceeding twenty dollars ($20.00) for each offense; and provided, further, that every person who shall take or kill within the town of 

Jamestown on any such Tuesday more than two (2) cock pheasant shall be fined not exceeding twenty dollars ($20.00) for each bird which such person shall so take or kill in excess of such two (2) birds. Whoever shall buy, sell or offer for sale at any time of the year or have in his possession any rail, black-breasted plover, golden plover, grouse, quail, or pheasant, during the time that they are herein protected by law, whenever or wherever killed, shall be fined not exceeding twenty dollars ($20.00) for each such bird.

20-29-7. Bag limits on grouse, pheasants, woodcock, and quail- 

Possession for sale.-Whoever takes, kills, or destroys in any one (1) day more than two (2) ruffed grouse, three (3) cock pheasants, six (6) woodcock, or six (6) quail, shall be fined not more than twenty dollars ($20.00) for each bird in excess thereof, and the possession of any such birds in excess of the numbers herein before prescribed shall be evidence of the taking or killing of the same, and whoever sells or offers for sale, or has in his possession, with intent to sell, any ruffed grouse, commonly called "partridge," quail, woodcock or undomesticated pheasant, whenever and wherever such bird may be taken or killed, shall be punished by a fine of twenty dollars ($20.00) for each bird, one-half thereof to the use of the complainant and onehalf thereof to the use of the state.

History of Section. 

P. L. 1905, ch. 1231, § 1; G. L. 1909, ch. 136, § 6; G. L. 1923, ch. 137, § 6; P. 

L. 1928, ch. 1201, § 3; G. L. 1938, ch. 

248, § 6. 

20-29-8. Hunting or possession of aquatic birds.-Every person who shall take, kill or pursue with intent to kill, destroy, sell, buy, 140

or offer for sale, or have in his possession any wood duck, swan, or any of the limicolae, commonly known as shore, marsh or beach birds, including curlew, dowitchers, dodwits, knots, phalaropes, plover, sandpipers, snipe, stilts, surf birds, turnstones, willett or Hungarian partridge, shall for each offense be fined not exceeding twenty dollars ($20.00); provided that the words "Hungarian partridge" shall not be construed to apply to the birds commonly called partridge or ruffed grouse; and provided, further, that this section shall not be construed as affecting the open season for woodcock, blackbreasted plover, golden plover, Wilson snipe, greater yellowlegs or lesser yellowlegs as already provided for in § 20-29-6.

20-29-9. Export of birds.-Every person who shall carry or send beyond the limits of this state at any time any wild duck, wild swan, wild goose, rail, any of the limicolae or shore, marsh, or beach birds, any woodcock, quail, commonly called "bobwhite," or ruffed grouse, commonly called "partridge," shall be fined not exceeding twenty dollars ($20.00) for each of said birds.

20-29-11. Possession as evidence of taking.-Possession by any person of any bird during the time when the taking, killing or destruction of such bird is prohibited by this chapter, shall be evidence before any court of competent jurisdiction that such bird was taken, killed or destroyed in violation of this chapter.

Killing or maiming of carrier pigeons.-Every person who shall wilfully maim, shoot, or otherwise kill any antwerp or homing pigeon, commonly called and known by the name of "carrier pigeon," either while in flight or at rest shall be fined for each such offense not exceeding twenty dollars ($20.00).

20-29-14. Forfeiture to landowner for shooting during closed season.-Every person who shall between the fourteenth day of December and the fifteenth day of October next following, shoot at or kill any bird upon land not owned or occupied by himself and without permission of the owner or occupant thereof, shall forfeit and pay to the owner, or occupant thereof, five dollars ($5.00) for the first offense and ten dollars ($10.00) for every subsequent offense, in addition to the damage sustained. 142

20-29-15. Watchaug pond and Poquiant brook.--No person shall take, kill, destroy or pursue or in any manner molest any wild bird in any season of the year in that part of the town of Charlestown known as Watchaug pond and Poquiant brook or within one hundred (100) yards of the shore or banks of the same. Any person violating any of the provisions of this section shall be guilty of a misdemeanor and upon conviction thereof shall be fined not exceeding one hundred dollars ($100).

201. Deer hunting unlawful except as provided.-It shall be unlawful to pursue or shoot deer in this state except in accordance with the provisions of the following sections. 

P. L. 1907, ch. 1455, § 1; G. L. 1909, ch. 137, § 4; G. L. 1923, ch. 140, § 4; P. L. 

1925, ch. 647, § 1; G. L. 1938, ch. 249, $ 4. 

20-30-2. Permit to landowner to protect property.-Any person owning or occupying any farm or orchard and any person in his employ, while on his own premises or the premises of his employer, may kill any deer which shall be found destroying any crops, vegetables, or fruit trees belonging to such person or his employer; provided, however, that no such person shall shoot any deer unless he has obtained from the director of agriculture and conservation a permit so to do; and the director of agriculture and conservation may, upon application, issue to any responsible owner, or his employees, a permit authorizing such person to shoot deer in accordance with the provisions of this section. No such permit shall be issued until such time as the director of agriculture and conservation shall cause an investigation to be made to determine that actual damage has been done to any crops, vegetables or fruit trees by any deer. When such a permit is issued, it shall expire sixty (60) calendar days after the date of issuance. 

History of Section. 

P. L. 1907, ch. 1455, § 2; G. L. 1909, ch. 137, §5; G. L. 1923, ch. 140, § 5; P. 

L. 1925, ch. 647, § 1; P. L. 1928, ch. 1201, $6; G. L. 1938, ch. 249, § 5; P. L. 1950, ch. 2554, § 1; P. L. 1956, ch. 3775, § 2. 

20-30-3. Archery permit.-Any person in possession of a resident or nonresident hunting or combination license may obtain a special archery permit, issued by the director of agriculture and conservation for a fee of five dollars ($5.00) for residents and twenty dollars ($20.00) for nonresidents. The permit shall be valid only from the 

5th to the 25th of January, inclusive, in the year of issue and shall allow the taking of one (1) deer only (either a doe or buck); shall be produced for examination upon the demand of any person; shall be of the design prescribed by the director of agriculture and conservation and may be revoked within the discretion of said director

clusive, shall be equipped with a nonmechanical bow capable of shooting a hunting arrow one hundred fifty (150) paces, and shall use only broadhead arrows with at least two (2) cutting edges seveneighths (78) inches or greater in width constructed without rivets. 

Each arrow used while hunting in accordance with the provisions of §§ 20-30-3 to 20-30-7, inclusive, shall carry the name and address. of the person licensed.

20-30-6. Notice of killing of deer-Tagging and cancelation of license. Any permittee killing a deer shall notify a conservation officer within a reasonable time. The officer shall tag the deer and cancel the license.

20-30-7. Possession of unlawful weapons while hunting--Possession of guns, pistols, or other firearms, crossbows, explosive points, poisonous or barbed points, or possession of any deer showing evidence that it was shot with a firearm while hunting deer with bow and arrow shall be a violation of §§ 20-30-3 to 20-30-7, inclusive. Any person violating the provisions of said sections shall be fined not exceeding one hundred dollars ($100). 

History of Section.

1956, ch. 3775, § 2. 

20-30-8. Prohibition of hunting by local authorities.-The city council of any city or the town council of any town is hereby authorized and empowered to prohibit hunting, possessing or taking deer under §§ 20-30-3 to 20-30-7, inclusive, within the boundaries of said city or town by ordinance duly enacted.

History of Section. 

P. L. 1956, ch. 3775, § 3. 

20-30-9. Traps Use of dogs-Jacklights.-No person shall make, set or use any trap or snare, salt lick or other device for the purpose of ensnaring, enticing, taking, injuring or killing a deer and no person shall use a dog for the purpose of pursuing, chasing, 147

hunting or killing a deer. No person shall use, attempt to use or possess any jacklight for the purpose of taking any deer. For the purpose of this section, a jacklight shall be construed as any artificial light when used in conjunction with any rifle larger than a twenty-two (22) long rifle, or with a shotgun and ball shells or shot larger than no. 4 shot. Possession of such articles by any person during the hours of darkness shall be prima facie evidence of a violation of this Section. Any person who shall violate any provision of this section shall for the first offense, be fined not less than fifty dollars ($50.00) nor more than two hundred dollars ($200) or be imprisoned not less than thirty (30) days nor more than six (6) months or be both fined and imprisoned, and for each subsequent offense shall be fined not less than fifty dollars ($50.00) nor more than two hundred dollars ($200) or be imprisoned for not more than one (1) year.

G. L., ch. 140, § 6, as enacted by P. L. 

1925, ch. 647, § 1; G. L. 1938, ch. 249, § 6; P. L. 1948, ch. 2054, § 1; P. L. 1949, ch. 2256, § 1. 

20-30-10. Report of killing or wounding- -Whoever wounds or kills a deer shall make a written report, signed by him, and send it within twenty-four (24) hours of such wounding or killing, to the chief of the division of fish and game, stating the facts relative to such wounding or killing, and any person violating the provisions of §§ 20-30-1 to 20-30-10, inclusive, shall be fined not less than one hundred dollars ($100) nor more than five hundred dollars ($500) for each offense.

20-30-11. Investigations-Enforcement powers.-It shall be the duty of the chief of the division of fish and game to forthwith investigate the circumstances surrounding the wounding and killing of all deer reported to him under § 20-30-10; and to enforce all provisions of this chapter and to prosecute every person who shall violate the same: He shall have power to arrest, without warrant, every person whom he shall find pursuing with intent to kill, taking or killing, or having in his possession any undomesticated deer or any part thereof contrary to the provisions of this chapter; provided, that any person so arrested without warrant shall not be detained longer than twenty-four (24) hours. 148

20-30-12. Immunity from liability for costs.-Whenever complaint is made by the chief of division, or by his deputies, of any violation of the provisions of this chapter, he shall not be required, either by himself or by his deputies, to furnish surety for costs, or be liable for costs on such complaint.

20-30-13. Deer colliding with vehicles.-Notwithstanding the provisions of any general or public law, or amendment thereto, to the contrary, the resident owner of every motor vehicle which has been substantially damaged by collision with a deer shall, within twenty-four (24) hours thereafter, report the accident to a conservation officer. The officer shall investigate and, if he finds the damage has been done as alleged, he shall give a certificate to such person entitling such person to the ownership of the carcass to be possessed and consumed only within the immediate family of the owner of said vehicle.

History of Section. 

P. L. 1956, ch. 3850, § 1. 149

20-31-1. Closed season-Night hunting-Landowners.-Every person who shall take, kill or destroy any undomesticated rabbit, hare or gray squirrel between the thirty-first day of December and the first day of November, next following, shall be punished by a fine of twenty dollars ($20.00) for each and every rabbit, hare, or gray squirrel so taken, killed or destroyed, one-half of said fine to be to the use of the complainant and the other half to the use of the state; provided, however, it shall be unlawful for any person to take any rabbit during the period from one (1) hour after sunset to one (1) hour before sunrise; and provided, further, that any person owning and operating any farm and any person in his employ may, while on his own premises or on the premises of his employer, kill any rabbit which shall be found destroying or mutilating any crops, vegetables or fruit trees belonging to such person or his employer; and provided, further, that the carcass of such rabbit shall not be sold or offered for sale but shall be retained by the owner of the farm for his own use; and provided, further, that the director of agriculture and conservation, or any of his deputies, may require the owner of the farm to so prove that the rabbit was killed on the premises of the owner, and in such case the provisions of § 20-34-1 shall not hold.

202. Additional open season on rabbits in Newport county. 

Any person holding a valid hunting license shall be allowed to take, kill or destroy any undomesticated rabbit or rabbits in Newport county, between the first day of November in any year until the fifteenth day of February, next following, notwithstanding the provisions of 20-31-1.

P. L. 1945, ch. 1586, § 1.

20-31-4. Bag limits-Unlawful sale or possession.-Whoever takes, kills or destroys in any one (1) day more than two (2) hares (commonly called snowshoe rabbits or white rabbits) or five (5) rabbits (two of which may be hares) or five (5) gray squirrels, shall be fined not more than twenty dollars ($20.00) for each animal in excess thereof, and the possession of any such animals in excess of the numbers hereinbefore prescribed shall be evidence of the taking or killing of the same; and whosoever sells or offers for sale or has in his possession with intent to sell any undomesticated rabbit, hare or gray squirrel contrary to the provisions of this section or § 2031-1 shall be punished by a fine of twenty dollars ($20.00) for each animal, one-half thereof to the use of the complainant and one-half thereof to the use of the state.

201. Closed season on muskrats, minks, otters, and skunks. 

Any person who shall take, kill, destroy, pursue with intent to kill, or set, maintain or tend any trap or snare for the purpose of taking, killing and destroying, any muskrat, mink or otter between the first day of February and the fifteenth day of November in any year, in the counties of Providence, Kent and Washington and between the fifteenth day of February and the first day of December in any year in the other counties of the state; any skunk between the fifteenth day of March and the fifteenth day of November in any year, shall be punished by a fine not exceeding twenty dollars ($20.00) for each offense.

20-32-2. Newport and between the first day of February and the fifteenth day of November in any year in the other counties of the state, shall be punished by a fine not exceeding twenty dollars ($20.00) for each offense.

20-32-4. Additional closed season on muskrats on island of Conanicut. Notwithstanding any of the provisions of this chapter, no person shall take, kill, destroy, pursue with intent to kill, or set, maintain or tend any trap or snare for the purpose of taking, killing and destroying any muskrat in the town of Jamestown upon the island of Conanicut during the period from February 15th to January 15th, annually. Any person who is found guilty of violating the provisions of this section shall be punished by a fine not exceeding twenty dollars ($20.00) for each offense. 154

P. L. 1953, ch. 3130, §§ 1, 2. 

20-32-5. Regulations of director as to hunting.-After investigation, or upon information otherwise obtained by the director of agriculture and conservation as to the supply of fur-bearing animals, the director may by appropriate rules and regulations fix the seasons, trapping hours, and daily season and possession limits, or remove protection, or increase or reduce seasons and bag limits for any or all species of fur-bearing animals throughout the state or any part thereof or prescribe the methods of trapping therein, when in his opinion such action is necessary to assure the maintenance of an adequate supply of such animals, or to reduce the numbers. thereof to safeguard game birds and animals. Any violation of such rules and regulations upon conviction shall be punished by a fine not exceeding twenty dollars ($20.00).

History of Section. 

G. L., ch. 250, § 1, as enacted by P. L. 

1949, ch. 2362, § 2. 

20-32-6. Bag limit on raccoons-Keeping of live raccoons.-It shall be illegal to take more than two (2) raccoons during any period from sunset of one day to sunset of the following day by any one (1) party, and not more than twenty (20) in one season.

Possession of more than twenty (20) raccoons, or parts thereof, shall be prima facie evidence that the provisions of this section have been violated unless said person is the holder of and has a trapping license in his possession.

For the purpose of this section "a party" is defined as a group of men or dogs hunting together as a unit. Nothing in this chapter shall be construed to prohibit a person from keeping a live raccoon or raccoons for either breeding or other purposes while having in his possession a permit issued by the division of fish and game to do so.

Any person violating any of the provisions of this section shall be fined not exceeding twenty dollars ($20.00) for each offense.

History of Section. 

G. L., ch. 249, § 12, as enacted by P. 

L. 1949, ch. 2258, § 1. 

20-32-7. Wire snares-Poisoning.-Whoever shall construct, erect, set, repair, maintain or tend any wire snare, or spread any poison, except within buildings located upon land owned or occupied by him, for the purpose of catching or killing any animal shall be punished by a fine of not less than fifty dollars ($50.00) nor more than one hundred dollars ($100), or be imprisoned not less than sixty (60) nor more than ninety (90) days, or both; pro- 155

vided, that the chief of the division of fish and game may, whenever in his discretion any given locality is overrun with vermin destructive to wild bird or animal life in this state, suspend the trapping laws in such locality by issuing permits authorizing the holder of such permit to set traps or snares in the open, under such rules and regulations as may be adopted by the said division, after hearing and after due notice to all persons interested, and any person holding such permit may lawfully set traps or snares in the open in such locality.

P. L. 1929, ch. 1424, § 3; G. L. 1938, ch. 250, § 3. 

20-32-8. Unlawful traps.-Any person who shall set, place, maintain or tend a steel trap with a spread of more than six (6) inches, or a steel trap with teeth jaws, or a "stop-thief" or choke trap with an opening of more than six (6) inches, shall be punished by a fine of not exceeding one hundred dollars ($100).

History of Section. 

P. L. 1929, ch. 1424, § 4; G. L. 1938, ch. 250, § 4. 

20-32-9. Consent of landowner for steel trap-Frequency of visits. -Any person who shall set, maintain or tend a steel trap on enclosed land of another without the consent in writing of the owner thereof, and any person who shall fail to visit at least once in twenty-four (24) hours a trap set or maintained by him, shall be punished by a fine not exceeding twenty dollars ($20.00).

History of Section. 

P. L. 1929, ch. 1424, § 5; G. L. 1938, ch. 250, § 5. 

20-32-10. Traps in protected places.-Any person who shall set, place, maintain or tend any steel trap, "stop thief" or choke trap for the purpose of catching or killing any wild animal, unless the same shall be placed in a hole, brush pile, stone wall or other protected place, so situated as to be inaccessible to any domestic animal, shall be punished by a fine of not exceeding twenty dollars ($20.00).

20-32-11. Green pelt as evidence of unlawful taking.-The possession of a green pelt or any part thereof by any person, except during the open season for such animal and for ten (10) days thereafter, shall be prima facie evidence that such pelt or part thereof was unlawfully taken and possessed. For the purposes of this section a "green pelt" is defined to mean a pelt which is not dried or cured, or tanned. 156

20-32-12. Placement of traps before opening of season.-No trap shall be set, staked or placed where fur-bearing animals might be found before 12 o'clock noon on the opening date of the season.

20-32-13. Digging or smoking out animals.-Any person or persons who shall remove or attempt to remove a live muskrat, raccoon, mink, otter, or skunk from any hole in the ground, from any stone wall, from within any ledge or from under any stone, from any den or house, or from any hole in any log or tree by digging or smoking; and whoever shall set a trap within eight (8) feet from a muskrat house shall be punished by a fine not exceeding one hundred dollars ($100). Provided: that this section shall not prohibit an owner or occupant of land from protecting his property as provided in this chapter.

CHAPTER 33 FOX BOUNTIES SECTION. 

SECTION. 

20-33-1. 20-33-2. 20-33-3. 

Amount of bounty. 

Proof and payment of bounty.

Consent of landowner for trapping-Tags on traps. 

20-33-4.  

20-33-1. Amount of bounty.-Every legal resident of the state of Rhode Island who, during the period between January first and December thirty-first in any year, shall legally kill, within the limits of the state, any wild fox not in captivity, shall, upon presentation of the proof hereinafter designated, all the provisions of § 20-33-2 having been complied with, receive therefor the sum of three dollars ($3.00) to be paid by the general treasurer. 

G. L., ch. 642, § 19, as enacted by P.

L. 1940, ch. 813, § 1. 

20-33-2. Proof and payment of bounty.-Before such legal resident shall be entitled to receive the sum specified in § 20-33-1, he shall exhibit, to either the town or city clerk of the town or city in which said legal resident resides, the entire carcass of the fox and shall make affidavit of the time and place of such killing. The officer before whom such animal shall be exhibited shall thereupon cause the tongue to be removed from said carcass and, if satisfied that said affidavit is true, certify that the person making such affidavit exhibited to said officer the entire carcass of the fox alleged to have been killed and that said officer examined such carcass and found it intact with no portion of the tongue removed or mutilated, and that he, the said officer, caused the tongue to be removed from such carcass; said affidavit with certificate shall be forwarded to the chief of the division of fish and game of the department of agriculture and conservation and, upon the approval of said chief, such affidavit and certificate shall be sent to the controller, said forms for affidavits, certificates and approvals to be furnished to the town and city clerks by the chief of said division of fish and game; and the controller shall, if said affidavit and certificate are in accordance with the provisions of this section and are presented within thirty (30) days after the time named in such affidavit, draw his order upon the general treasurer in favor of the person signing such affidavit. The general assembly shall annually appropriate such sum as it may deem necessary for the purpose of carrying out the provisions of this section and § 20-33-1.

G. L., ch. 642, § 20, as enacted by P.

CHAPTER 34 POSSESSION AND SALE OF GAME

20-34-1. Unlawful possession of rabbits, squirrels, or deer.-No person shall have in his possession at any time when the killing of the same is prohibited by law any undomesticated rabbit, hare, gray squirrel or deer, unless the same shall have been legally taken under the provisions of chapter 30 or chapter 31 of this title or shall have been legally imported from another state or country, where the same shall have been legally taken or killed, and the chief of said division or his deputy may seize without warrant any undomesticated rabbit, hare, gray squirrel or deer found in the possession of any person, at any time when the killing of the same is prohibited by law, unless satisfied that the same is legally held under the provisions of chapter 30 or chapter 31 of this title. Any person violating the provisions of this section shall be fined not more than five hundred dollars ($500) for each offense.

G. L., ch. 113, § 6, as enacted by P. L.

20-34-2. Permit to import birds or game animals.-The chief of the division of fish and game may grant a permit, under such conditions as he may deem expedient, to any person to transport into the state any birds or other game protected in any manner by the laws of the state and which have been lawfully killed without the state 162

and the transportation of which is lawful. No person shall sell or offer for sale any birds or game held in possession under a permit authorized under the provisions of this section. Any permit so granted may be suspended or revoked by the chief of said division. 

Any person violating any provision of this section shall be punished by a fine of not more than twenty dollars ($20.00) for each bird or game quadruped.

G. L., ch. 137, § 33, as enacted by P.

20-34-3. Possession of wild birds not for sale.-No person shall have in his possession, at any time, more than fifteen (15) wild birds, the sale of which is prohibited by law. Whoever violates the provisions of this section shall be punished by a fine of not exceeding twenty dollars ($20.00) for each bird or part thereof.

20-34-4. Possession by dealers or restaurants of game not authorized for sale. No person, firm, corporation or association dealing in poultry, meats, fish, groceries or other provisions, or engaged in the cold storage business, or any other business which requires, or has an ice box or cold storage or freezing vault, of any description, for use in connection with his, their or its business, and no hotel, club, public house, restaurant or victualers house shall have in his, their or its possession, at any time of year, at such place of business, or at any other place where any of his, their or its provisions, which are for use in his, their or its business, are kept, any bird or animal the sale of which is prohibited by chapter 29 of this title.

206. Issuance, execution and return of warrant for unlawfully possessed game.-Whenever complaint is made on oath to any magistrate authorized to issue warrants in criminal cases, that the complainant believes and has reasonable cause to believe that wild birds or wild animals are being possessed contrary to law, or that the laws in relation to wild birds or wild animals have been or are being violated in any building or place, such magistrate, if satisfied that there is reasonable cause for such belief, shall issue a search warrant authorizing any officer competent to serve a warrant, to search such place or building; but no search shall be made after sunset, unless specially authorized by the magistrate upon satisfactory cause shown; and all such wild birds or wild animals shall be taken and be kept by such officer in some proper place of security until they can be proceeded against in the manner hereinafter provided; and the officer shall summon the owner or keeper, if any be named in the complaint, if to be found by him, and the warrant shall be returned by said officer with his doings thereon, as soon as may be, to the district court having jurisdiction of the same in the district where such wild birds or wild animals shall have been seized. 

G. L. 1896, ch. 112, § 18; G. L. 1909, ch. 136, § 24; P. L. 1916, ch. 1385, § 2; 

G. L. 1923, ch. 137, § 22; G. L. 1938, ch. 248, 18. 

20-34-7. Posting of notice of seizure.-If the owner or keeper be not named in the warrant or not found, the court shall order a notice of such seizure to be given by posting up written notices in at least three (3) public places in the town or city, one of which shall be on the building or near the place, where the seizure was made, and such other notice shall be given as the court shall deem to be necessary. 

History of Section. 

G. L., ch. 136, § 24, as enacted by P. 

L. 1916, ch. 1385, § 2; G. L. 1923, ch. 137, § 22; G. L. 1938, ch. 248, § 18. 

20-34-8. Forfeiture of unlawfully possessed animals.-All wild birds and wild animals seized under the provisions of this chapter and title, which were possessed or kept in violation of law, shall be forfeited to the state, and the officer making such seizure, or an officer designated by the court for the purpose, shall forthwith proceed to prosecute for the forfeiture thereof in the manner provided by law.

If the cause of forfeiture be proved, the court before which the complaint or information shall be tried shall enter sentence of forfeiture to the state against said wild birds or wild animals; and 1.64

any person aggrieved by such sentence may appeal therefrom to the superior court according to the provisions relating to criminal appeals from district courts. If the cause of forfeiture be not proved, then all such seized wild birds or wild animals shall be forthwith restored to the proper owner thereof or to the place from which they were taken by the officer who seized the same. 

Upon the entry of sentence of forfeiture against such wild birds or wild animals, if no appeal be then taken or no recognizance given as prescribed in other criminal cases, the court shall forthwith issue a warrant to the officer, commanding him forthwith to destroy or to distribute to public hospitals, or if alive, to liberate said wild birds or wild animals; and every such officer shall execute said warrant and shall return the same, with his doings thereon, to the court, within such time as the court shall direct. In case of final judgment for the state on appeal, the court entering such final judgment shall issue the like warrant, as aforesaid, for the destruction or liberation of the wild birds or wild animals seized.

G. L., ch. 136, § 24, as enacted by P. 

L. 1916, ch. 1385, § 2; G. L. 1923, ch. 

137, § 22; G. L. 1938, ch. 248, § 18. 

20-34-9. Fees of officers.-There shall be allowed and paid to the officer who shall make the same for every seizure made under the foregoing provisions, the sum of five dollars ($5.00), with two dollars ($2.00) additional for every day after the first that such officer shall be necessarily employed in causing the wild birds or wild animals. seized to be condemned and ten cents (104) for every mile that he shall necessarily travel therein, together with such reasonable sum as the court may deem just for necessary expense incurred in providing storage for wild birds or wild animals seized, pending proceedings for condemnation, all which costs shall be taxed in the bill of costs and the amount allowed by the court in which final judgment shall be rendered to such officers, shall be paid by the state controller out of any money in the treasury not otherwise appropriated; provided, that no fees herein provided to be paid to the officer making such seizure shall be paid to any officer, who at the time of such seizure is under pay as a deputy in the division of fish and game.

CHAPTER 35 FIELD TRIALS AND SHOOTING PRESERVES SECTION. 

20-35-1. Field trial licenses. 

20-35-2. Shooting preserves. 

20-35-3. Laws inapplicable to field trials 20-35-4. and shooting preserves. 

Restriction to open season. 

20-35-1. Field trial licenses. The director of agriculture and conservation is hereby authorized to issue without charge to persons or organizations applying therefor licenses for the holding of field trials at which liberated domestic game may be taken by shooting.

Such game may be taken by shooting during the months of September through April 15th.

P. L. 1948, ch. 2156, § 1; P. L. 1950, ch. 2635, § 1; P. L. 1953, ch. 3138, § 1; 

P. L. 1955, ch. 3576, § 1. 

20-35-2. Shooting preserves.-The director of agriculture and conservation is hereby authorized in his discretion to issue to an owner or lessee of wholly enclosed lands a shooting preserve license permitting him to possess and to release and to take by shooting thereon liberated domestic game. The license shall expire on December 31st annually and the fee therefor shall be ten dollars ($10.00). 

Before any shooting may be done the licensee must advise the department of agriculture and conservation in writing of the number of each species of domestic game purchased and/or otherwise acquired for liberation, and request and receive in writing a shooting authorization which shall state the number of each species of domestic game which may be taken by shooting. Such authorizations shall be based on the number and age of the birds liberated which in no case shall be less than eighty per cent (80%) of the number of birds released for shooting. In addition, on each area or portion thereof which is licensed for the first time, or for which the license has not been renewed for a period of one (1) year or more, the licensee shall, in the case of pheasants, make an initial liberation to be witnessed by a representative of the department of a number of birds equal to one (1) bird for each ten (10) acres in such licensed premises in the ratio of one (1) cock to four (4) hens. The licensee or with his written consent the holder of a license to hunt in this state may take domestic game liberated in accordance with this section by shooting 166

under the rules prescribed by the department. The taking or killing of domestic game refers only to the taking or killing on the premises specified in the shooting preserve license.

P. L. 1948, ch. 2156, § 2. 

20-35-3. Laws inapplicable to field trials and shooting preserves. 

The provisions of § 4-1-16, 20-29-6, 20-29-7, 20-29-11 and 20-34-3 shall not be construed to apply to shooting preserves, to field trials upon liberated domestic game, or to the possession, releasing, or shooting of liberated domestic game at field trials or upon shooting preserves licensed in accordance with the provisions of this chapter. 

History of Section. 

P. L. 1948, ch. 2156, § 4. 

20-35-4. Restriction to open season.-Nothing herein shall be construed so as to authorize the taking of game by shooting, other than liberated, domestic pheasant, quail and mallard ducks properly banded, at any time other than during the open hunting season. 

History of Section. 

P. L. 1948, ch. 2156, § 5; P. L. 1950, ch. 2635, § 1; P. L. 1953, ch. 3138, § 1; 

P. L. 1955, ch. 3576, § 1. 167

201. Birds protected.-Every person who shall take, kill, destroy, buy, sell or offer for sale, or have in his possession any wild bird, or birds, including grebes, loons, gulls, terns, bitterns, herons, cranes, doves, woodpeckers, nighthawks, meadowlarks, commonly called "marsh quail," and blue jays at any season of the year, except as hereinafter provided, shall be fined not exceeding twenty dollars ($20.00) for each of such birds.

20-29-3. Game birds not protected.-Sections 20-29-1 and 20-29-2 shall not apply to the shooting of anatidae, including brant, wild ducks and wild geese, between the first day of October and the fifteenth day of January, next following, inclusive; rails between the first day of September and the thirtieth day of November, inclusive;

Wilson snipe commonly called "jack snipe," between the first day of

204. Penalty for destruction of nest or eggs.-Every person who shall wilfully disturb or destroy the nest or eggs of any wild bird, except as hereinafter provided, shall be fined twenty dollars ($20.00) for each offense.

G. L. 1896, ch. 112, § 3; P. L. 1900, ch. 746, § 3; G. L. 1909, ch. 136, § 3; G.

20-29-5. Nests not protected-Protection of poultry.-Sections 

20-29-1, 20-29-2 and 20-29-4 shall not apply to the killing of, or the destruction of the nests or eggs of, English or European house sparrows (passer domesticus), European starlings (sturnus vulgaris), or crows (genus corvus); nor shall they be deemed to make it unlawful for the owner or occupant of land to kill hawks or owls when in the act of destroying poultry, the property of said owner or occupant, on the land owned or occupied by said owner or occupant.

20-29-6. Closed season on game birds-Cock pheasants in New 

Shoreham and Jamestown.-Every person who shall take, kill or pursue with intent to kill, any of the anatidae, commonly known as "waterfowl," including any brant, wild duck or goose between the fifteenth day of January and the first day of October next following; any rail between the thirtieth day of November and the first day of 

September following; Wilson snipe between the thirtieth day of 

November and the first day of October following; woodcock between the thirtieth day of November and the first day of November following; ruffed grouse, commonly known as "partridge," quail, commonly known as "bobwhite," or cock pheasant, between the thirty-first day of December and the first day of November following, or hen pheasant at any season of the year, shall be fined not exceeding twenty dollars ($20.00) for each offense; provided, that every person who shall take, kill or pursue with intent to kill within the town of New 

Shoreham at any time except on the first or third Wednesday in 

November, or the first or third Wednesday in December in any year any cock pheasant shall be fined not exceeding twenty dollars ($20.00) for each offense; and every person who shall take or kill within the town of New Shoreham on any such Wednesday more than two (2) cock pheasant, shall be fined not exceeding twenty dollars 139

($20.00) for each bird which such person shall take or kill in excess of such two (2) birds; and provided, further, that every person who shall take, kill or pursue with intent to kill within the town of Jamestown at any time except on each Tuesday in the months of November and December in any year any cock pheasant shall be fined not exceeding twenty dollars ($20.00) for each offense; and provided, further, that every person who shall take or kill within the town of 

Jamestown on any such Tuesday more than two (2) cock pheasant shall be fined not exceeding twenty dollars ($20.00) for each bird which such person shall so take or kill in excess of such two (2) birds. Whoever shall buy, sell or offer for sale at any time of the year or have in his possession any rail, black-breasted plover, golden plover, grouse, quail, or pheasant, during the time that they are herein protected by law, whenever or wherever killed, shall be fined not exceeding twenty dollars ($20.00) for each such bird.

20-29-7. Bag limits on grouse, pheasants, woodcock, and quail 

Possession for sale.-Whoever takes, kills, or destroys in any one (1) day more than two (2) ruffed grouse, three (3) cock pheasants, six (6) woodcock, or six (6) quail, shall be fined not more than twenty dollars ($20.00) for each bird in excess thereof, and the possession of any such birds in excess of the numbers herein before prescribed shall be evidence of the taking or killing of the same, and whoever sells or offers for sale, or has in his possession, with intent to sell, any ruffed grouse, commonly called "partridge," quail, woodcock or undomesticated pheasant, whenever and wherever such bird may be taken or killed, shall be punished by a fine of twenty dollars ($20.00) for each bird, one-half thereof to the use of the complainant and onehalf thereof to the use of the state.

20-29-8. Hunting or possession of aquatic birds.-Every person who shall take, kill or pursue with intent to kill, destroy, sell, buy, 140

or offer for sale, or have in his possession any wood duck, swan, or any of the limicolae, commonly known as shore, marsh or beach birds, including curlew, dowitchers, dodwits, knots, phalaropes, plover, sandpipers, snipe, stilts, surf birds, turnstones, willett or Hungarian partridge, shall for each offense be fined not exceeding twenty dollars ($20.00); provided that the words "Hungarian partridge" shall not be construed to apply to the birds commonly called partridge or ruffed grouse; and provided, further, that this section shall not be construed as affecting the open season for woodcock, blackbreasted plover, golden plover, Wilson snipe, greater yellowlegs or lesser yellowlegs as already provided for in § 20-29-6.

20-29-9. Export of birds.-Every person who shall carry or send beyond the limits of this state at any time any wild duck, wild swan, wild goose, rail, any of the limicolae or shore, marsh, or beach birds, any woodcock, quail, commonly called "bobwhite," or ruffed grouse, commonly called "partridge," shall be fined not exceeding twenty dollars ($20.00) for each of said birds.

20-29-10. Trapping of birds-Hunting at night.-Every person who shall at any time of the year take, kill or destroy any quail, partridge, pheasant or other bird, the taking or killing of which is prohibited by this chapter, by means of any trap, snare, net or spring, or who shall construct, erect, set, repair, maintain or tend any trap, snare, net or spring for the purpose of taking, killing or destroying any quail, partridge, pheasant or other bird, the taking or killing of which is prohibited by this chapter, or who shall shoot any water fowl by means or by use of any battery gun, swivel gun, punt gun or pivot gun, or who shall take, kill or pursue with intent to kill any migratory game or insectivorous birds between sunset and one-half hour before sunrise shall be fined for each offense not exceeding twenty dollars ($20.00). 141

20-29-11. Possession as evidence of taking.-Possession by any person of any bird during the time when the taking, killing or destruction of such bird is prohibited by this chapter, shall be evidence before any court of competent jurisdiction that such bird was taken, killed or destroyed in violation of this chapter.

20-29-12. Killing or maiming of carrier pigeons.-Every person who shall wilfully maim, shoot, or otherwise kill any antwerp or homing pigeon, commonly called and known by the name of "carrier pigeon," either while in flight or at rest shall be fined for each such offense not exceeding twenty dollars ($20.00).

20-29-13. Entrapment or detention of carrier pigeons.-Every person who shall wilfully entrap, catch or detain any antwerp or homing pigeon, commonly called and known by the name of "carrier pigeon," shall be fined for each such offense not exceeding twenty dollars ($20.00); provided, however, that such pigeon shall at the time have the name of the owner stamped upon its wing or tail, or have a band with the owner's name, initial or number on its leg.

20-29-14. Forfeiture to landowner for shooting during closed season. Every person who shall between the fourteenth day of December and the fifteenth day of October next following, shoot at or kill any bird upon land not owned or occupied by himself and without permission of the owner or occupant thereof, shall forfeit and pay to the owner, or occupant thereof, five dollars ($5.00) for the first offense and ten dollars ($10.00) for every subsequent offense, in addition to the damage sustained.

Watchaug pond and Poquiant brook.-No person shall take, kill, destroy or pursue or in any manner molest any wild bird in any season of the year in that part of the town of Charlestown known as Watchaug pond and Poquiant brook or within one hundred (100) yards of the shore or banks of the same. Hunting on Patience island.-Notwithstanding the provisions of any other law, a person rearing wild birds or domestic game birds under a license issued by the department of agriculture and conservation, and liberating the same on Patience island in the town of Portsmouth, if said island is owned by said licensee, may himself, and with his consent the holder of a license to hunt in this state, take such birds by shooting from September 1 to the last day of February in each year on said island; but, subject to such rules and regulations as may be prescribed by the department of agriculture and conservation.

201. Deer hunting unlawful except as provided.-It shall be unlawful to pursue or shoot deer in this state except in accordance with the provisions of the following sections. 

P. L. 1907, ch. 1455, § 1; G. L. 1909, ch. 137, § 4; G. L. 1923, ch. 140, § 4; P. L. 

1925, ch. 647, § 1; G. L. 1938, ch. 249, 

§ 4. 

20-30-2. Permit to landowner to protect property.-Any person owning or occupying any farm or orchard and any person in his employ, while on his own premises or the premises of his employer, may kill any deer which shall be found destroying any crops, vegetables, or fruit trees belonging to such person or his employer; provided, however, that no such person shall shoot any deer unless he has obtained from the director of agriculture and conservation a permit so to do; and the director of agriculture and conservation may, upon application, issue to any responsible owner, or his employees, a permit authorizing such person to shoot deer in accordance with the provisions of this section. No such permit shall be issued until such time as the director of agriculture and conservation shall cause an investigation to be made to determine that actual damage has been done to any crops, vegetables or fruit trees by any deer. When such a permit is issued, it shall expire sixty (60) calendar days after the date of issuance. 

History of Section. 

P. L. 1907, ch. 1455, § 2; G. L. 1909, ch. 137, §5; G. L. 1923, ch. 140, § 5; P. 

L. 1925, ch. 647, § 1; P. L. 1928, ch. 1201, § 6; G. L. 1938, ch. 249, § 5; P. L. 1950, ch. 2554, § 1; P. L. 1956, ch. 3775, § 2. 

20-30-3. Archery permit.-Any person in possession of a resident or nonresident hunting or combination license may obtain a special archery permit, issued by the director of agriculture and conservation for a fee of five dollars ($5.00) for residents and twenty dollars ($20.00) for nonresidents. The permit shall be valid only from the 

5th to the 25th of January, inclusive, in the year of issue and shall allow the taking of one (1) deer only (either a doe or buck); shall be produced for examination upon the demand of any person; shall be of the design prescribed by the director of agriculture and conservation and may be revoked within the discretion of said director

1956, ch. 3775, § 2. 

20-30-4. Equipment used by archers.-The permittee, while hunting, pursuing, or taking deer pursuant to §§ 20-30-3 to 20-30-7, in- 146

clusive, shall be equipped with a nonmechanical bow capable of shooting a hunting arrow one hundred fifty (150) paces, and shall use only broadhead arrows with at least two (2) cutting edges seveneighths (8) inches or greater in width constructed without rivets. 

Each arrow used while hunting in accordance with the provisions of §§ 20-30-3 to 20-30-7, inclusive, shall carry the name and address of the person licensed.

Hunting for deer with bow and arrow shall be permitted on state owned land from January 5 to 25, inclusive.

20-30-6. Notice of killing of deer-Tagging and cancelation of license. Any permittee killing a deer shall notify a conservation officer within a reasonable time. The officer shall tag the deer and cancel the license.

20-30-7. Possession of unlawful weapons while hunting--Possession of guns, pistols, or other firearms, crossbows, explosive points, poisonous or barbed points, or possession of any deer showing evidence that it was shot with a firearm while hunting deer with.bow and arrow shall be a violation of §§ 20-30-3 to 20-30-7, inclusive. Any person violating the provisions of said sections shall be fined not exceeding one hundred dollars ($100).

1956, ch. 3775, § 2. 

20-30-8. Prohibition of hunting by local authorities.-The city council of any city or the town council of any town is hereby authorized and empowered to prohibit hunting, possessing or taking deer under §§ 20-30-3 to 20-30-7, inclusive, within the boundaries of said city or town by ordinance duly enacted.

History of Section. 

P. L. 1956, ch. 3775, § 3. 

20-30-9. Traps Use of dogs-Jacklights.-No person shall make, set or use any trap or snare, salt lick or other device for the purpose of ensnaring, enticing, taking, injuring or killing a deer and no person shall use a dog for the purpose of pursuing, chasing, 147

hunting or killing a deer. No person shall use, attempt to use or possess any jacklight for the purpose of taking any deer. For the purpose of this section, a jacklight shall be construed as any artificial light when used in conjunction with any rifle larger than a twenty-two (22) long rifle, or with a shotgun and ball shells or shot larger than no. 4 shot. Possession of such articles by any person during the hours of darkness shall be prima facie evidence of a violation of this Section. Any person who shall violate any provision of this section shall for the first offense, be fined not less than fifty dollars ($50.00) nor more than two hundred dollars ($200) or be imprisoned not less than thirty (30) days nor more than six (6) months or be both fined and imprisoned, and for each subsequent offense shall be fined not less than fifty dollars ($50.00) nor more than two hundred dollars ($200) or be imprisoned for not more than one (1) year. 

G. L., ch. 140, § 6, as enacted by P. L. 

1925, ch. 647, § 1; G. L. 1938, ch. 249, § 6; P. L. 1948, ch. 2054, § 1; P. L. 1949, ch. 2256, § 1. 

20-30-10. Report of killing or wounding- -Whoever wounds or kills a deer shall make a written report, signed by him, and send it within twenty-four (24) hours of such wounding or killing, to the chief of the division of fish and game, stating the facts relative to such wounding or killing, and any person violating the provisions of §§ 20-30-1 to 20-30-10, inclusive, shall be fined not less than one hundred dollars ($100) nor more than five hundred dollars ($500) for each offense.

20-30-11. Investigations-Enforcement powers.-It shall be the duty of the chief of the division of fish and game to forthwith investigate the circumstances surrounding the wounding and killing of all deer reported to him under § 20-30-10; and to enforce all provisions of this chapter and to prosecute every person who shall violate the same: He shall have power to arrest, without warrant, every person whom he shall find pursuing with intent to kill, taking or killing, or having in his possession any undomesticated deer or any part thereof contrary to the provisions of this chapter; provided, that any person so arrested without warrant shall not be detained longer than twenty-four (24) hours. 148

G. L., ch. 113, § 4, as enacted by P. L.

20-30-12. Immunity from liability for costs. Whenever complaint is made by the chief of division, or by his deputies, of any violation of the provisions of this chapter, he shall not be required, either by himself or by his deputies, to furnish surety for costs, or be liable for costs on such complaint.

20-30-13. Deer colliding with vehicles.-Notwithstanding the provisions of any general or public law, or amendment thereto, to the contrary, the resident owner of every motor vehicle which has been substantially damaged by collision with a deer shall, within twenty-four (24) hours thereafter, report the accident to a conservation officer. The officer shall investigate and, if he finds the damage has been done as alleged, he shall give a certificate to such person entitling such person to the ownership of the carcass to be possessed and consumed only within the immediate family of the owner of said vehicle.

History of Section. 

P. L. 1956, ch. 3850, § 1. 149

Closed season-Night hunting-Landowners.-Every person who shall take, kill or destroy any undomesticated rabbit, hare or gray squirrel between the thirty-first day of December and the first day of November, next following, shall be punished by a fine of twenty dollars ($20.00) for each and every rabbit, hare, or gray squirrel so taken, killed or destroyed, one-half of said fine to be to the use of the complainant and the other half to the use of the state; provided, however, it shall be unlawful for any person to take any rabbit during the period from one (1) hour after sunset to one (1) hour before sunrise; and provided, further, that any person owning and operating any farm and any person in his employ may, while on his own premises or on the premises of his employer, kill any rabbit which shall be found destroying or mutilating any crops, vegetables or fruit trees belonging to such person or his employer; and provided, further, that the carcass of such rabbit shall not be sold or offered for sale but shall be retained by the owner of the farm for his own use; and provided, further, that the director of agriculture and conservation, or any of his deputies, may require the owner of the farm to so prove that the rabbit was killed on the premises of the owner, and in such case the provisions of § 20-34-1 shall not hold.

202. Additional open season on rabbits in Newport county. 

Any person holding a valid hunting license shall be allowed to take, kill or destroy any undomesticated rabbit or rabbits in Newport county, between the first day of November in any year until the fifteenth day of February, next following, notwithstanding the provisions of 20-31-1.

P. L. 1945, ch. 1586, § 1.

20-31-4. Bag limits Unlawful sale or possession.-Whoever takes, kills or destroys in any one (1) day more than two (2) hares (commonly called snowshoe rabbits or white rabbits) or five (5) rabbits (two of which may be hares) or five (5) gray squirrels, shall be fined not more than twenty dollars ($20.00) for each animal in excess thereof, and the possession of any such animals in excess of the numbers hereinbefore prescribed shall be evidence of the taking or killing of the same; and whosoever sells or offers for sale or has in his possession with intent to sell any undomesticated rabbit, hare or gray squirrel contrary to the provisions of this section or § 2031-1 shall be punished by a fine of twenty dollars ($20.00) for each animal, one-half thereof to the use of the complainant and one-half thereof to the use of the state.

20-31-5. Use of ferrets and weasels.-Except as herein otherwise specifically provided every person who shall take, kill or destroy any rabbit or hare by the use of any ferret or weasel, shall be imprisoned in the adult correctional institutions ten (10) days for every such offense, provided, however, that when in any given locality rabbits are found to be destroying or mutilating fruit trees, crops, or vegetables, the director of agriculture and conservation may issue permits allowing the use of ferrets under the supervision of the division of fish and game and all live rabbits so taken by the use of ferrets shall be used by the office of fish and game for restocking purposes in other portions of the state. 151

20-31-6. Possession of ferret or weasel while hunting.-Except as otherwise specifically provided in $ 20-31-5, every person who shall have in his possession any ferret or weasel while hunting for or after any hare or rabbit, or who shall be associated in hunting for hares or rabbits with others who shall have in their possession or under their control any ferret or weasel, shall be fined not exceeding five dollars ($5.00) for every such offense.
