20-31-7. Enforcement of chapter.-It shall be the duty of the chief of the division of fish and game to enforce all provisions of this chapter and to prosecute every person who shall violate the same. He shall have power to arrest, without warrant, every person whom he shall find pursuing with intent to kill, taking or killing, or having in his possession any undomesticated rabbit, hare or gray squirrel or any part thereof contrary to the provisions of this chapter; provided, that any person so arrested without warrant shall not be detained longer than twenty-four (24) hours.

Immunity from liability for costs.-Whenever complaint is made by the chief of division, or by his deputies, of any violation of the provisions of this chapter, he shall not be required, either by himself or by his deputies, to furnish surety for costs, or be liable for costs on such complaint.

201. Closed season on muskrats, minks, otters, and skunks. 

Any person who shall take, kill, destroy, pursue with intent to kill, or set, maintain or tend any trap or snare for the purpose of taking, killing and destroying, any muskrat, mink or otter between the first day of February and the fifteenth day of November in any year, in the counties of Providence, Kent and Washington and between the fifteenth day of February and the first day of December in any year in the other counties of the state; any skunk between the fifteenth day of March and the fifteenth day of November in any year, shall be punished by a fine not exceeding twenty dollars ($20.00) for each offense.

P. L. 1929, ch. 1424, § 1; P. L. 1930, ch. 1578, § 1; G. L. 1938, ch. 250, § 1; 

P. L. 1949, ch. 2362, § 2. 

20-32-2. Closed season on raccoons.-Any person who shall take, kill or pursue with intent to kill any raccoon between the first day of February and the ninth day of October in any year, or set, maintain or tend any trap or snare for the purpose of taking, killing or destroying any raccoon between the first day of February and the first day of December in any year in the counties of Bristol and Newport and between the first day of February and the fifteenth day of November in any year in the other counties of the state, shall be punished by a fine not exceeding twenty dollars ($20.00) for each offense.

History of Section. 

P. L. 1929, ch. 1424, § 1; P. L. 1930, ch. 1578, § 1; G. L. 1938, ch. 250, § 1; 

P. L. 1949, ch. 2362, § 2. 

20-32-3. Taking and killing by landowners.-Nothing in $$ 20-32- 

1 and 20-32-2 shall be construed to prevent any person from taking or killing at any time of the year any skunk, muskrat, raccoon or mink upon his own land.

History of Section. 

P. L. 1929, ch. 1424, § 2; G. L. 1938, ch. 250, § 2. 

20-32-4. Additional closed season on muskrats on island of Conanicut. Notwithstanding any of the provisions of this chapter, no person shall take, kill, destroy, pursue with intent to kill, or set, maintain or tend any trap or snare for the purpose of taking, killing and destroying any muskrat in the town of Jamestown upon the island of Conanicut during the period from February 15th to January 15th, annually. Any person who is found guilty of violating the provisions of this section shall be punished by a fine not exceeding twenty dollars ($20.00) for each offense. 154

P. L. 1953, ch. 3130, §§ 1, 2. 

20-32-5. Regulations of director as to hunting.-After investigation, or upon information otherwise obtained by the director of agriculture and conservation as to the supply of fur-bearing animals, the director may by appropriate rules and regulations fix the seasons, trapping hours, and daily season and possession limits, or remove protection, or increase or reduce seasons and bag limits for any or all species of fur-bearing animals throughout the state or any part thereof or prescribe the methods of trapping therein, when in his opinion such action is necessary to assure the maintenance. of an adequate supply of such animals, or to reduce the numbers thereof to safeguard game birds and animals. Any violation of such rules and regulations upon conviction shall be punished by a fine not exceeding twenty dollars ($20.00).

History of Section. 

G. L., ch. 250, § 1, as enacted by P. L. 

1949, ch. 2362, § 2. 

20-32-6. Bag limit on raccoons-Keeping of live raccoons.-It shall be illegal to take more than two (2) raccoons during any period from sunset of one day to sunset of the following day by any one (1) party, and not more than twenty (20) in one season.

Possession of more than twenty (20) raccoons, or parts thereof, shall be prima facie evidence that the provisions of this section have been violated unless said person is the holder of and has a trapping license in his possession.

For the purpose of this section "a party" is defined as a group of men or dogs hunting together as a unit. Nothing in this chapter shall be construed to prohibit a person from keeping a live raccoon or raccoons for either breeding or other purposes while having in his possession a permit issued by the division of fish and game to do so.

Any person violating any of the provisions of this section shall be fined not exceeding twenty dollars ($20.00) for each offense. 

History of Section. 

G. L., ch. 249, § 12, as enacted by P. 

L. 1949, ch. 2258, § 1. 

20-32-7. Wire snares-Poisoning.-Whoever shall shall construct, crect, set, repair, maintain or tend any wire suare, or spread any poison, except within buildings located upon land owned or occupied by him, for the purpose of catching or killing any animal shall be punished by a fine of not less than fifty dollars ($50.00) nor more than one hundred dollars ($100), or be imprisoned not less than sixty (60) nor more than ninety (90) days, or both; pro- 155

vided, that the chief of the division of fish and game may, whenever in his discretion any given locality is overrun with vermin destructive to wild bird or animal life in this state, suspend the trapping laws in such locality by issuing permits authorizing the holder of such permit to set traps or snares in the open, under such rules and regulations as may be adopted by the said division, after hearing and after due notice to all persons interested, and any person holding such permit may lawfully set traps or snares in the open in such locality.

P. L. 1929, ch. 1424, § 3; G. L. 1938, ch. 250, § 3. 

20-32-8. Unlawful traps.-Any person who shall set, place, maintain or tend a steel trap with a spread of more than six (6) inches, or a steel trap with teeth jaws, or a "stop-thief" or choke trap with an opening of more than six (6) inches, shall be punished by a fine of not exceeding one hundred dollars ($100).

History of Section. 

P. L. 1929, ch. 1424, § 4; G. L. 1938, ch. 250, § 4. 

20-32-9. Consent of landowner for steel trap-Frequency of visits. -Any person who shall set, maintain or tend a steel trap on enclosed land of another without the consent in writing of the owner thereof, and any person who shall fail to visit at least once in twenty-four (24) hours a trap set or maintained by him, shall be punished by a fine not exceeding twenty dollars ($20.00).

History of Section. 

P. L. 1929, ch. 1424, §5; G. L. 1938, ch. 250, § 5. 

20-32-10. Traps in protected places.-Any person who shall set, place, maintain or tend any steel trap, "stop thief" or choke trap for the purpose of catching or killing any wild animal, unless the same shall be placed in a hole, brush pile, stone wall or other protected place, so situated as to be inaccessible to any domestic animal, shall be punished by a fine of not exceeding twenty dollars ($20.00). 

History of Section. 

P. L. 1929, ch. 1424, § 6; G. L. 1938, ch. 250, § 6. 

20-32-11. Green pelt as evidence of unlawful taking.-The possession of a green pelt or any part thereof by any person, except during the open season for such animal and for ten (10) days thereafter, shall be prima facie evidence that such pelt or part thereof was unlawfully taken and possessed. For the purposes of this section a "green pelt" is defined to mean a pelt which is not dried or cured, or tanned. 156

G. L., ch. 250, § 7, as enacted by P. L. 

1949, ch. 2362, § 3. 

20-32-12. Placement of traps before opening of season.-No trap shall be set, staked or placed where fur-bearing animals might be found before 12 o'clock noon on the opening date of the season.

History of Section. 

G. L., ch. 250, § 8, as enacted by P. 

20-32-13. Digging or smoking out animals.—Any person or persons who shall remove or attempt to remove a live muskrat, raccoon, mink, otter, or skunk from any hole in the ground, from any stone wall, from within any ledge or from under any stone, from any den or house, or from any hole in any log or tree by digging or smoking; and whoever shall set a trap within eight (8) feet from a muskrat house shall be punished by a fine not exceeding one hundred dollars ($100). Provided: that this section shall not prohibit an owner or occupant of land from protecting his property as provided in this chapter.

20-32-14. Disturbing traps of another.-Any person who shall disturb, tend or possess a trap of another, or take a fur-bearing animal from the traps of another unless specifically authorized to do so by the owner shall be punished by a fine not exceeding one hundred dollars ($100).

--32-16. Application for trapping license Fees. Trapping licenses shall be issued by the chief of the division of fish and game upon application and the payment of the license fee as hereinafter provided.

20-32-22. Fur buyer's license-Fee.-No person, firm or corporation shall purchase raw furs within this state unless such person, firm or corporation shall have a valid fur buyer's license. 20-32-23. Expiration of buyers' licenses-Record and reports.- 

All raw fur buyers' licenses shall expire on the thirtieth day of June next following the date of issuance, and said licensee shall keep records of purchases of furs within the state, which shall be open to the inspection by personnel of the division of fish and game at all times. A complete and accurate record of purchases within the state shall be furnished the division of fish and game upon request. 

Failure to do so within fourteen (14) days may be punishable by forfeiture of license and no future license shall be granted if in the opinion of the chief of the division of fish and game, information is being deliberately withheld.

CHAPTER 33 FOX BOUNTIES 

Proof and payment of bounty.

Consent of landowner for trapping-Tags on traps. 

20-33-4.  

20-33-1. Amount of bounty.-Every legal resident of the state of Rhode Island who, during the period between January first and December thirty-first in any year, shall legally kill, within the limits of the state, any wild fox not in captivity, shall, upon presentation of the proof hereinafter designated, all the provisions of s 20-33-2 having been complied with, receive therefor the sum of three dollars ($3.00) to be paid by the general treasurer. 

G. L., ch. 642, § 19, as enacted by P.

L. 1940, ch. 813, § 1. 

20-33-2. Proof and payment of bounty.-Before such legal resident shall be entitled to receive the sum specified in § 20-33-1, he shall exhibit, to either the town or city clerk of the town or city in which said legal resident resides, the entire carcass of the fox and shall make affidavit of the time and place of such killing. The officer before whom such animal shall be exhibited shall thereupon cause the tongue to be removed from said carcass and, if satisfied that said affidavit is true, certify that the person making such affidavit exhibited to said officer the entire carcass of the fox alleged to have been killed and that said officer examined such carcass and found it intact with no portion of the tongue removed or mutilated, and that he, the said officer, caused the tongue to be removed from such carcass; said affidavit with certificate shall be forwarded to the chief of the division of fish and game of the department of agriculture and conservation and, upon the approval of said chief, such affidavit and certificate shall be sent to the controller, said forms for affidavits, certificates and approvals to be furnished to the town and city clerks by the chief of said division of fish and game; and the controller shall, if said affidavit and certificate are in accordance with the provisions of this section and are presented within thirty (30) days after the time named in such affidavit, draw his order upon the general treasurer in favor of the person signing such affidavit. The general assembly shall annually appropriate such sum as it may deem necessary for the purpose of carrying out the provisions of this section and § 20-33-1.

CHAPTER 34 POSSESSION AND SALE OF GAME 

Unlawful possession of rabbits, squirrels, or deer.

20-34-1. Unlawful possession of rabbits, squirrels, or deer.-No person shall have in his possession at any time when the killing of the same is prohibited by law any undomesticated rabbit, hare, gray squirrel or deer, unless the same shall have been legally taken under the provisions of chapter 30 or chapter 31 of this title or shall have been legally imported from another state or country, where the same shall have been legally taken or killed, and the chief of said division or his deputy may seize without warrant any undomesticated rabbit, hare, gray squirrel or deer found in the possession of any person, at any time when the killing of the same is prohibited by law, unless satisfied that the same is legally held under the provisions of chapter 30 or chapter 31 of this title. Any person violating the provisions of this section shall be fined not more than five hundred dollars ($500) for each offense.

G. L., ch. 113, § 6, as enacted by P. L.

20-34-2. Permit to import birds or game animals.-The chief of the division of fish and game may grant a permit, under such conditions as he may deem expedient, to any person to transport into the state any birds or other game protected in any manner by the laws of the state and which have been lawfully killed without the state 162

and the transportation of which is lawful. No person shall sell or offer for sale any birds or game held in possession under a permit authorized under the provisions of this section. Any permit so granted may be suspended or revoked by the chief of said division. 

Any person violating any provision of this section shall be punished. by a fine of not more than twenty dollars ($20.00) for each bird or game quadruped.

20-34-3. Possession of wild birds not for sale.-No person shall have in his possession, at any time, more than fifteen (15) wild birds, the sale of which is prohibited by law. Whoever violates the provisions of this section shall be punished by a fine of not exceeding twenty dollars ($20.00) for each bird or part thereof.

20-34-4. Possession by dealers or restaurants of game not authorized for sale. No person, firm, corporation or association dealing in poultry, meats, fish, groceries or other provisions, or engaged in the cold storage business, or any other business which requires, or has an ice box or cold storage or freezing vault, of any description, for use in connection with his, their or its business, and no hotel, club, public house, restaurant or victualers house shall have in his, their or its possession, at any time of year, at such place of business, or at any other place where any of his, their or its provisions, which are for use in his, their or its business, are kept, any bird or animal the sale of which is prohibited by chapter 29 of this title.

20-34-5. Penalty for unlawful possession-Compromise of violation. Whoever violates the provisions of § 20-34-4 shall be punished by a fine of not exceeding twenty dollars ($20.00) for each bird or animal or part thereof found in his possession in respect to which the violation occurs; the chief of the division of fish and game, or any deputy, who shall compromise or settle any violation of the bird and game law out of court, shall be guilty of a misdemeanor, and shall be punished by a fine of not exceeding one hundred dollars ($100).

206. Issuance, execution and return of warrant for unlawfully possessed game.-Whenever complaint is made on oath to any magistrate authorized to issue warrants in criminal cases, that the complainant believes and has reasonable cause to believe that wild birds or wild animals are being possessed contrary to law, or that the laws in relation to wild birds or wild animals have been or are being violated in any building or place, such magistrate, if satisfied that there is reasonable cause for such belief, shall issue a search warrant authorizing any officer competent to serve a warrant, to search such place or building; but no search shall be made after sunset, unless specially authorized by the magistrate upon satisfactory cause shown; and all such wild birds or wild animals shall be taken and be kept by such officer in some proper place of security until they can be proceeded against in the manner hereinafter provided; and the officer shall summon the owner or keeper, if any be named in the complaint, if to be found by him, and the warrant shall be returned by said officer with his doings thereon, as soon as may be, to the district court having jurisdiction of the same in the district where such wild birds or wild animals shall have been seized. 

G. L. 1896, ch. 112, § 18; G. L. 1909, ch. 136, § 24; P. L. 1916, ch. 1385, § 2; 

G. L. 1923, ch. 137, § 22; G. L. 1938, ch. 248, 18. 

20-34-7. Posting of notice of seizure.-If the owner or keeper be not named in the warrant or not found, the court shall order a notice of such seizure to be given by posting up written notices in at least three (3) public places in the town or city, one of which shall be on the building or near the place, where the seizure was made, and such other notice shall be given as the court shall deem to be necessary. 

History of Section. 

G. L., ch. 136, § 24, as enacted by P. 

L. 1916, ch. 1385, § 2; G. L. 1923, ch. 137, § 22; G. L. 1938, ch. 248, § 18. 

20-34-8. Forfeiture of unlawfully possessed animals.-All wild birds and wild animals seized under the provisions of this chapter and title, which were possessed or kept in violation of law, shall be forfeited to the state, and the officer making such seizure, or an officer designated by the court for the purpose, shall forthwith proceed to prosecute for the forfeiture thereof in the manner provided by law.

If the cause of forfeiture be proved, the court before which the complaint or information shall be tried shall enter sentence of forfeiture to the state against said wild birds or wild animals; and 1.64

any person aggrieved by such sentence may appeal therefrom to the superior court according to the provisions relating to criminal appeals from district courts. If the cause of forfeiture be not proved, then all such seized wild birds or wild animals shall be forthwith restored to the proper owner thereof or to the place from which they were taken by the officer who seized the same. 

Upon the entry of sentence of forfeiture against such wild birds or wild animals, if no appeal be then taken or no recognizance given as prescribed in other criminal cases, the court shall forthwith issue a warrant to the officer, commanding him forthwith to destroy or to distribute to public hospitals, or if alive, to liberate said wild birds or wild animals; and every such officer shall execute said warrant and shall return the same, with his doings thereon, to the court, within such time as the court shall direct. In case of final judgment for the state on appeal, the court entering such final judgment shall issue the like warrant, as aforesaid, for the destruction or liberation of the wild birds or wild animals seized.

G. L., ch. 136, § 24, as enacted by P. 

L. 1916, ch. 1385, § 2; G. L. 1923, ch. 

137, § 22; G. L. 1938, ch. 248, § 18. 

20-34-9. Fees of officers.-There shall be allowed and paid to the officer who shall make the same for every seizure made under the foregoing provisions, the sum of five dollars ($5.00), with two dollars ($2.00) additional for every day after the first that such officer shall be necessarily employed in causing the wild birds or wild animals seized to be condemned and ten cents (104) for every mile that he shall necessarily travel therein, together with such reasonable sum as the court may deem just for necessary expense incurred in providing storage for wild birds or wild animals seized, pending proceedings for condemnation, all which costs shall be taxed in the bill of costs and the amount allowed by the court in which final judgment shall be rendered to such officers, shall be paid by the state controller out of any money in the treasury not otherwise appropriated; provided, that no fees herein provided to be paid to the officer making such seizure shall be paid to any officer, who at the time of such seizure is under pay as a deputy in the division of fish and game, History of Section.

CHAPTER 35 FIELD TRIALS AND SHOOTING PRESERVES SECTION. 

20-35-1. Field trial licenses. 20-35-2. 

Shooting preserves. 

20-35-3. Laws inapplicable to field trials and shooting preserves. 

20-35-4. Restriction to open season. 

20-35-1. Field trial licenses.-The director of agriculture and conservation is hereby authorized to issue without charge to persons or organizations applying therefor licenses for the holding of field trials at which liberated domestic game may be taken by shooting. 

Such game may be taken by shooting during the months of September through April 15th.

P. L. 1948, ch. 2156, § 1; P. L. 1950, ch. 2635, § 1; P. L. 1953, ch. 3138, § 1; 

P. L. 1955, ch. 3576, § 1. 

20-35-2. Shooting preserves.-The director of agriculture and conservation is hereby authorized in his discretion to issue to an owner or lessee of wholly enclosed lands a shooting preserve license permitting him to possess and to release and to take by shooting thereon liberated domestic game. The license shall expire on December 31st annually and the fee therefor shall be ten dollars ($10.00). 

Before any shooting may be done the licensee must advise the department of agriculture and conservation in writing of the number of each species of domestic game purchased and/or otherwise acquired for liberation, and request and receive in writing a shooting authorization which shall state the number of each species of domestic game which may be taken by shooting. Such authorizations shall be based on the number and age of the birds liberated which in no case shall be less than eighty per cent (80%) of the number of birds released for shooting. In addition, on each area or portion thereof which is licensed for the first time, or for which the license has not been renewed for a period of one (1) year or more, the licensee shall, in the case of pheasants, make an initial liberation to be witnessed by a representative of the department of a number of birds equal to one (1) bird for each ten (10) acres in such licensed premises in the ratio of one (1) cock to four (4) hens. The licensee or with his written consent the holder of a license to hunt in this state may take domestic game liberated in accordance with this section by shooting 166

under the rules prescribed by the department. The taking or killing of domestic game refers only to the taking or killing on the premises specified in the shooting preserve license.

P. L. 1948, ch. 2156, § 2. 

20-35-3. Laws inapplicable to field trials and shooting preserves.- 

The provisions of § 4-1-16, 20-29-6, 20-29-7, 20-29-11 and 20-34-3 shall not be construed to apply to shooting preserves, to field trials upon liberated domestic game, or to the possession, releasing, or shooting of liberated domestic game at field trials or upon shooting preserves licensed in accordance with the provisions of this chapter. 

History of Section. 

P. L. 1948, ch. 2156, § 4. 

20-35-4. Restriction to open season.-Nothing herein shall be construed so as to authorize the taking of game by shooting, other than liberated, domestic pheasant, quail and mallard ducks properly banded, at any time other than during the open hunting season. 

History of Section. 

P. L. 1948, ch. 2156, § 5; P. L. 1950, ch. 2635, § 1; P. L. 1953, ch. 3138, § 1; 

P. L. 1955, ch. 3576, § 1. 167

CHAPTER 114. 

AN ACT IN AMENDMENT OF SECTIONS 20-30-9 AND 20-30-11 OF, AND IN ADDITION TO CHAPTER 20-30, OF THE GENERAL LAWS, ENTITLED "DEER HUNTING", IN AMENDMENT OF SECTION 20-31-7 OF CHAPTER 20-31 OF THE GENERAL LAWS, ENTITLED “HARES AND SQUIRRELS", AND IN AMENDMENT OF SECTION 20-34-1 OF CHAPTER 20-34 OF THE GENERAL LAWS, ENTITLED "POSSESSION AND SALE OF GAME". 

SECTION 1. Section 20-30-9 of chapter 20-30 of the general laws, entitled "Deer hunting," is hereby amended to read as follows: 

"Sec. 20-30-9. TRAPS USE OF DOGS JACKLIGHTS.- 

No person shall make, set or use any trap or snare, salt lick or other device for the purpose of ensnaring, enticing, taking, injuring or killing a deer (Odocoileus virginianus), and no person shall use a dog for the purpose of pursuing, chasing, hunting or killing a deer (Odocoileus virginianus). It shall be unlawful for any person to take, attempt to take, or hunt any deer (Odocoileus virginianus) during the period from one hour after sunset to one hour before sunrise. No person shall use, attempt to use or possess any jacklight for the purpose of taking any deer. For the purpose of this section, a jack light shall be construed as any artificial light when used in conjunction with any rifle larger than a 22 long rifle, or with a shotgun and ball shells or shot larger than no. 4 shot or any other lethal weapon. Possession of such articles by any person during the hours of darkness shall be prima facie evidence of a violation of this section. Any person who shall violate any provision of 20

this section shall for the first offense, be fined not less than $50.00 nor more than $200.00 or be imprisoned not less than 30 days nor more than 6 months or be both fined and imprisoned, and for each subsequent offense shall be fined not less than $50.00 nor more than $200.00 or be imprisoned for not more than one year."

SEC. 2. Section 20-30-11 of said chapter 20-30 and sec tion 20-31-7 of chapter 20-31 of the general laws entitled "Hares and squirrels" are hereby amended to read as follows: 

"Sec. 20-30-11.

INVESTIGATIONS-ENFORCEMENT POW- 

ERS.-It shall be the duty of the chief of the division of fish and game to forthwith investigate the circumstances surrounding the wounding and killing of all deer reported to him under section 20-30-10; and to enforce all provisions of this chapter and to prosecute every person who shall violate the same. He shall have power to arrest, without warrant, every person whom he shall find pursuing with intent to kill, taking or killing, or having in his possession any undomesticated deer or any part thereof contrary to the provisions of this chapter: Provided, that any person so arrested without warrant shall not be detained longer than 24 hours. Any person violating the provisions of this section shall be fined not more than $500.00 for each offense. 

Deer hunting, investigations and enforcement powers. 

"Sec. 20-31-7. ENFORCEMENT OF CHAPTER.-It shall be the duty of the chief of the division of fish and game to enforce all provisions of this chapter and to prosecute every person who shall violate the same. He shall have 21 

Rabbits and squirrels; enforcement powers

Unlawful possession of rabbits, squirrels and deer. the power to arrest, without warrant, every person whom he shall find pursuing with intent to kill, taking or killing, or having in his possession any undomesticated rabbit, hare or gray squirrel or any part thereof contrary to the provisions of this chapter: Provided, that any person so arrested without warrant shall not be detained longer than 24 hours. Any person violating the provisions of this section shall be fined not more than $500.00 for each offense."

SEC. 3. Section 20-34-1 of chapter 20-34, entitled 

"Possession and sale of game," is hereby amended to read as follows: 

"Sec. 20-34-1. UNLAWFUL POSSESSION OF RABBITS, 

SQUIRRELS, OR DEER.-No person shall have in his possession at any time when the killing of the same is prohibited by law; any cottontail rabbit (Sylvilagus floridanus and Sylvilagus transitionalis), varying hare (Lepus americanus), gray squirrel (Sciurus carolinensis) or deer (Odocoileus virginianus); it shall be a defense for any person charged with a violation of this section to establish affirmatively that the same shall have been legally taken under the provisions of this title; or to produce a seal or tag to establish that the same had been legally imported from another state or country where the same shall have been legally taken or killed; the chief of the division of fish and game, and each conservation officer may seize without a warrant any such animal or animals. Any person violating the provisions of this section shall be fined not more than $500.00 for each offense."

SEC. 4. Chapter 20-30 is hereby amended by adding thereto the following section: 22

T 

"Sec. 20-30-13. 'DEER' DEFINED.—FORFEITURE OF WEAP- 

ON. Wherever in this chapter the word 'deer' appears, it shall mean Virginia white tail deer, (Odocoileus virginianus). Upon conviction of a volation of any provisions of this chapter, any weapon used in killing a deer illegally shall be forfeited to the state." "Deer". 

Forfeiture of weapon.

SEC. 5. If any provision of this act or of any rule or Act severable. regulation made thereunder, or the application thereof to any person or circumstance, is held invalid by a court of competent jurisdiction, the remainder of the act, rule or regulation, and the application of such provision to other persons or circumstances shall not be affected thereby. The invalidity of any section or sections or parts of any section or sections of this act shall not affect the validity of the remainder of the act.

SEC. 6. This act shall take effect upon its passage and all acts and parts of acts inconsistent herewith are hereby repealed.
